# Backlog

Each item is a self-contained prompt for a new thread. Ordered by suggested priority; reorder freely. When an item is done, tick it and move it to the Completed section at the bottom, under the matching heading, so follow-ups can still be traced back to it.

## Next up

**Role walkthrough, Pass 1 (Vet) — 2026-09-27. Work these first, then re-run the
walkthrough from the top** (Lutan). The twelve items live under their own headings
rather than being copied here; this is the order to take them in and what blocks
what. Record is `docs/uat/2026-09-27.md`; the script is `docs/role-walkthrough.md`.

| Order | Item | Where | Why here |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | A refused page and a booked vet visit dump you on the public website | Quick wins | Smallest of the twelve, and it blocks re-testing: it ejects the tester mid-checklist on every refused route. Also cuts load on the CPU path the 1102s are hitting |
| 2 | Doctors belong to a vet — managed list rather than free text | Medical records | **The real gate.** A vet account has no link to a clinic at all today, so items 4 and 8 cannot be built until this exists. Schema PR of its own, ahead of both |
| 3 | Cut the vet's world down to residents and their own tasks | Auth | Independent of the schema, and it is most of what Lutan wants the vet role to feel like. Menu is easy; the five unguarded route trees are the work |
| 4 | A vet should see only the residents their own clinic treats | Auth | Largest item from Pass 1. Needs 2 first, and needs the rule **decided with Lutan before anyone builds it** — a scope that hides a resident from the vet about to treat them is worse than one that shows too much |
| 5 | Recurring jobs: only offer people who can actually do the job | Management | Independent. Stops the Pass 0 situation arising rather than handling it |
| 6 | Prescription should not attach to a future vet visit | Medical records | Small, independent, correctness |
| 7 | One weight per vet visit | Medical records | Small, independent, correctness |
| 8 | Vet visit form: clinic picker and the Status dropdown | Medical records | The clinic half needs 2; the Status question is a decision, not code |
| 9 | A vet's photo upload limited to the Medical folder | Medical records | **Do with the Medical-folder item already at the top of this section** — both are about what Medical means as a destination, and splitting them means touching the same upload path twice |
| 10 | A date field cannot be cleared once set, on mobile | Mobile | Small, and it affects every optional date in the app rather than the one it was found on |
| 11 | A role-based manual | Documentation | Pairs with 3: as the vet's menu shrinks, a manual still explaining Enclosures is actively wrong |
| 12 | File-type icon for non-images; release notes by role | Quick wins, Documentation | Nice to haves. Last |

**Fold the schema into one PR first, or the migration rule will be the bottleneck.**
Four of the twelve want a migration — 2 (a `vet_doctors` table, and the link from a
vet *account* to a clinic that does not exist today), 4 (the RLS policies that scope
residents to a clinic, which need that same link), 7 (a unique index behind one
weight per visit) and 6 (a check against the appointment date). CLAUDE.md allows
**only one in-flight branch carrying a migration at a time**, and says that where two
streams both need schema their migrations go in one PR. Done separately these four
serialise behind each other no matter how many worktrees are open; done as a single
additive schema PR up front, every feature half that follows can run in parallel.
2 and 4 need the same link anyway, so they are not really two decisions.

**Item 4 is gated on a decision, not on throughput.** It is the one thing here that
should not be picked up by a session and built: the rule for what a vet may see
wants settling with Lutan first, because a scope that hides a resident from the vet
about to treat them is worse than the current one that shows too much.
Not on this list and not forgotten: **Cloudflare 1102 on the test site**, expected
to clear when the Pi becomes the origin (see Deployment), now deferred to
Friday 2026-10-02, so expect it to persist until then. It is the environment,
not a defect in the app.

- [x] **Bug: uploading a Shelter Friend logo fails [token fixed and retested 2026-09-25; code follow-ups done 2026-09-26] with "Google OAuth token refresh failed: invalid_grant: Token has been expired or revoked."** Reported by Lutan 2026-09-25 with a screenshot of the Shelter Friend card on a contact (Save pressed after choosing a logo; the address/map opt-ins visible above). **Environment: UAT, lannacare.org** (confirmed by Lutan). **Urgent: uploads on the site the customer is testing are down.** lannacare.org is served by the `production` block in `wrangler.jsonc`, whose Drive is still the dev Google account (lannaanimalfoundationbwm@gmail.com), so that account's Google Cloud project is where to look. Checked 2026-09-25: the local `.env.local` / `.dev.vars` credentials now fail with `invalid_client` ("The OAuth client was not found"), so they can't stand in for UAT's, and a new token has to be minted. **Cause is not the logo code:** the message comes from `src/lib/google/drive.ts` (token refresh, ~line 105): Google refused `GOOGLE_OAUTH_REFRESH_TOKEN` for that environment. So **every Drive upload on that environment is failing** (resident photos, hero, logos, project photos, backups), not just this one. Different from the dev-only `invalid_client` seen 2026-09-24 (a dead client); `invalid_grant` means the refresh token itself was revoked or expired. Usual reasons: the OAuth consent screen of the Drive client's project is in *Testing* (refresh tokens die after 7 days), the Google account's password was changed or access removed, or a newer refresh token was issued for the same client and account (Google keeps a limited number). **Fix:** (1) find the reason in the Drive client's Google Cloud project (under the account that owns the Drive; see the 90-day-token item too), publish the consent screen if it is in Testing, (2) mint a new refresh token and set it as the Worker secret for the affected environment (and `.dev.vars` / `.env.local` locally), (3) redeploy and retry the logo upload. **Code follow-ups, same PR or next:** show staff a plain message ("Photo storage is not connected — tell an admin") instead of Google's raw text, and make sure a failed logo upload doesn't throw away the rest of the Shelter Friend form; report it on the release/health check (e.g. a Drive token check in `scripts/` or the admin page) so an expired token shows up before a user hits it. **Fixed 2026-09-25 (token):** cause confirmed. The Drive client "Lanna Care Drive Access" (Desktop, created 2026-09-18) lives in the lanna account's project **Lanna Care - Dev** (`lanna-care-dev`), which was still in *Testing*, so its refresh token expired 7 days later, on 2026-09-25. The v0.5.0 deploy was not involved (no secret changes in `wrangler deployments list`). Published the project (logo removed, since a logo forces verification; home page `https://lannacare.org` and privacy `https://lannacare.org/privacy` added; `lannacare.org` added as an authorised domain), minted a new token, and set it in `.env.local` and as `GOOGLE_OAUTH_REFRESH_TOKEN` on the `production` and `test` Workers. Also removed a stray space after `=` on the Google keys in `.env.local`. Lutan retested the Shelter Friend logo upload on lannacare.org 2026-09-25: works. **Code follow-ups done 2026-09-26** (`claude/drive-upload-errors`): a dead token or client now raises `DriveNotConnectedError`, and every upload (logo, Website photos, resident/project/maintenance photos, attachments) and Drive folder sync says "Photo storage is not connected — tell an admin.", with Google's text going to the server log. The form itself was never lost (the logo uploads on its own, apart from Save, and the draft survives), but its message showed below Save, out of sight; it now sits by the logo. Settings (`/admin`) shows whether Drive is connected, and `scripts/check-drive-token.mjs` checks an env file.

- [x] **Give the public site some life: spring motion on text and content blocks.** Lutan, 2026-09-26: some public websites have a "springy" feel to their text blocks, and the public pages should feel less static. **What it means here:** headings, text blocks and cards ease into place as they scroll into view (a short rise and fade with a slight spring overshoot, staggered within a group), and interactive cards (the "How you can help" cards, resident cards, Shelter Friend logos, buttons) give a small springy lift on hover and press. Subtle, not a show: short distances, around 300 to 600 ms, each block animates once. **How:** prefer plain CSS with the `linear()` easing function (it can express a real spring curve) and an `IntersectionObserver` that adds a class, so there is no animation library and almost no bundle cost. Reach for a library (e.g. `motion`) only if something needs physics CSS cannot fake, and say why in `docs/decisions.md`. **Must:** respect `prefers-reduced-motion` (no movement at all, content simply present), never hide content from anyone without JavaScript or from search engines (start visible and animate only once JS has run, or make the no-JS state the final one), no layout shift, and keep the staff app itself calm (public pages only). **When:** build it on the redesigned components, so after `public-site-shell` (batch 2 of the 2026-09-25 plan). It can ride with or follow `public-site-home`. First, agree the feel with Lutan on one section (e.g. the "How you can help" cards) with two or three variants in a preview before rolling it across the site. Reference sites Lutan likes would help; ask for a link.

Draw an original dog (ideally the shelter's logo dog, or a dog and a cat taking turns) as a looping run cycle. **Format:** an animated SVG (CSS keyframes on a few shapes) or a Lottie file rather than a GIF, so it is tiny, crisp at any size, recolourable to the redesign palette, and can be paused. **Where it shows:** the public pages' `loading.tsx` (Next's route-level loading UI) and client-side route changes, plus any wait over about a second such as a form submit or a photo upload. The staff app gets it too (Lutan, 2026-09-26): a small inline version for uploads and slow pages, not a full-screen overlay. **Behaviour:** appear only after a short delay (about 300 ms) so fast navigations don't flash it, and have an accessible label ("Loading", en and th) with `role="status"`. Under `prefers-reduced-motion` show a still dog with the label instead of the run cycle. **When:** with or after `public-site-shell` (redesign part 1), so it uses the new palette; it pairs naturally with the spring-motion item above. **Reference and concept (Lutan, 2026-09-26):** not a running dog after all, but **a puppy working on a laptop**, drawn from Lutan's own video (`C:UsersLeidosOneDrivePicturesIMG_0004 (2).MOV`, 2.6 s, portrait): a fluffy apricot poodle-cross in orange patterned pyjamas, leaning in and peering at an open laptop, nose close to the screen. ## Security

**Moved above Deployment on 2026-09-30 (Lutan): severity governs here, not
file position.** The review appended this section at the end of the file,
which by this file's own convention ("ordered by suggested priority") read as
the lowest priority in the backlog while it held the only High finding and a
live open redirect. That was an artifact of how it was written, not a
judgement — so the section was moved rather than left to mislead the next
planner.

From the 2026-09-30 security assessment (`docs/security/security-assessment-2026-09-30.docx`, `.pdf`; commit d26103b, release 0.10.0). Finding IDs (DB-n, CODE-n, WEB-n) are the report's. No Critical finding; one High. Items are grouped so each is one stream; schema items are `claude/<name>-schema` PRs first, per the migrations rules. Effort is the report's. None of these makes the app harder for field staff.

- [ ] **Encrypt the weekly backup and keep session tables out of it (DB-1, High).** `scripts/backup.mjs:44` dumps `public` and `auth` with `pg_dump` and uploads the file to the shelter's Drive as-is; `auth` holds password hashes, TOTP secrets in plain text and live refresh tokens, and line 104 logs the shareable link to `backup.log`. Encrypt before upload with a key kept only by the administrator (age, gpg or `openssl enc -aes-256-cbc -pbkdf2`; key in a password manager, never the repo or `.env`), add `--exclude-table=auth.refresh_tokens --exclude-table=auth.sessions --exclude-table='auth.mfa_*'`, put `Backups/` in a folder shared with the admin only rather than the application root, and stop logging the web link. Then rehearse a restore. Half a day. **Do first.** **Update 2026-09-30:** the weekly backup now runs on the Raspberry Pi as well as the laptop (`--local-copy`, cron Sundays 03:00), so **the Pi's SSD also holds plaintext dumps**, and its `~/backups/backup.log` collects the Drive link every week. Encrypt with `age` public-key encryption so the decrypting key never sits on the Pi, and encrypt before both the upload and the local copy. **Status 2026-09-30:** code done in PR #243 (age public key, exclusions, link no longer logged, tile reads `.dump.age`); **left open until Lutan** generates the key pair, installs the public key on the Pi and laptop, does the first run and the restore rehearsal, deletes the old plaintext dumps and the Pi's `backup.log`, re-shares `Backups/` and rotates (test plan manual table).
- [ ] **Backups from an always-on machine, and a rehearsed restore (DB-2).** The scheduled task runs only while the developer's Windows account is logged on (`scripts/backup-schedule.ps1:43-46`); `backup.log` shows two manual runs (21 and 26 Sep), and the restore procedure has never been tested. Move the schedule to a GitHub Actions workflow or the Pi in `docs/pi-hosting.md`, restore once into a scratch Supabase project and write the steps down, repeat quarterly, and have the "Weekly backup" status tile alert admins when it goes red. A day, including the rehearsal. Do together with the item above.
- [ ] **Close the sign-in open redirect (WEB-1).** `safeNextPath` in `src/lib/auth/next-path.ts` rejects `//` but not `/\`, which browsers treat as `/`, so `?next=/\evil.com` sends a user off-site straight after a genuine sign-in. Parse with `new URL(next, "http://x")`, return null unless the origin is unchanged, reject backslashes and control characters, and return `pathname + search`. Five lines plus a test. **Do first.**
- [x] **HTTP security headers and Secure cookies (WEB-2).** *Done in the Worker; the CSP is report-only, tightening owed (see docs/decisions/2026-09-30-security-headers-in-the-worker.md).* Nothing sets CSP, X-Frame-Options, HSTS, Referrer-Policy, Permissions-Policy or nosniff, and the Supabase cookie is created without `secure`. Add `headers()` in `next.config.ts` (or the Worker): `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Referrer-Policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin`, `Permissions-Policy: camera=(self), geolocation=()`, HSTS if the Cloudflare zone does not already send it; CSP in report-only first (`default-src 'self'; img-src 'self' data: blob:; frame-src https://www.google.com`), tighten after a quiet week; `cookieOptions: { secure: true }` in production on both Supabase client factories. Camera must stay allowed for photos and QR scanning. Also covers CODE-7's proxy half (`nosniff` and `Content-Disposition` on `src/app/api/photos/[fileId]/route.ts`).
- [ ] **Tighten and collect the CSP (WEB-2 follow-up).** The Worker sends `Content-Security-Policy-Report-Only` (`worker/security-headers.mjs`, shipped 2026-09-30) and nothing collects the reports. After a quiet week: add a `report-to` endpoint (or read the browser console on the main pages), drop `'unsafe-inline'` from `script-src` with nonces if feasible, then switch to `Content-Security-Policy`. Consider raising HSTS above six months once stable.
- [ ] **Check the row count before deleting Drive files (CODE-1, CODE-2).** Under RLS a refused delete affects zero rows without an error, and the code then deletes the Drive file anyway: `blood-tests/actions.ts:32-42`, `procedures/actions.ts:32-42`, `maintenance/actions.ts:361-369`, `projects/actions.ts:322-329`. A vet can select more attachments than `vet_delete_attachments` (0110) lets it delete, so an outside clinic can destroy another clinic's document while the row survives. Use `.delete().eq(...).select('id')` and abort unless exactly one row came back; better, a security-definer RPC like `delete_resident_photo`, and move Drive files to trash rather than deleting permanently (`drive.ts:408-412`). Separately `deleteMaintenanceJob` (`maintenance/actions.ts:308-323`) deletes the job's attachment rows before it learns the caller may not delete the job, so any volunteer can strip a job's photo records: delete the job first and let a foreign-key cascade take the attachments, or check the role up front as `deceased/actions.ts:92-95` does. A morning.
- [ ] **Guard placement history in the database (DB-3, DB-4) — schema PR first.** (DB-3) Staff and management have an unrestricted update policy on `placement_history` (0001:529, 0039) and the immutability trigger leaves `end_date` writable, so a resident's placement can be closed or reopened by hand; make `enforce_placement_history_immutability()` refuse an `end_date` change unless a transaction-local flag set by `close_prior_placement()` is present (the deceased lock's pattern). (DB-4) A `ChangeEnclosure` placement (volunteers can create one) can target the Deceased or Adopted pseudo-enclosure, which skips the deceased workflow, takes no cascade snapshot, engages every lock and defeats `undo_deceased_placement()`; add a before-insert trigger refusing a Lifecycle-zone target unless the placement type matches. One migration, no UI change.
- [ ] **Narrow what vets and volunteers can read (DB-5, DB-8) — decision, then a schema PR.** `vet_read_contacts` and `volunteer_read_contacts` (0001:548, 569) expose every carer's and donor's phone, email, home address, LINE/WhatsApp/Messenger and notes; 0105 records that this was deliberately not narrowed. Proposal: vets get a view of id, name and type (or no access), volunteers name and phone at most, staff and above everything (Thai PDPA is the reason). Check the vet's resident page still renders. (DB-8, Low) `app_users` (0055, 0063, 0086) shows every login's email to vets and volunteers; hide `email` for those roles. Lutan to confirm what volunteers actually need before the migration.
- [ ] **Decide the public roster (DB-7).** `public_resident_cards` (0103) returns every resident, not only `is_public_visible` ones, including deceased and adopted, and codes are sequential (`R-0001`…, `0022`, case-insensitive lookup in `src/lib/residents/card.ts:55`), so `/r/<code>` can be walked to inventory every animal the shelter has had. No personal data, so this is privacy and reputation, possibly exactly what a tag that must always answer needs. Decision for the shelter: if the full roster is fine, record it in `docs/decisions/` and close this; if not, return only public animals to signed-out callers with a "Sign in to see this animal" state (staff are redirected to the hub anyway) and add a random suffix to newly printed codes.
- [ ] **Bound sign-ups and make admin two-step non-optional (WEB-3, WEB-4).** (WEB-3) "Continue with Google" creates an `auth.users` row that stays under Access requests, and the anon key can call sign-up directly; `listUsers` reads page 1 only (`admin/security/page.tsx:22`, `access-requests.ts:43`), so junk sign-ups can push real logins off the Security page. Loop the pages or filter server-side; in Supabase turn off open sign-up (admin-created accounts still work) or restrict Google sign-in to the shelter's domain; show requests newest-first with a dismiss that deletes the orphan row. (WEB-4) Enrolment is lazy, so an admin who has never opened Settings → Security has no second factor, and a password-only session can enrol its own authenticator (`admin/security/verify/actions.ts:44-48`). Prompt at first admin sign-in (a banner on My tasks), highlight admins with `twoStep: false` in `UsersTable.tsx:32`, and optionally refuse the admin role until the person has enrolled.
- [ ] **Placement lifecycle and configuration guards (DB-9, DB-10).** (DB-9) Staff can rename or delete the Lifecycle zone and its five pseudo-enclosures (Deceased, Adopted, Hospital, Fostered, Unassigned) that status logic matches by name (0001:522-523; 0026, 0090); add a trigger or protected flag. (DB-10) `scripts/check-migration-grants.mjs:146-191` does not check that a new table enables RLS, that a security-definer function pins `search_path`, or that a new function revokes anon; add those, forbid `grant … to anon` on objects not named `public_*` or `site_*`, run `check-public-views.mjs` in CI against the test database, and expose `site_content`, `site_pages` and `site_content_photos` to anon through fixed-column views (they are base tables with `select *` today, so a new column is world-readable). Small; can share a schema PR with the two above.
- [ ] **Audit trail and soft delete for resident, medical and contact records (DB-6).** Only four tables carry `updated_by` and nothing keeps a before-image; hard deletes are allowed on weights, attachments, maintenance jobs, medications and diet types (0001:532, 0093:89-90). Add `audit_log(table_name, row_id, op, actor, old_row, new_row, at)` written by a security-definer after-trigger on residents, contacts, prescriptions, vet_appointments, weight, attachments and immunization_records, readable by admins only, and prefer `archived_at` over hard delete for medical rows (as contacts and users already do). Half a day for the migration; a "recent changes" page and undo for accidental edits are the payoff and can follow. Schema PR first.
- [ ] **Secrets on the laptop (CODE-3).** `.env.local` holds the service-role key, an account-wide Supabase personal access token, the Google OAuth secret and refresh token and a dev user's password; `.env.deploy.production` holds the production service-role key, database password and Google sign-in secret; `worktree.mjs new` copies `.env.local` into every worktree. Move the PAT and DB password into Windows Credential Manager or a password-manager CLI and inject at run time (the scripts already read `process.env`), use a scoped token for backups and deploys, rotate the DB password and service-role key afterwards, confirm BitLocker is on, and have worktrees copy only the dev keys they need. Half a day plus rotation.
- [ ] **Small code hardening batch (CODE-4, CODE-5, CODE-6, CODE-7, WEB-5, WEB-6, WEB-8, WEB-9).** One PR of trivial fixes: (CODE-4) `csvField` in `src/lib/csv.ts` must prefix values starting `=`, `+`, `-`, `@`, tab or CR with `'` unless they parse as a number; (CODE-5) about a dozen actions return Postgres `error.message` verbatim (`blood-tests/new`, `diets`, `immunizations/new`, `procedures/new`, `vet-visits/new`, `residents/new`) — map unique, check and RLS refusals to the translated sentences (`prescriptions/actions.ts:36-41`, `src/lib/action-result.ts:28-36`) and send the rest through `unexpectedFailure`, which also fits the #441 sweep; (CODE-6) escape backslashes before quotes in the three Drive `q=` names (`drive.ts:473,890,953`); (CODE-7) require `%PDF-` as the first non-whitespace bytes in `file-signature.ts:74`; (WEB-5) minimum password 10 to 12 (`password-change.ts:47`, now 8), no complexity rules, and turn on leaked-password protection in the Supabase dashboard; (WEB-6) run `contact_map_url` (`admin/website/actions.ts:144`) through `checkHttpsUrl` and add the same DB check constraint the social links have; (WEB-8) narrow the `src/proxy.ts:19` matcher to `/_next/static`, `/_next/image`, `/favicon.ico` and use segment-boundary matching in `isPublicPath` as `isPublicPage` does; (WEB-9) set `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL` per environment so `x-forwarded-host` is never consulted (`src/lib/site-origin.ts`).
- [ ] **Defence in depth and hygiene (WEB-7, CODE-8, CODE-9, CODE-10, DB-11).** (WEB-7) One shared helper refusing `Sec-Fetch-Site: cross-site` or a mismatched `Origin` on the five multipart upload routes (`src/app/api/residents/[id]/photos/route.ts` and siblings). (CODE-8) A Cloudflare rate-limiting rule on `/api/*` and `/login` (dashboard, no code). (CODE-9) Bump wrangler (fixes the undici chain), add `npm audit --omit=dev --audit-level=high` to CI as a non-blocking job, pin GitHub Actions to SHAs. (CODE-10) A `getUser()` check in `src/app/manual/pdf/route.ts:37-42`, and optionally a shared layout guard so new pages inherit it. (DB-11) State a retention period for `assistant_actions.request_text` (0070, say 90 days) and add a scheduled delete, or store only the parsed intent.
- [ ] **Confirm dialogs, offline notice and admin button size (WEB-10).** About 25 files use `window.confirm`, including archive, delete and two-step reset on Settings → Security (`UsersTable.tsx:114-145,222`); `ConfirmDialog` (cancel focused, consequences listed) is used in six. Migrate the Security page and every record-deletion confirm to it, naming the thing deleted; give admin table buttons a 44 px minimum height; add a "You're offline, changes will not save" banner from `navigator.onLine` plus a failed-fetch fallback. Improves field use as well as safety.
- [ ] **Session inactivity timeout (WEB-11, Info).** Sessions never expire by design (400-day cookie, no time-box). Keep that, but set a 60-day inactivity timeout in Supabase so a lost or sold phone eventually signs itself out; a phone used weekly never notices. Dashboard setting on dev and production; record it in `docs/decisions/`.
- [ ] **Supabase, Cloudflare and Google checklist (assessment appendix).** Things the code review could not see: sign-up setting, leaked-password protection, auth and MFA rate limits, redirect allow-list, session timeouts, TOTP enabled on the production project; Cloudflare HSTS, WAF and bot rules; Google OAuth consent and Drive folder sharing; the Pi origin and laptop disk encryption. Walk the appendix on both projects and tick what is set. Not code.

## Deployment (before go-live)

- [x] **Write the missing release record for `0.8.0`.** Done 2026-09-28 by the release manager, commit `84a8a31`: `docs/releases/2026-09-27.md` gains a `0.8.0` section, appended to the deploy date beside `0.7.0` as a day log. **Less was lost than feared** — the `>>` redirect added after `0.6.1` meant the whole sequence survived in `deploy.log`, so the drift result, the three applies, `strip-baked-env`, the Worker version and the mail counts are quoted rather than inferred. It also settled what the cut plan could not: drift reported 104 files / 101 applied, so `0096`-`0101` were already on production, every dependency was met, and `0102`-`0104` applied cleanly first. **What is genuinely unrecoverable:** nobody ran `check-public-views.mjs` or `check-app-access-gate.mjs` against production, and this was the release that redefined two public views; the record lists them as not run rather than ticking them because the deploy succeeded. **How it got past the release step:** there is no release step to get past. `deploy.mjs` guards the cut and CI guards test plans on PRs; neither knows about the record, because the record is written *after* the deploy *from* its output. It has no trigger of its own, so it happens only when whoever deploys hands the output to whoever writes records - which held for `0.5.0`, `0.6.1` and `0.7.0` because the session was in the conversation at the time, and did not here. It is the only release artifact with no automated trigger and the only one that cannot be reconstructed later. Two options are in the record, neither built: a nudge at the end of `deploy.mjs` naming the record now owed (the better trade - it fires while the output is still on screen), or a script comparing `latestRelease.version` against `docs/releases/`. Passed to Lutan by the `release-notes-tidy` stream; his call. **Not covered by this tick:** the release that is due (`unreleased` holds five lines) is still outstanding. Found 2026-09-28 by the `release-notes-tidy` stream: `/api/releases/current` reports `0.8.0` on both `test.lannacare.org` and `lannacare.org`, but `docs/releases/` stops at `2026-09-27.md`, which covers `0.7.0`. So 0.8.0 went out without the per-release pass from `docs/release-smoke-test.md`, and without the pre-deploy check that every PR in it had a completed checklist. The stream was dropped: the plan had been to strip the Pet relocation line from 0.8.0 on the grounds that it was never deployed. Since it was, the entry stays as history, and the `unreleased` line saying the page was replaced is correct (Lutan, 2026-09-28). The release manager should write `docs/releases/<deploy date>.md` for 0.8.0 from whatever the deploy output and the release mail still show, say plainly which checks can no longer be done after the fact, and find out how a production deploy got past the release step. Also: the 0.8.0 title says "two new website pages", and one of them has since been replaced. Leave it as history too, unless Lutan says otherwise. A release is also due: `unreleased` holds five lines.

- [ ] **Put the Google Cloud setup under one account.** Lutan, 2026-09-25, while fixing the Drive token. Today it is split: staff Google sign-in is **LCA App** (`endless-bonus-458210-d4`) in Lutan's account (lutan.bennett2@gmail.com), while Drive storage is the client "Lanna Care Drive Access" in **Lanna Care - Dev** (`lanna-care-dev`) in the lanna account (lannaanimalfoundationbwm@gmail.com), which also holds an "LCA Application" web client for the dev Supabase. Finding the right project took a long session. Decide which account owns everything (ideally the shelter's own account, lannacareforanimals@gmail.com, once available; ties in with "Give production its own Drive tree" above), move or recreate the clients there, publish it, re-point Supabase Auth and the Worker secrets, and write down in `docs/decisions.md` which project holds what. **Scope narrowed 2026-09-30 (Lutan): Test and UAT only, everything onto `lannaanimalfoundationbwm` / `lanna-care-dev`; production is separate and later.** Progress: `docs/google-cloud-inventory.md` (inventory + runbook), `docs/decisions/2026-09-30-google-cloud-one-account.md` and a project/account readout in `scripts/check-drive-token.mjs` are in. **Left for Lutan:** the console work in the runbook (recreate the UAT sign-in client in `lanna-care-dev`, re-point Supabase `dbkodyyxxhtygxcxmfcu`, check the consent screen is still In production, Search Console ownership), then retire `LCA App`. Drive needs no move. Item closes when the sign-in client has moved.
Environments exist since 2026-09-21 (README "Environments"): `test.lannacare.org` runs `main` against the dev database; `lannacare.org` runs the production Supabase project (`dbkodyyxxhtygxcxmfcu`, all 63 migrations applied, public views checked, Google provider and redirect URLs set, Lutan seeded as admin). Google Drive is the dev account in every environment for now. What still separates this from a live shelter system:

- [ ] **Cut production over to `lannacareforanimals.org` and turn `lannacare.org` into the pre-prod UAT environment.** The customer bought `lannacareforanimals.org` on 2026-09-23 through **sav.com** — it matches the shelter's own Google account (`lannacareforanimals@gmail.com`), so it is also the right home for the shelter's email later. Not urgent, we are not ready to cut over, but it gates go-live, and the Pi origin item below is worth doing *after* it so the tunnel hostnames are created once, on the right zone. Order of work:
  1. **Host the zone on Cloudflare.** It was not registered there, so: add the site in the Cloudflare dashboard on the same account that holds `lannacare.org`, take the two assigned nameservers, set them in sav.com's domain manager, and wait for the zone to go Active (minutes to a couple of days, depending on the old TTLs). Nothing else can start until it is. Recreate the DNS that matters — the `google-site-verification` TXT record if Search Console is re-verified for the new domain (it is needed for the consent screen's brand verification), and Email Routing if the shelter wants `…@lannacareforanimals.org`.
  2. **Stand up a new Supabase project as production** and demote the existing one. Agreed 2026-09-23 and the right way round: the current production project (`dbkodyyxxhtygxcxmfcu`) already holds the real AppSheet migration — realistic volumes and real edge cases, which is exactly what UAT sign-off needs — while the live database should start clean and be loaded at cutover from a fresh export, with the 2026-09-22 import as the rehearsal. New project → `node scripts/apply-migrations.mjs --env production` applies every migration from zero (no baseline needed; dev was baselined at 0027 only because it predated the runner) → Google provider, redirect URLs and SMTP → seed Lutan as admin → re-run `import-appsheet.mjs` against it at cutover. Then `.env.deploy.production` takes the new project's values and the old ones move to `.env.deploy.uat`.
  3. **Decide where the third project lives** — the open cost question. The free tier allows two projects per organisation and this makes three (dev, UAT, production). Recommended: put **production alone in its own organisation on the Pro plan**, which also buys the live system daily backups and PITR — worth having on its own merits, since `scripts/backup.mjs` exists precisely because the free tier has none, and it makes a UAT mistake unable to touch production's quota or settings. The free alternative is a second organisation for production; free projects pause after a week idle, which the live site never would be, but it leaves prod with no managed backups.
  4. **Point the domains.** Move the `lannacare.org` / `www.lannacare.org` custom-domain routes in `wrangler.jsonc`'s `production` environment to `lannacareforanimals.org` and `www.`, give the new `uat` environment the `lannacare.org` route, deploy both, and set the UAT project ref in `src/lib/app-env.ts` in the same change. No application code changes for the hostname itself: `src/lib/site-origin.ts` derives the origin from the request, so copied tag and QR links (`src/lib/tags/origin.ts`, `CopyTagLink`) follow the domain on their own — but the tags and cards already printed carry `lannacare.org`, so either reprint them at the end of the parallel run or leave `lannacare.org` redirecting the tag paths to production.
  5. **Follow the domain everywhere it is registered outside the repo:** Supabase Auth Site URL and redirect URLs on *both* projects; the Google sign-in client's authorised origins and redirect URIs in the `LCA App` Cloud project (endless-bonus-458210-d4), plus the consent screen's homepage, privacy-policy and authorised domains — the app is **published**, so expect a re-review, and both domains need to be listed while UAT still signs in with Google; the Cloudflare WAF rule and `ORIGIN_HOST` for the Pi tunnel; and the tunnel hostnames in `scripts/pi/cloudflared-config.yml` (`pi.lannacare.org`, `test-pi.lannacare.org` and both `httpHostHeader` lines), `scripts/pi/setup.sh` and `scripts/pi/deploy-pi.sh`.
  6. **Split the Drive trees at the same time.** Production moves to a copy of the folder tree in the shelter's own Google account and UAT keeps the existing one on `lannaanimalfoundationBWM` — see the Drive item below, which the cutover should carry out rather than leave for later. Until that is done, UAT and production would share every photo and archive PDF by file ID, and a tester deleting one would delete the customer's copy.
  7. **Treat UAT as holding real personal data**, because it will: adopter and carer names and contact details, not just animals. Same RLS, same role gating, same care with who gets a login as production — a lower environment is not a reason to be looser. Decide too how UAT stays realistic over time: restoring a recent production backup into it before each sign-off round is the obvious answer, and it doubles as the restore rehearsal the item below asks for.
  8. **Then the docs:** README "Environments" and "Deploying to Cloudflare", `docs/pi-hosting.md`, `docs/data-migration.md`, the usage comments at the top of `scripts/deploy.mjs`, and a `docs/decisions.md` entry for why the old production database became UAT rather than the other way round.
- [ ] **Send all auth mail from one sender domain — Resend's free tier holds one, and seven shelters do not fit.** From the seven-shelter discussion, Lutan 2026-09-28, and it lands at **shelter #2, not shelter #7**. Resend was chosen on 2026-09-23 on the explicit constraint that it "must cost nothing", and the free tier holds **one verified domain**. The cutover item above already notes the near version of this: production on `lannacareforanimals.org` needs a second free account or the paid plan. Seven shelters on seven domains makes that seven sender domains for Supabase Auth's password-reset and invite mail. **The free fix is to stop tying the sender to the shelter's domain**: send every shelter's auth mail from one shared sender — `noreply@lannacareforanimals.org` or similar — whatever domain the shelter's own site is on. One Resend domain, seven shelters, no cost, and it is arguably better anyway because there is one DNS setup to keep working rather than seven. The alternative, a free Resend account per shelter, leans on the free tier in a way not worth building on and multiplies the ways mail can quietly stop. **Decide it at the cutover**, because that is when the production sender domain gets configured and `docs/email-sending.md` §2 gets rewritten — doing it then costs nothing, doing it after shelter #2 means re-verifying DNS. Check what the mail actually says while there: a reset mail from `lannacareforanimals.org` arriving for a user of a different shelter's site needs wording that does not look like phishing. Also confirm whether Supabase Auth allows a per-project sender only (it does) and therefore whether a pooled database forces one sender regardless — if so this is not a choice, it is a consequence, and the wording work is the whole item.
- [ ] **Rehearse a restore from a production backup.** `scripts/backup.mjs` runs weekly (2026-09-21, README "Backups") but the restore recipe in the README has never been run. Once, into a scratch Supabase project — the org's free tier allows two, so this waits for a moment when the dev project can be recreated from a restore, or for a third project elsewhere: `pg_restore` the `public` schema, then `auth` data-only, then check `apply-migrations.mjs --status` and sign in. Correct the README recipe with whatever it took.

Done 2026-09-22 afternoon: re-exported the sheet and diffed every tab against the 2026-09-21 15:34 UTC snapshot — nothing was entered in AppSheet after it, so nothing to re-enter; all seven `drive-unclaimed` scratch folders are in Drive's trash (`Test Dog Alpha (R-0001)` was hiding under `Deceased/` behind its R-code; `Dang 1 (3c1ac03f)` was *not* empty — it held Dang 1's 2025-12-08 blood-test scan that production's attachment points at, so its `Blood Tests/` was moved into `Dang 1 (R-0043)` first, file ID unchanged); Retry archive — which had never worked on Cloudflare (`docs/decisions.md` 2026-09-22, the PDF-pipeline entry, PR #38) — run on all six deceased residents in production, PDFs and offline indexes verified in Drive. **Done 2026-09-23: the AppSheet app is read-only** — every table set to *Are updates allowed?* → Read-Only in the AppSheet editor. The app was not paused, so it stays readable as reference, but nothing can be entered in it any more — which is what made the parallel run safe: from here the two systems cannot diverge, because only one of them accepts writes. Still by hand: the 1–2 week parallel run from the requirements doc §9 — until about 2026-10-06 — reprinting QR / RFID at its end and retiring the AppSheet app and its Apps Script triggers (`archiveDeceasedResidentFolders`, `onBulkUploadFormSubmit`). Original brief: connect the existing Drive photos. Real records, not the dev scratch data; rehearse against the dev database first (it is disposable), then run against production. Same rehearsal for the Drive folder linking.
- [x] **Publish the production Google sign-in app before go-live.** Done 2026-09-22: consent screen in the Google Cloud project `LCA App` (endless-bonus-458210-d4, Lutan's account — renamed from `My First Project`) is **In production**, so any Google account can reach the Supabase sign-in on `lannacare.org` (Supabase still refuses accounts without a role). Google demanded a privacy-policy URL first — hence `/privacy` (#37, `docs/decisions.md`). Branding: app name `Lanna Care for Animals`, home `https://lannacare.org`, privacy `https://lannacare.org/privacy`, authorised domains `lannacare.org` + the Supabase host; scopes `openid`/`email`/`profile` only, so no verification. Client ID/secret remain in `.env.deploy.production` (`GOOGLE_SIGNIN_*`, not read by the app — kept for re-applying to Supabase). The `1036…` Drive/dev clients live in a project under the dev Google account, not here. Brand-verified the same day so the consent screen says "Lanna Care for Animals" rather than the Supabase host: that needed `lannacare.org` verified in Google Search Console (Domain property, Lutan's account) via a `google-site-verification=…` TXT record on the apex in Cloudflare DNS — **keep that record**.
- [ ] **A 90-day token expiry is set somewhere — expect a breakage around 2026-12-21.** On 2026-09-22 Lutan set an OAuth/API token's validity to the 90-day maximum but couldn't later recall which product; it is *not* in the `LCA App` Google Cloud project (checked: Auth Platform Settings, the sign-in client, the consent screen). Candidates: Supabase (Authentication → Sessions, or a personal access token used by `scripts/apply-migrations.mjs`), a Cloudflare API token used by `scripts/deploy.mjs`/Wrangler, or the Drive client's project under the dev Google account. If sign-in, deploys, migrations or the weekly backup start failing in mid-December, start here; when found, either extend it or note the renewal date on this item and tick it.
- [ ] **Give production its own Drive tree on the shelter's account, and leave UAT on the current one.** Decided 2026-09-23, replacing the earlier "transfer ownership of the existing tree" plan: production gets a **copy** of the tree in the shelter's own Google account (`lannacareforanimals@gmail.com`), and the existing tree stays where it is on `lannaanimalfoundationBWM` for UAT, dev and test. The point is isolation — UAT is where a tester deletes a photo, re-runs an archive, or drops a resident to see what happens, and it must not be able to do any of that to the customer's real files. A copy is what buys that; an ownership *transfer* does not, because the old owner keeps edit access to what is now the customer's data, and removing that access breaks every legacy file ID in the UAT database. It also means the shelter owns its own files, which a developer's personal Drive account holding the only copy of the archive never did. Steps: copy `Residents/`, `Projects/` and the rest of the root into the new account (`files.copy` in a small script, structure and folder *names* preserved — the import matches on them); mint a refresh token for that account (`scripts/google-oauth-setup.mjs`); put the four `GOOGLE_*` values, including the new `GOOGLE_DRIVE_ROOT_FOLDER_ID`, in `.env.deploy.production`, and the current ones in `.env.deploy.uat`; `node scripts/deploy.mjs --env production --secrets`. **No remap script needed** — `scripts/import-appsheet.mjs` resolves attachments by walking the tree under `GOOGLE_DRIVE_ROOT_FOLDER_ID` and *recording* the file IDs it finds, so the cutover import against the copied tree records the copies' IDs by itself. **But rehearse it against the dev database first:** the 2026-09-22 import matched resident folders partly by the AppSheet id in their names, and those folders have since been renamed to R-codes, so the resolution that worked then may not work now. Two smaller things to check: the shelter's free 15 GB has to hold the tree and the archive PDFs it will keep generating, and the `1036…` Drive OAuth client lives in a project under the dev Google account — the new refresh token can keep using it, or it moves alongside.
- [x] **Configure SMTP in the production Supabase project before go-live** Done 2026-09-23 for the current project (`dbkodyyxxhtygxcxmfcu`, which becomes UAT): Resend free, `lannacare.org` verified, sender `Lanna Care UAT <noreply@lannacare.org>`, and a real Forgot password mail was received. The new production project at the cutover needs the same on `lannacareforanimals.org` (cutover item step 2; `docs/email-sending.md` last section). (redirect URLs are done). Forgot password (done 2026-09-21) sends through Supabase Auth, whose built-in mailer is development-only. **Provider decided 2026-09-23: Resend free tier** (Lutan; must cost nothing). Lutan creates the account, adds the DNS on `send.lannacare.org` and fills Supabase SMTP. Every field is in `docs/email-sending.md` §2 (lands with PR #62). Resend free holds one domain, so Production on `lannacareforanimals.org` needs a second free account or the paid plan; decide at the cutover.
- [x] **Dev and Test look different from production.** Done 2026-09-22: anything built against the dev Supabase project is teal with a green-cast background and a **Dev** badge in the header; production keeps the orange (`src/lib/app-env.ts`, README "Environments", `docs/decisions.md`).

- [x] **Clear the stocktake counts out of the UAT database — no physical count has ever been done.** Lutan, 2026-09-27, during the role walkthrough. **Done the same day** by `scripts/throwaway-clear-uat-stock.mjs --env production --apply`, dry-run first and verified clear by an independent read afterwards. What was actually there, once looked at rather than assumed: **nine medications and two diets** carrying a `stock_on_hand` figure, and **seven `stock_counts` rows**. Two medications and both diets were typed that morning by accident while signed into UAT instead of test; the other seven medications were typed on 2026-09-25. The seven history rows were the `0093` back-fill, not a stocktake — `counted_by` null, each with its own `stocktake_id` holding one row, which is the back-fill's signature for items edited seconds apart. `record_stocktake()` has never run against UAT. `stock_receipts` was **empty**: the earlier draft of this item said the delivery rows were test data too, and that was wrong — no delivery has ever been recorded on UAT. Nulling `stock_on_hand` was enough for both tables, because `0083`'s trigger clears `stock_counted_at` with it and callers cannot set the stamp directly. Deliberately **not** a numbered migration: a `0101` that deletes stock rows would run against dev too and wipe the walkthrough's own test data. One-off correction to one environment, so a throwaway script through the same Management API endpoint, as `throwaway-utc-date-audit.mjs` was. Delete the script once this is read.

- [ ] **Confirm the Pi's first scheduled backup (Sunday 2026-10-04, 03:00 Thailand), then retire the laptop's.** From the Pi setup (2026-09-30): the Pi's cron job was proven by hand and under `env -i` but has not fired on schedule. On Monday 5 Oct check `ls -l ~/backups/lannacare`, `tail ~/backups/backup.log` on the Pi and the newest dump in Drive `Backups/` (Settings → System status shows it). If it is there, run `scriptsackup-schedule.ps1 -Remove` on the laptop and drop the laptop paragraph from README "Backups". Two schedulers until then is deliberate. A few minutes.
- [ ] **Run the app on its own user on the Pi, separate from the backup.** From the Pi setup (2026-09-30): `lanna-care.service` and the backup cron both run as `lutan`, so a compromised app process could read the dumps and both `.env` files. A dedicated service user for `next start` (owning only what it needs) closes the gap the single-Pi decision records as not done (`docs/decisions/2026-09-30-one-pi-hosting-and-backup.md`). Do it with the security assessment's deployment-surface findings, and after DB-1, which shrinks what a read of the dumps would give.

## Quick wins

- [x] **Move the "Public website" link off the left nav and onto the header logo.** Done 2026-09-22: the roundel + wordmark in `AppHeader` is one `<Link href="/">` that opens in a new tab (`target="_blank"`, `rel="noopener"`), titled and labelled `t.nav.publicSite`; the nav entry is gone and the manual's navigation topic says so. Original brief: Customer request 2026-09-22. `src/app/NavLinks.tsx` lists `{ href: "/", label: t.nav.publicSite }` as an ordinary nav entry; the logo roundel + "LCA" wordmark in `src/app/AppHeader.tsx` is plain markup that links nowhere. Make the logo and wordmark one `<Link href="/">` (with `title`/`aria-label` from `t.nav.publicSite` so it is discoverable and screen-reader friendly), drop the nav entry, and check the phone drawer still has nothing referring to it. Consider opening the public site in a new tab (`target="_blank"`) since staff are mid-task when they click it; decide with the user. Update the manual page that mentions the Public website link if there is one (`src/lib/manual/`).
- [x] **Accordion the Management and Admin nav sections, collapsed by default.** Done 2026-09-22: a chevron button (`aria-expanded` / `aria-controls`) beside the parent link toggles the group; the label still navigates to the landing page. Collapsed on load, auto-opens whenever the route is inside the group, and the user's choice is kept in `sessionStorage` (`nav-expanded`); sidebar and drawer share the state. Original brief: Customer request 2026-09-22. In `src/app/NavLinks.tsx` the two parent items with `children` (Management: dashboard, contacts, vets, medications, diets, translations; Admin: website, security, enclosures, zones, immunization/procedure/blood-test types) always render their children expanded, so an admin sees ~15 links before the fold. Make each parent a toggle (chevron, `aria-expanded`) that starts collapsed on app load, auto-expands when the current route is inside it (so a refresh on `/admin/zones` still shows where you are), and remembers the user's choice for the session (`sessionStorage`, wrapped in try/catch). Clicking the parent label should still navigate to its landing page (`/management`, `/admin`) — put the toggle on the chevron, or expand on first click and navigate on the second; decide with the user. Same behaviour in the phone drawer. Non-admin roles are unaffected (they never see these groups).
- [x] **Remove the Assistant link from the left nav — it lives in the header now.** Raised 2026-09-24 by Lutan. The header button (`AssistantPanel` in `src/app/AppHeader.tsx`, gated by `canUseAssistant`) opens the same conversation as a slide-over on every screen, so the sidebar entry (`src/app/NavLinks.tsx`, its own one-item group between the reference lists and Management/Settings) is a duplicate. Drop that group, the `t.nav.assistant` string if nothing else uses it, and `NAV_ICONS.assistant` likewise. **Decide with the user first:** the nav entry is the only link to the full-page `/assistant` — once it goes, that page can be reached only by typing the URL. Either keep the page as a deep link (and say so in the nav comment), add a "full page" link from the slide-over, or retire the route. Update the user manual topic that tells people to find the assistant in the left nav, and add a line to `unreleased` in `src/lib/releases.ts` — users will notice the entry disappear.
- [x] **A refused page and a booked vet visit both dump you onto the public website.** Lutan, 2026-09-27, Pass 1 (Vet) of the role walkthrough. `/` is the public home page, so every `redirect("/")` in the app throws a signed-in user out of the app entirely — no explanation, no way back except the menu they no longer have. Found when a vet tapped a stocktake recurring job. Four sites, and the last is the worst: `src/app/stocktake/page.tsx:37` and `src/app/deliveries/page.tsx:56` (role refusal), `src/lib/auth/require-management.ts` (every `/management/*` refusal), and **`src/app/vet-visits/new/actions.ts:71`, which is the success path** — *every* role that books a vet visit lands on the public website afterwards, not just vets. **Two different fixes.** The success path should go back to the resident or the visit it just created. The refusals should say "you do not have access to this" on a page inside the app, or fall back to `DEFAULT_SIGNED_IN_PATH` (`/my`) rather than `/` — `signedInLandingPath` in `src/lib/auth/app-access.ts` already knows the difference between an app user and a public viewer, and these guards do not use it. Worth a sweep for other `redirect("/")` calls added since. Small, and it makes every role-gating item below testable without the tester being ejected mid-pass.
- [x] **Show a file-type icon for attachments that are not images.** Done 2026-09-28 (see `docs/decisions.md`): `FileTypeIcon` (lucide `FileText` / `FileImage` / `File`, PDFs in red, an accessible name in both languages) replaces the 📄 glyph in procedures, blood tests, maintenance, project photos and the pending-files list; one `fileKind()` in `src/lib/uploads/file-kind.ts` replaces four copies of the image regex. Original item: Lutan, 2026-09-27, Pass 1 (Vet) of the role walkthrough., noted while procedures passed. Uploading both an image and a PDF to a procedure worked, and both opened. The PDF just has nothing to look at in the list. A small type icon (PDF, doc, generic) where the thumbnail would be makes a list of mixed attachments readable at a glance. Nice to have, not blocking.

## Mobile

- [x] **Review which admin features (if any) belong on mobile.** *Done 2026-09-24: verdict table in decisions.md ("Admin on mobile"); desktop-only setup pages show a "Best on a larger screen" notice below `md`.* Intent: the phone experience stays a field tool — what a volunteer or staff member needs while standing in an enclosure (find a resident, log weight/immunization/vet visit/procedure, move or hospitalise, add photos, check enclosure occupancy). Admin-type work (Admin → Security, Website, Zones, Enclosures, Immunization types, future Vets / Contacts / Projects / Medications admin pages, and any bulk or reporting screens) most likely lives on desktop only, but decide that per screen rather than by assumption. Go through every route under `/admin` plus the desktop-only affordances elsewhere (residents table bulk selection, zone/enclosure filters, edit forms with many fields) and for each record: needed in the field / nice-to-have / desktop only, with a one-line reason. Output is a short table in `docs/decisions.md` and the resulting changes: hide desktop-only nav entries and pages below the `md` breakpoint (or show a "use a larger screen" notice rather than a broken layout), and make sure anything marked field-needed is covered by the Mobile responsiveness sweep below. Also check the role angle — volunteers never see admin pages anyway, so the question is mainly what admins/staff reach for on a phone.
- [x] **Inline-edited rows show stale data until reload.** *Closed 2026-09-24 as not reproducible on Next 16.3.5: with `refresh()` removed, button-driven rename and delete updated the row in place on zones and procedure-types (the page it was reported on). No code changed; see decisions.md, 2026-09-24.* Found 2026-09-21 while building `/admin/procedure-types`: a server action called from a button (`startTransition(() => updateX(...))`, as every admin/management table's Save / Delete / Merge does) revalidates the path but does *not* refresh the client router on Next 16, so the row keeps showing the old name until the next navigation — only `<form action>` submissions refresh on their own. The fix is `refresh()` from `next/cache` after the `revalidatePath` calls in the action (procedure-types does this; see its `revalidateProcedureTypePages`). Apply the same one-liner to the immunization-types, zones, enclosures, security, contacts, vets and medications actions.
- [ ] **Mobile responsiveness sweep.** Go through every screen (residents list, resident hub, forms, admin pages, login) at phone width and fix layout, table overflow, and spacing so it feels professional in portrait. **Start from the admin-on-mobile list** (decisions.md, 2026-09-24, "Admin on mobile"): its field-needed rows (enclosure filters, resident edit, intake) and Security's users-table role column. **Known defect found there:** `/enclosures` scrolls the whole page sideways at 375px (`scrollWidth` 492 on dev data) — the per-zone card grids (`grid gap-3 sm:grid-cols-2 …`) have no column template below `sm`, so the single implicit column grows to the widest card's content (Main Zone section 468px). `grid-cols-1` (`minmax(0,1fr)`) is the likely fix, unverified; the `-mx-6` zone-chip strip also pushes its wrapper to 351px.

- [x] **A date field cannot be cleared once it has been set, on mobile.** Lutan, 2026-09-27, Pass 1 (Vet) of the role walkthrough., found on a diet's end date. Tapping an optional date field commits a value, and there is then no way to get back to empty — so an optional date becomes mandatory the moment it is touched by accident, and the only escape is abandoning the form. Likely affects **every optional date in the app**, not just the diet end date, so fix it in the shared date input rather than one form: a visible Clear control beside a populated optional field, and make sure the empty value actually reaches the server as null. Check the desktop behaviour too — the native picker may allow clearing there, which is exactly how this stays invisible to whoever built it.
- [x] **Residents list on a phone: drop the Resident ID column.** Done 2026-09-30: the ID `<th>`/`<td>` are `hidden md:table-cell`; comment and manual caption updated. Lutan, 2026-09-29. Below `md`, `/residents` still shows two data columns, the R-code and the name (plus the copy-link and edit icons), and the ID pushes long names (with their Thai and other names) into wrapping, so each row is taller than it needs to be. Hide the ID `<th>` and `<td>` below `md` the same way the other columns are (`hidden md:table-cell` in `src/app/residents/ResidentsTable.tsx`, around lines 136 and 181), so the name gets the width; desktop keeps the column. Update the comment above the table ("Phones show only ID and name") and the manual caption in `src/lib/manual/en.ts` (~line 282: "Phones show just the ID and name"; there is no Thai manual file yet). The R-code is still on the resident hub, and search by R-code (the adopted-residents search item) is unaffected. Check at 375px with a long bilingual name, and that tapping a row still opens the resident. A releases line (staff will notice the tidier list). No schema.

## Resident operations

- [x] **Record updates from adopters on an adopted resident.** Lutan, 2026-09-26: adopters send news from time to time, and it should go on the animal's record. An adopted animal's hub gets an **Adoption updates** section: date, who sent it (the adopter contact from the Adopt placement's `carer_id`, preselected), how it came in (LINE, Facebook, email, visit), a note and photos. Photos go through the existing resident photo route into a `Residents/<Name> (<ID>)/Adoption updates/` folder. **Keep each photo tagged with its update** (Lutan, 2026-09-26): the photo's record links to the update, so wherever it appears (the update itself, the resident's Photos tab, a future public "Happy endings" card) it shows where it came from: sent by the adopter, on which date, via which channel (LINE, Facebook, email, visit). Photos taken by staff at the shelter stay untagged, so the two can be told apart and filtered. Adopted residents are not locked like the deceased, so no trigger change is needed; check RLS for staff and management. Probably its own table (`adoption_updates`) rather than notes on the placement, so there can be many, in a schema PR first. **Nice follow-on, ask first:** with the adopter's permission, a published update could feed the public "Happy endings" strip on `/adopt` (0061), a strong adoption story for the website. Manual (residents list search, adopted hub), both dictionaries, a releases line.
- [x] **My dashboard — "what I need to do today", starting with my maintenance jobs.** Lutan, 2026-09-25. A page for the signed-in user (e.g. `/my`, first entry in `NavLinks.tsx` for every role) listing the work assigned to *them*. **Version 1: maintenance only.** Every job assigned to the user that is not `Done` (user in `maintenance_assignees`, 0063; a job can go to a team, so show who else is on it), grouped by `due_date` (0033) — overdue, due today, later, no due date — with status, zone and a link to `/maintenance/[id]`, plus quick status changes through the existing maintenance actions — no new write path. Empty state that says there is nothing assigned. No schema needed: assignment and due date already exist (confirmed by Lutan, 2026-09-25). **Built to grow:** later sources plug into the same page as further sections, each a small loader returning a common task shape (title, what it is about, when, link, done action). Named so far: residents to take to the vet (vet visits already exist under `/vet-visits`), giving medications to a zone of animals (prescriptions, grouped by zone), and ordering stock (the stock-on-hand work in progress). Most of those need an *assignee* they do not have today, so each is its own later item with its own schema PR. Leave assignment of those out of v1. Also consider a count badge on the nav entry and whether this becomes the page staff land on after sign-in (today `/` is the public home for everyone). Manual topic in `src/lib/manual/en.ts` / `th.ts`.
- [x] **Recurring jobs for staff, feeding My dashboard.** [done 2026-09-26: schema `0095` (#155), then Management → Recurring jobs (`src/app/management/recurring-jobs`) and the Recurring jobs section of My tasks (`src/lib/my-tasks/recurring.ts`), including the sick-week hand-over form; see docs/decisions.md 2026-09-26.] Lutan, 2026-09-25. Let management set up jobs that repeat on a calendar and are assigned to a person (or a team, like maintenance, 0063). Examples: "stocktake of on-hand medication, every Monday morning, staff member X", then "order and buy medication, every Monday after the stocktake is done, staff member Y". **Templates vs occurrences:** a `recurring_jobs` template (title, description, assignee(s), recurrence rule, time of day such as morning/afternoon, optional link to the screen the job is done on, active flag) produces *occurrences* that are transient. Each one shows on the assignee's My dashboard (the item above) for its day and is marked done or skipped, and only that result is kept (who, when, a note), so there is a record that the stocktake happened. Decide whether occurrences are generated ahead (a cron in `worker/`, or on first read of the day) or computed from the rule and stored only when acted on; the second avoids a job table filling with future rows. **Recurrence:** a small, explicit subset rather than full iCal RRULE: every N weeks on chosen weekday(s) (weekly, fortnightly with a start date as the anchor), monthly on day N, monthly on the Nth weekday ("first Monday"), with a start date and an optional end date. Dates are in Asia/Bangkok. **Order between jobs:** "after the stocktake" means an occurrence can depend on another template's occurrence the same day, and shows as "waiting for stocktake" until that one is done. **Missed jobs:** decide whether an undone occurrence stays overdue on the dashboard until done or skipped (suggest yes, with how many days late) or quietly expires. **Admin:** a Management page to create, edit, pause and reassign templates, with a preview of the next few dates, and reassignment when a login is archived. The stocktake example links naturally to the "Stock on hand" item below; the job can deep-link to its count screen, `/stocktake` (`?tab=diets` opens the Diets tab), which staff and volunteers can use (0091). Needs its own schema PR first. **Reassigning one week** (Lutan, 2026-09-26): if someone is sick, management can hand that date's occurrence to someone else without changing the job, and the next week goes back to the usual person; the schema for it is in `0095` (cover team per date, `reassign_recurring_job()`, docs/decisions.md 2026-09-26), and the admin page needs the form, including "all of X's jobs for these dates → Y". Manual topic in `src/lib/manual/en.ts` / `th.ts`.
- [ ] **Deceased residents: confirm what happens to the archive when photos or the bio change after death.** Customer question 2026-09-22 — check the business logic and close the gaps. *How it works today:* the three edits still allowed on a deceased resident (0052: bio via `/residents/[id]/edit`, photo upload via `src/app/api/residents/[id]/photos/route.ts`, set-profile-photo and delete-photo in `src/app/residents/[id]/photos/actions.ts`) each call `refreshDeceasedArchiveIfNeeded()` (`src/lib/archive/refresh-deceased-archive.ts`), which re-runs `archiveDeceasedResident()` — the folder move no-ops and the summary PDF and `index.html` in `Residents/Deceased/<Name> (<ID>)/` are replaced in place, so the archive follows the record. *Gaps to decide on:* (1) it is **best effort and silent** — every caller ignores the returned `{ error }`, so if Drive is down the edit saves but the PDF/index stay stale and nobody is told; either surface it (hub banner "Archive out of date — Retry", reusing the existing Retry button) or record a `deceased_archive_stale_at` on `residents` that the hub shows. (2) It only runs when `deceased_archived_at` is set: a resident whose first archive never completed does not get one from an edit — fine, but the hub should say so. (3) A multi-photo upload regenerates the PDF once **per file** (sequential uploads); cheap for a handful, but debounce or run once after the batch if it turns out slow. (4) A HEIC profile photo is never embedded in the PDF (`EMBEDDABLE_IMAGE_TYPES`), so choosing a phone photo as profile after death gives a PDF without a picture — convert on upload or note it in the manual. (5) Verify in dev end to end: mark a test resident deceased, add a photo, change the bio, set a different profile photo, delete a photo — open the PDF and `index.html` in Drive after each step and confirm the change is there; write the outcome into the manual's deceased topic.
- [ ] **Regenerate the six production deceased-resident summary PDFs once the layout fix is live.** Filed 2026-09-24 from `claude/deceased-pdf`. Every archive PDF generated before that fix has the resident's name printed over the subtitle and no footer (name, ID, generated date, page N / M), and a deploy does not rewrite them — only a bio/photo edit on the deceased resident, or the hub's Retry for an archive that never completed, calls `archiveDeceasedResident()`. Six residents in production have one. Release-time work, after the deploy that carries the fix: regenerate each (re-saving the bio unchanged should do it via `refreshDeceasedArchiveIfNeeded()` — confirm that a no-op save actually triggers it, or add an admin "Regenerate archive" action if not) and open each PDF in Drive to check.
- [ ] **Foster carer portal (future).** New RBAC role with access scoped to the residents currently fostered with that carer (`resident_current_state.current_carer_id`), allowing them to log weight, immunizations, vet visits and appointments. Needs a `user_id` on `contacts` (or a join table) to link a login to a carer, plus RLS policies keyed off it.
- [x] **Low priority: check the residents list's filter and search logic for adopted residents.** Done 2026-09-30 as a hardening, **not a reproduction**: the disappearance was not reproduced, and (a)–(c) are the code-explained causes; (d) was not checked against live data (see `docs/decisions/2026-09-30-residents-list-adopted-search.md`). Search now matches the R-code, an adopted-matches pointer appears when a place, zone or enclosure filter hides them, and there is an Adopted chip. Lutan, 2026-09-26: a search for adopted Panda with Show all on once found nothing, but it could not be reproduced later the same day, so this is an investigation rather than a known bug. The code says adopted residents should appear. `resident_list_view` (0058) has no status filter, and `src/app/residents/page.tsx` hides only Deceased (`NOT_DECEASED`). Adopted is left out only by the On-site / Off-site place filter (`STATUSES_IN_PLACE` in `src/lib/residents/place.ts`: "Adopted and Deceased are history, not a place"). Likely causes to check: (a) the list was on **On-site** or **Off-site**, where adopted animals never show and the Show all toggle is ignored (`showAll` needs Everywhere), yet nothing says so; (b) a **zone chip** was selected, and adopted animals sit in the Lifecycle pseudo-zone; (c) the search was by **R-code**, but search only matches name, Thai name and other names; (d) RLS or data on the site used (the adopted row's `current_status`). **Improvements worth making anyway:** make search reach adopted (and deceased) animals from any place, the way a deceased name match already offers "show" (`deceasedMatchesHref`), e.g. "2 adopted residents match, show"; add R-code to the search; and consider an **Adopted** chip or "Past residents" view so staff can find them on purpose rather than by accident.

- [x] **Move a resident photo to a different folder when it was filed wrongly.** Lutan, 2026-09-27. A photo uploaded under the wrong category — typically recorded as **Shelter** when it is really a **Medical** photo — can today only be deleted and uploaded again, which loses its date taken and caption and is easy to skip. Add a **Move to folder** action in the photo viewer (`src/components/PhotoGallery.tsx`) offering the other `PHOTO_CATEGORIES` (Shelter / Medical / Foster / Adoption). **It matters for privacy, not just tidiness:** `0101` keeps Medical photos off the public site by `attachments.sub_folder`, and the migration's own note says it "cannot catch a medical photo filed in the wrong folder; that is for staff to refile" — this item is that refile. **Two halves, both needed:** update `attachments.sub_folder`, and move the Drive file to `Residents/<Name> (<ID>)/Photos/<Category>/<YYMM>/` (reuse `ensureResidentPhotosFolder` in `src/lib/google/drive.ts` with the photo's own date taken, then change the file's parents), so Drive and the database never disagree. Do the Drive move first and write the row only once it succeeds. **Rules to settle in the build:** moving a photo *into* Medical when it is the resident's profile photo must be refused or clear the profile photo, the same rule as `src/lib/residents/profile-photo.ts`; a role limited to one folder (a vet can only upload to Medical) should only be able to move photos into that folder, not out of it; adoption-update photos (dated folders, no category) are out of scope. Works for deceased residents too, since photos stay editable after death (decisions.md 2026-09-21) — check the archive path. Manual: a line in the photos topic in `en.ts` / `th.ts`; a releases line.
- [x] **Microchip number on a resident, searchable from a chip scanner.** Done 2026-09-30 across 0113, PR 221, 0116 and `claude/microchip-vet-feature` (0117); see the second-half item below and `docs/decisions/2026-09-30-microchip-second-half-vet-form-public-boolean.md`. Not built: a header search (the app has none) and the optional duplicate-scan box on the intake wizard's first step. Lutan, 2026-09-29. Two reasons: overseas adoptions need it (a microchip is the first thing an export permit and the destination country ask for, and `/adopt/international` already lists it), and the app should encourage shelters to chip their animals rather than treat it as optional. **Why now, when it isn't standard practice:** `0060_adoption_recommendation.sql` and `docs/decisions.md` (2026-09-21) left the field out because microchipping isn't done as a matter of course in Thailand. That is still true (Lutan, 2026-09-29); the field is wanted anyway, to support overseas adoptions, to make chipping easy for the shelters that do it, and as groundwork for the public register item under Public website. Add a `docs/decisions.md` entry saying so, which extends rather than contradicts the 0060 one. "Microchipping" already exists as a procedure type (0031), so the act of chipping can already be logged, just not the number. **Schema PR first:** `residents.microchip_number text` (nullable), stored as digits only (strip spaces and dashes on save), **unique** across residents where set (a partial unique index, so a mistyped duplicate is caught), with a check that allows the ISO 11784/11785 standard 15 digits and, if the shelter has older chips, 9/10-digit ones too (ask Lutan whether any exist before tightening it). Optional `microchip_implanted_on date`. Locked with the rest of the record on death (the 0026 trigger's column list). **Where it is entered:** Edit resident and the intake wizard (Health step), and offered straight after a "Microchipping" procedure is logged ("Record the chip number?"). Show it on the hub's details card, in the deceased archive PDF and index, and on the vet-visit and procedure views where a vet needs it. **Scanner search:** handheld and USB/Bluetooth microchip readers act as a keyboard: they type the 15 digits and press Enter. So (1) the `/residents` search and the header search match `microchip_number` exactly (after stripping non-digits), alongside name and R-code; an exact chip match jumps straight to that resident's hub, whatever the place/zone filters or deceased toggle say; (2) a small **Scan a chip** box (autofocused input, e.g. on `/residents` and the intake wizard's first step) so a staff member can scan a stray at the gate: a known chip opens the resident, an unknown one offers "New resident with this chip" prefilled. Phones cannot read the common 134.2 kHz (FDX-B) chips over NFC, so do not promise phone scanning; test with a real reader Lutan has, or by typing into the box. **Privacy and public site:** the number stays staff-only. Do not add it to any `public_*` view; the public resident page may show "Microchipped: yes" (a boolean derived from the column), which is the flag 0060's "Desexed / Vaccinated" ticks were meant to have. Vets see it for residents in their scope. **Encouraging chipping:** a count on the management dashboard (residents without a chip), a filter on `/residents` ("No microchip"), and a gentle prompt on the hub for a resident marked ready for adoption with no chip. Keep it a nudge, not a block. Manual (intake, resident hub, a short "Scanning a microchip" topic), both dictionaries, a releases line. Read it beside the multi-shelter spike (Architecture): a chip number is globally unique, so the same animal turning up at two shelters is a real case to design for there. **Schema half done 2026-09-29 (0113, see `docs/decisions/2026-09-29-microchip-number-on-residents-15-digits-staff-only.md`):** `microchip_number` (exactly 15 digits, unique where set) and `microchip_implanted_on` on `residents`, locked on death, no public view touched; `check-public-views.mjs` now refuses anon both columns. **The check is 15-digit only** (Lutan, 2026-09-29: no 9/10-digit chips), so ignore the "9/10-digit" allowance above. **Entry, display and scanner search done 2026-09-29 (PR 221):** number and implant date on Edit resident and intake, shown on the hub with a no-chip nudge, and the Scan a chip box on `/residents` (exact match jumps to the hub, unknown chip offers a prefilled new resident). **Still to do:** see the follow-on item below (vet entry, procedure prompt, archive PDF, vet views, public boolean, dashboard count, No microchip filter).
- [x] **Microchip, second half: vet entry, procedure prompt, archive, public flag, dashboard nudge.** Done 2026-09-30 (0117, see `docs/decisions/2026-09-30-microchip-second-half-vet-form-public-boolean.md`): one Record chip / Correct form through `set_resident_microchip()` on the hub, the Vet appointments and Procedures tabs and a visit's Edit page, for admin, staff and vets in scope, with each refusal mapped to its own message; "Record the chip number?" after a Microchipping procedure; the chip in the deceased summary PDF and offline index; "Microchipped" on `/adopt/[id]` from `public_resident_profiles.is_microchipped` (boolean only); an in-care "no microchip" count on the management dashboard; a No microchip filter on `/residents`; the Scanning a microchip manual topic (English only; no Thai manual yet). The optional intake duplicate-scan box was not built. Lutan, 2026-09-29. The rest of the item above after PR 221. **Vet entry (asked for 2026-09-29):** a vet must be able to add or correct a chip from their own interface. Vets can read residents in their clinic's scope (0108) but have no update on `residents`, so this needs a **schema PR first** (`claude/microchip-vet-schema`, next free number after `0114`): a security-definer function, e.g. `set_resident_microchip(resident_id, number, implanted_on)`, that allows staff and a vet whose resident is in `current_vet_resident_ids()`, refuses a deceased resident, strips nothing (the app strips; the 15-digit check and unique index still apply) and does not widen any other column. Then the feature stream: a vet form on the resident hub and vet views, using that function. **Also:** offer "Record the chip number?" straight after a Microchipping procedure is logged; the chip in the deceased archive PDF and index; on the vet-visit and procedure views; **"Microchipped: yes" on `/adopt/[id]`** as a boolean derived from the column (a `public_*` view exposes the boolean only, never the number, and `check-public-views.mjs` must still refuse anon the number); a count on the management dashboard (residents with a chip vs without); a "No microchip" filter on `/residents`; the Scanning a microchip manual topic and Thai text if a Thai manual exists by then. The duplicate-scan box on the intake wizard's first step is optional. **Vet-entry schema done 2026-09-29 (0116, see `docs/decisions/2026-09-29-vet-microchip-write-via-definer-function.md`):** `set_resident_microchip(p_resident_id uuid, p_number text, p_implanted_on date default null) returns void`, security definer, for admin, staff and a vet whose resident is in `current_vet_resident_ids()`. It refuses a deceased resident (`restrict_violation`) and a vet outside scope (`insufficient_privilege`), and leaves the 0113 check and unique index to refuse malformed or duplicate numbers (`check_violation` / `unique_violation`), so the app must strip first. Null clears the chip, and the date is overwritten with what is passed, so the form should always send both. The vet form and the rest are the feature stream.

## Medical records

- [x] **Stocktake page: count everything in one go.** Lutan, 2026-09-25. Stock on hand shipped in 0.5.0 (0083) as one editable cell per row on Management → Medications and → Diets (`updateMedicationStock` and its diet twin), so a stocktake of 100 medications means 100 separate edits.
