Add one count sheet, e.g. `/management/stocktake` with Medications and Diets tabs: every item in a list with its unit, the last count and when it was taken, and an input for today's count, plus search and a sort that follows how the cupboard is laid out if the shelter can tell us (name for now). **Save once:** one Save sends every changed row in a single server call that writes them in one transaction (an RPC), so a stocktake is all-or-nothing and every row gets the same counted-at time. Show a summary of what changed before saving (old → new, and the big differences highlighted). **Blank vs unchanged:** in today's cell a blank clears the count back to "not counted" and re-saving the same figure confirms it (0083). On the sheet, a blank input must mean "not counted this time, leave it alone", with a per-row "same as last time" tick to confirm an unchanged figure. Don't let the two meanings mix. **For phones:** staff will do this walking the shelves, so numeric keypad, Enter moves to the next row, big tap targets, and a warning before leaving with unsaved counts. Same roles as today's stock edit. Link it from both Management tables, and later from the "stocktake every Monday" recurring job (Resident operations) as its screen. Manual: the stock section of the Management topic in `src/lib/manual/en.ts` / `th.ts`. **Done 2026-09-26** at `/stocktake` rather than under `/management`: Lutan widened it to staff and volunteers, so `0091` made `record_stocktake` security definer and the page sits with the daily pages in the menu. Name order still; the cupboard order is a follow-up.
- [x] **Actual usage from stocktakes, compared with planned usage.** [done 2026-09-26 as count-to-count change against the plan, not "actual usage": count history is `0093`, the page is Management → Stock between counts (`src/app/management/stock-usage`). Stock received is **not** recorded, so part (2) — `stock_receipts` and "Record a delivery" — is split out as its own backlog item; CSV download and the percentage column were left for it, since both only mean something once receipts exist. See docs/decisions.md 2026-09-26.] Lutan, 2026-09-26. Work out what was really used of each medication and diet between two stocktakes, and set it beside what the prescriptions and diets said would be used over the same dates, so big gaps stand out (waste, spillage, doses not given, theft, or a forecast that is simply wrong). **The gap in today's data:** `stock_on_hand` (0083) is one figure per item, overwritten at every count, so the previous count is gone. The formula `actual used = previous count + stock received − new count` needs two things that are not recorded: (1) **count history**, a `stock_counts` row per item per stocktake (item kind medication/diet, item id, counted quantity in the item's unit, `counted_at`, `counted_by`, stocktake id so one session's rows group together), written by the stocktake save call. `0088_record_stocktake.sql` has merged without it, and the stocktake sheet is in review (#143, with schema PR #140 for 0091). So add the table in the next schema PR: fold it into #140 if that is still open, otherwise its own small PR. Then have the stocktake save call write the history rows, so every stocktake from then on counts. (2) **stock received**, because without deliveries every restock looks like negative usage: a `stock_receipts` row (item, quantity, received on, supplier contact optional, cost optional, note), entered from a "Record a delivery" action on Medications / Diets. It later meets the "order and buy medication" recurring job and the Cashflow actuals follow-up. **Planned usage** already exists: `medication_forecast(from, to)` (0044) and `diet_forecast(from, to)` (0051) over the dates between the two counts, living residents only. **Page:** Management → Usage (or a tab on Medications / Diets). Pick two stocktakes (default: the last two). One row per item: planned, received, actual, difference, and difference as a percentage. Rows over a threshold are flagged (e.g. ±20% and more than a minimum quantity, so tiny items don't shout). Sort by the biggest gap, with a link to the item and a CSV download like Cashflow's. **Cautions to show on the page:** an item counted in only one of the two stocktakes has no result (say so, don't show zero). "As needed" prescriptions and per-resident diet overrides make the plan approximate. A resident who arrived, left or died mid-period is already handled by the forecast functions, but check it. Receipts that were forgotten will look like overuse, so the first line of any big gap should say "check deliveries were recorded". **Depends on** the Stocktake page (Medical records) and should follow it in the plan. Manual topic, both dictionaries, and a `docs/decisions.md` entry for the formula and the threshold.
- [x] **Record stock deliveries, so Stock between counts can state usage.** [done 2026-09-27 as `claude/stock-deliveries`: /deliveries, and Stock between counts shows received and used from `stock_count_intervals`; the CSV / percentage / floor extras split out as their own item] Split out 2026-09-26 from "Actual usage from stocktakes" (done as `claude/stock-usage`). Management → Stock between counts shows count-to-count change beside the plan, but cannot say what was used: `used = previous count + received − new count` and nothing records **received**. Add `stock_receipts` (item kind + medication_id / diet_type_id as `stock_counts` does, quantity in the item unit, unit at receipt, received_on, supplier contact optional, cost optional, note, recorded_by), entered from a "Record a delivery" action on Management → Medications / Diets (and perhaps the Stocktake sheet). Schema PR first. Then the page can show received and actual used, and its rise / "less than planned" wording changes: a rise stops being "a delivery nobody logged" once deliveries are logged (though forgotten ones still look like overuse — keep a "check deliveries were recorded" line on big gaps). Carried over from the original item: a CSV download like Cashflow, a difference-as-percentage column, and a minimum-quantity floor so tiny items do not shout. Related: the plan reads low for residents who have since left (forecasts filter by status today) — a forecast over placement history would fix that; see docs/decisions.md 2026-09-26. Later meets the "order and buy medication" recurring job and Cashflow actuals.
- [x] **Stock between counts: CSV download, difference as a percentage, and a floor so tiny items do not shout.** [done 2026-09-27 as `claude/stock-counts-followups`: a Difference column with the percentage (a dash when nothing was planned), Download CSV through Cashflow's writer moved to `src/lib/csv.ts`, the floor as what two counts can get wrong per item (one of a counted unit, 5% of the stock for one read by eye), and a Before / After tag on the recent-deliveries list. See docs/decisions.md 2026-09-27.] Split out 2026-09-27 from "Record stock deliveries" (done as `claude/stock-deliveries`), which carried these over from the original usage item. Now that the page shows *used* from recorded deliveries: a CSV of the table like Cashflow's, the used-vs-planned gap as a percentage beside the quantity, and a way to stop tiny items being marked — decisions.md 2026-09-26 explains why a single absolute floor does not fit (units differ per item), so this is probably per-unit or per-item, or a minimum on the planned figure. Also worth a look while there: the recent-deliveries list shows two deliveries recorded before and after a same-day stocktake at the same minute, so it cannot show which side of the count each fell — a "before / after the stocktake" tag would.
- [ ] **Medication label photos, a card-by-card stocktake on phones, and Stocktake only for the people who do it.** Lutan, 2026-09-27. Three parts, one theme: make the count quicker and harder to get wrong. **(1) Label photo on medication setup.** On Management → Medications each medication gets an "Upload label" action (take a photo on a phone or pick a file; replace; remove), and the photo shows as a thumbnail in the table and on the stocktake. It is there so a person holding a box can match it at a glance, when counting and when a delivery comes in (the "Record a delivery" form from the Stock deliveries item should show it too). Store it on Drive like the other uploads (Shelter Friend logos, resident photos), as a `label_drive_file_id` on `medication`, served through the existing image route and resized for mobile. Schema PR first (next number is one past the highest on `main` — check open `*-schema` PRs before claiming it). Worth deciding in the same PR whether `diet_types` gets one too, so the Diets tab can use the same card later; the schema costs one column. Return `{ok, error}` from the upload action rather than throwing (the #441 lesson), with the size limit and file type checked up front. **(2) Card-by-card medication stocktake on phones.** On a narrow screen the Medications tab of `/stocktake` becomes one card at a time, like a dating app: the label photo large at the top (a placeholder with the name when there is none), and below it the name, strength/form, unit, the last count and when it was taken. Under that a big numeric input with the keypad already open, a **Save** button, and **Same as last time** and **Skip** as secondary actions. Saving moves the card on to the next medication with a short slide; a progress line says "23 of 100", and there is a way back to the previous card and to the list. Swipe gestures are a nice extra but buttons must do everything (one-handed use, accessibility). The desktop sheet stays as it is. **Saving:** the sheet today saves once at the end through `record_stocktake()` (0088, 0091, history in 0093), all-or-nothing. On the cards a phone could lose a half-done count, so decide between saving each card as it is entered (one stocktake id shared across the session so `stock_counts` rows still group as one stocktake) and keeping the counts on the device and sending them together at the end with the summary; record the choice in `docs/decisions.md`. Either way keep the sheet's rule that blank means "not counted this time", and that "same as last time" is an explicit confirmation, not an empty save. Skipped cards come round again at the end ("5 skipped — count them now?") before the summary. **(3) Stocktake in the menu only for the people who do it.** Today `canStocktake(role)` (`src/lib/management/stocktake.ts`, `STOCKTAKE_ROLES` = admin, management, staff, volunteer) shows Stocktake to everyone. Change it to admin and management always, plus any staff member or volunteer who is an assignee of an active recurring job whose `link_path` starts with `/stocktake` — on the job itself (`recurring_job_assignees`) or through a one-date hand-over (`recurring_job_occurrence_assignees`, so someone covering a sick week gets it for that week). Apply the same check in three places, not only the menu: `NavLinks.tsx`, the page guard in `src/app/stocktake/page.tsx`, and the save (`record_stocktake()` is security definer since 0091, so it must check this itself, which probably means a small SQL function such as `can_stocktake(uid)` in the schema PR). Decide whether a hand-over grants access only on its date or for the week around it, and whether a team assignee counts every member. Test with a staff login that is not on the job (no menu entry, the URL redirects, a direct save is refused) and one that is. Manual: the stocktake section in `src/lib/manual/en.ts` / `th.ts` (who can see it, the phone cards, how to add a label photo), both dictionaries, and a line in `src/lib/releases.ts`. Related: "Stocktake in cupboard order" (Management) — the cards should follow that order once it exists.
- [ ] **Units of measure: buy and count in one unit, feed or dose in another, with a conversion per item.** From the backlog page's suggestion box (2026-09-26), confirmed by Lutan 2026-09-29. Dry kibble is bought in 20 kg bags but given to dogs by the cup; medicines arrive by the box or bottle but are given by the tablet or ml. **Today each item has exactly one unit**, used for everything: `diet_types.unit` (0051: "daily_qty_* and cost_per_unit are in this unit") and `medication.dose_unit` (0027). Stock on hand (0083), stock counts (0093) and deliveries (`stock_receipts`, 0096, which records the unit at receipt and expects "2 boxes of 50" to be converted by hand before saving) all use that same unit. So staff either count kibble in cups or feed in kilograms, and the forecast, stock and cost figures are only as right as that mental arithmetic. **Shape:** keep the item's existing unit as its **base unit** (the one feeding or dosing is recorded in, and what the forecasts already compute), and add a small per-item **conversion table**: other units it is bought, received or counted in, each with how many base units one of it holds (e.g. kibble: base `cup`; `kg` = 10 cups, `bag (20 kg)` = 200 cups; a tablet medicine: `box` = 50 tablets, `strip` = 10). Per item, not global, because a cup of one kibble weighs differently from another. Mark one conversion as the **purchase unit** and one as the **count unit** (can be the same) so forms default sensibly. **Where it is used:** "Record a delivery" takes a quantity in any of the item's units and stores both what was entered (quantity + unit) and the base-unit equivalent. The stocktake sheet (and the phone cards in the item above) counts in the count unit, e.g. "3 bags and 4 kg" if mixed entry is worth it, and saves base units through `record_stocktake()`. Stock on hand and days-of-stock can be shown in the purchase unit ("2.4 bags, about 11 days"). Cost moves to a **price per purchase unit** (what the receipt says: 850 baht a bag), with cost per base unit derived for the diet and medication forecasts and Cashflow. Doses, prescriptions, diets and forecasts stay in the base unit, so nothing there changes. **History must not shift:** existing counts and receipts are in today's single unit, which becomes the base unit, so they need no rewrite. Editing a conversion factor later must not silently re-value past rows, so each row keeps the base-unit amount it was saved with (0096 already snapshots the unit for the same reason). **Schema PR first** (a conversions table keyed on medication or diet type, like `stock_counts`' item-kind pair, plus the entered quantity/unit columns on `stock_receipts` and `stock_counts`); admin and management edit conversions on Management → Medications / Diets. Decide while building: whether a conversion factor can be approximate (a cup of kibble) and whether the page should say so; mixed-unit entry on the stocktake or one unit per line; and whether this settles the related open item "A single-cell stock edit writes no history". Check Stock between counts and Cashflow arithmetic end to end with a real kibble example. Manual (Medications, Diets, Stocktake, Deliveries topics), both dictionaries, a releases line and a `docs/decisions.md` entry for the base-unit rule. **Schema half done 2026-09-30 as `claude/schema-units-of-measure` (0118; see `docs/decisions/2026-09-30-units-of-measure-schema.md`) — the feature half builds against this:** table `item_unit_conversions` (`item_kind` + `medication_id` / `diet_type_id` as in `stock_counts`; `unit`, `base_units_per` > 0, `is_purchase_unit`, `is_count_unit` with at most one of each per item, `note`; unique per item by lower-cased unit; admin and management write, admin / management / staff / volunteer read). The base unit is the item's existing `dose_unit` / `unit`, unchanged. **The point-in-time factor** is `entered jsonb` on both `stock_receipts` and `stock_counts`: `[{"quantity","unit","factor"}]`, a copy of the factors in force when the row was saved, so editing a conversion never re-values history; `quantity` / `counted_quantity` stay the base-unit total and a CHECK requires sum(quantity × factor) to equal it. Null `entered` = entered in the base unit (all existing rows). `record_stocktake(jsonb, jsonb)` keeps its signature and now accepts an optional `entered` per element beside `id` and `count` (count in base units). Deliveries are written straight to `stock_receipts`: set `quantity` (base) and `entered` together. No cost column: price per purchase unit ÷ `base_units_per`. Still to do in the feature half: the forms and pages, refusing a conversion named like the base unit, stamping `factor` from the live conversion at save time, and the open decisions above.
- [x] **Stock on hand and days-of-stock.** Neither medications nor diets record what's actually in the cupboard, so the forecasts say what will be used but not when it runs out. If the shelter wants that: a `stock_on_hand` (and maybe `reorder_at`) figure on `medication` and `diet_types`, edited in place on the two management pages, and a "days of stock" column computed from the 30-day rate — with a flag when it's inside the reorder lead time. Split out of the medication requirement page item when that closed (2026-09-21).

- [x] **Doctors belong to a vet: investigate a managed list rather than free text.** Lutan, 2026-09-27, during Pass 0 of the role walkthrough. A clinic has many doctors — Novel has around 20 on staff — and the concern raised was that the relationship is modelled the wrong way round. It is not: `doctor_name` sits on `vet_appointments`, not on `vets`, so one vet already has many doctors. What is missing is anywhere to **manage** them. The column is free text by Lutan's own call on 2026-09-24 (`0074`, `docs/decisions.md`), with the cost written down at the time — "Dr Somchai" and "Somchai" stay two strings, so counting visits per doctor means grouping by hand — and that migration closed with "Promoting it to a lookup is its own item if that is ever wanted". This is that item. Today the only affordance is `loadDoctorNamesByVet` (`src/lib/vets/doctors.ts`), which harvests the distinct names already typed against each vet and offers them as a `datalist` on the vet-visit form. That stops a name being typed three ways, but a doctor nobody has recorded yet is not suggested, a typo becomes a permanent suggestion, and there is no screen where a clinic's roster can be seen or corrected. **To investigate:** whether a `vet_doctors` table (vet_id, name, active) with a nullable `vet_appointments.doctor_id` beside the existing text column earns its keep; how the names already recorded would be reconciled into it; and whether the shelter will actually maintain a 20-name roster per clinic or would rather keep typing with better suggestions. Schema-first as its own PR if it goes ahead, and the free-text column stays for historical rows either way. Note for whoever picks this up: the walkthrough's Pass 0 line "Management → Vets has at least one vet, and at least one has a doctor name" was wrong — the vet record has no doctor field — and has been corrected in `docs/role-walkthrough.md`. **Decided 2026-09-27 (Lutan): build both.** Schema half is #176 (`0102_vet_doctors_and_vet_accounts.sql`, applied to dev): a `vet_doctors` list per clinic that fills itself from typed names by trigger, `doctor_name` kept and following the list, `merge_vet_doctors`, `p_doctor_name` on `schedule_bulk_appointments`, and `user_roles.vet_id` + `current_user_vet_id()` for the vet-account→clinic link items 4 and 8 need. **Feature half still to do:** the list on the clinic hub (view, rename, deactivate, merge), the booking form passing `p_doctor_name` instead of its second write, suggestions from the list instead of `loadDoctorNamesByVet`, choosing a vet account's clinic in `/admin/security`, manual and release-notes line; tick this item then. **Done 2026-09-28** (`claude/vet-doctors-roster-screen`): Management → Vets → Doctors lists each clinic's doctors with their visit counts, and renames (saying how many visits the new spelling reaches), merges (pointing out look-alike spellings such as "Somchai" / "Dr Somchai"), marks a doctor as left, and adds or deletes an unused one; the clinic hub shows the list read-only; booking passes `p_doctor_name` in the one RPC call; the visit forms suggest the clinic's active doctors. The `/admin/security` clinic choice had already shipped with the vet-scope work.

- [x] **A single-cell stock edit writes no history, but the `0093` back-fill treated past ones as counts.** [done 2026-09-29 as `claude/stock-edit-history`, migration `0112`: Lutan chose one way in — `record_stock_correction()` writes a `source = correction` row, kept out of usage intervals; see docs/decisions.md 2026-09-29] Found 2026-09-27 while clearing UAT's stock figures. `record_stocktake()` writes a `stock_counts` row per item (`0093`), and the stocktake sheet goes through it. `updateMedicationStock` and its diet twin (`src/app/management/medications/actions.ts`) do not: they `update` the column directly, so `0083`'s trigger stamps `stock_counted_at` and nothing lands in the history. The result is that **Stock between counts cannot see a figure typed on the Management page** — four such edits on UAT this morning left no trace in `stock_counts` at all. What makes it more than a design choice is that `0093`'s back-fill *did* capture exactly those edits, one synthetic stocktake each, because all it had to go on was `stock_on_hand` and `stock_counted_at`. So a cell edit made before `0093` is history and the same edit made after it is not, which is the kind of split nobody will reconstruct later from the data. **To decide:** either route the single-cell edit through `record_stocktake()` so there is one way in, or leave it out of history on purpose and say so on the page — and either way `0093`'s comment should stop implying the two agree. Worth settling before the first real count, since that is when the history starts being worth something.

- [x] **A vet's photo upload should be limited to the Medical folder.** Lutan, 2026-09-27, Pass 1 (Vet) of the role walkthrough. **Done 2026-09-27** (`claude/medical-photos-profile`): no picker for a vet, and the upload route answers 403 to any other folder. Confirmed a vet can add photos from the resident hub, which is intended (`assertPhotoWriteAccess` allows every app role), but the folder picker offers all of them. A vet has no reason to file into the shelter's general or project folders, and a misfiled clinical photo is awkward to find later. Restrict the choice to **Medical** for the vet role — ideally by not showing the picker at all when there is only one valid destination, so it reads as "this goes in Medical" rather than a one-item dropdown. Enforce it on the server too, not only in the form: the upload route is the thing that has to refuse.
- [x] **Five more places take a UTC day for a shelter day.** Found 2026-09-28 by `claude/utc-visit-date`, grepping `slice(0, 10)` across `src/` after fixing the three visit forms. Each takes the leading date of a stored UTC timestamp as a shelter day, so it is a day early between 00:00 and 07:00 Bangkok. (1) `residents/[id]/ResidentHub.tsx` builds `today` from `now.slice(0, 10)` (`now` is `new Date().toISOString()` from the page), so a prescription that ended yesterday still counts as active on the hub until 07:00. (2)–(4) `hospital/return/ReturnFromHospitalForm.tsx`, `rehome/RehomeForm.tsx` and `rehome/return/ReturnToShelterForm.tsx` set the date input's `min` from `placement_history.start_date.slice(0, 10)`. That column is `timestamptz`, and `placementStartDate()` stores `now` for a same-day move, so a placement made at 03:00 has a `min` one day too early. The server still refuses the date, but with an error rather than a greyed-out day. (5, minor) `maintenance/MaintenanceBoard.tsx` falls back to `updated_at.slice(0, 10)` against a shelter-day cutoff. Fix with `todayIso(new Date(x))`, as `visitDate()` does. Checked and fine: `immunizations/new/actions.ts` (date arithmetic on a date), `lib/management/cashflow.ts` (a `date` month), `lib/status/usage.ts` (Cloudflare analytics days are UTC by design).
- [x] **Vet visit form: scope the clinic picker, and reconsider the Status dropdown.** **Done 2026-09-27** (`claude/vet-visit-form-scoping`, see `docs/decisions.md`): both halves. A vet account books and edits visits for its own clinic only, checked in the actions too; a vet account with no clinic is refused (Lutan). The clinic is set in Security, which gained a Clinic choice for vet accounts. Status stays a dropdown, because a past visit still Scheduled is how the app shows overdue, and cancelled comes from the deceased cascade; its options now say what they mean. Original: Lutan, 2026-09-27, Pass 1 (Vet) of the role walkthrough. Two things on the booking form. **(1)** The Vet dropdown lists every clinic. When a doctor is recording their own visit it should offer the clinic that doctor works in, not the shelter's whole list — which depends on the doctor/clinic hierarchy item, since today nothing links a doctor to a clinic or a vet account to either. **(2)** Status is a dropdown, but it looks derivable from the appointment date — a visit in the future is scheduled, one in the past happened. If it is genuinely automatic, it should be a **label showing the derived state**, not a field asking the user to agree with the calendar; if it is not automatic (a cancelled or missed visit is neither), then the dropdown is right and the options should say so. Settle which before changing it — turning a real field into a label loses the cancelled case.
- [x] **One weight per vet visit: hide visits that already have one.** Lutan, 2026-09-27, Pass 1 (Vet) of the role walkthrough. Recording a weight offers a linked vet visit, and the list includes visits a weight has already been recorded against, so the same visit can carry two. Filter the picker to visits with no weight yet, and back it with a constraint rather than only the form — a unique index on the linking column is the version that survives a second tab and an import. Decide what happens to any duplicates already recorded before adding the constraint, and whether correcting a weight means editing the existing row (it should) rather than adding a second. **Also one weight per DAY** (Lutan, 2026-09-27): a resident may have only one weight recorded on a given date, not just one per visit. Two independent constraints on `weight` (`0001`, plus `vet_appointment_id` from `0028`): unique on `vet_appointment_id` — **partial**, `where vet_appointment_id is not null`, or every unlinked weight collides — and unique on `(resident_id, date)`. The per-day rule is the stricter one and will have far more existing violations, so count those first. Check the intake path before adding it: `0029_intake_weight.sql` records a weight at intake, so a resident taken in and seen by a vet the same day is a legitimate-looking collision the per-day rule would refuse — decide whether intake wins, the visit wins, or it overwrites. **Done 2026-09-27:** duplicates counted first — none on dev (23 readings) or in the AppSheet snapshot (18); `0106` (#187) adds both indexes, unlinks extra visit links and refuses same-day pairs by name if it ever meets them. Intake: the visit's reading *corrects* the intake reading in place (new kg, linked to the visit) — `/weight/new` says so as soon as a taken day is picked and saves as a correction. Correcting any reading is `/weight/[id]/edit` (Edit on the Weight tab, Edit weight on its visit). Picker: `loadLinkableVisits` in `src/lib/vets/linkable.ts`, which also hides future visits (`notInFuture`) — for the prescription item below.
- [x] **A prescription should not be attachable to a future vet visit.** Lutan, 2026-09-27, Pass 1 (Vet) of the role walkthrough. The linked-visit dropdown on a prescription lists visits that have not happened yet. A prescription written against a visit that is still scheduled is either a mistake or a plan, and the record cannot tell which afterwards. Limit the picker to visits on or before today. Same question as the weight item about whether the rule belongs in the database as well as the form; a check against the appointment date would do it.
- [x] **Blood test, procedure and hospital forms default a visit's date from its UTC day.** Found 2026-09-28 by `claude/prescription-no-future-visit`. `BloodTestForm.tsx`, `ProcedureForm.tsx` and `residents/[id]/hospital/page.tsx` each take `appointment_date.slice(0, 10)` — the leading date of the stored UTC timestamp — as the visit's day. For a visit between 00:00 and 07:00 Bangkok that is the day before, so a blood test or procedure logged from such a visit defaults to the wrong date, and the hospital page's "visit is not in the future" test is a day early. `visitDate()` in `src/lib/vets/linkable.ts` now returns the shelter day (`todayIso(new Date(...))`), which the weight and prescription forms use; switch these three to it. Small, no schema; check each against a visit at 03:00 Bangkok.

## Facility

- [ ] **A facility map: find your way round the site and open an enclosure from it — scope first.** Lutan, 2026-09-28. Lutan has drawings of the facility floor plan. Investigate how to bring them into the app as a map people use to get around: tap an enclosure on the plan to open its details and the residents in it (`/enclosures/[id]`), and see at a glance where things are. **The deliverable is a short scope with a recommendation and a prototype, not the finished build**, agreed with Lutan before building. **Questions to answer:** (1) **The drawings** — what format they are in (PDF, CAD, photo of paper) and how many (one site plan, or one per building / zone). Get copies and store them in Drive or the repo. (2) **How the map is drawn** — options, cheapest first: the drawing as a background image with clickable areas laid over it (SVG polygons in percentages, so it scales on phones); a traced SVG of the plan, which is sharper and themeable but more work; or real map coordinates (a mapping library on a satellite base), probably overkill for one site. (3) **How enclosures are placed on it** — an admin "place on map" editor (draw or drag a shape per enclosure, stored as e.g. `enclosures.map_shape` jsonb plus a `facility_maps` table for the plan images, one per zone if there are several) rather than hand-editing coordinates, so it stays correct when enclosures are added or renamed. **Enclosures not on the map** (off-site / fosters, see the On-site / Off-site filter) are simply not placed. (4) **What the map shows** — status on each shape using data the enclosures page already has: occupancy, open maintenance, special diets; zones outlined and labelled; a "you are here" is only possible with GPS and is probably not worth it indoors. (5) **Where it lives** — a Map tab or toggle on `/enclosures` next to the list is the natural home; or it may belong in the new Shelter Operations section once the Settings/Management review settles where Enclosures goes. **Mobile matters most**: it is used walking round the site, so pinch-zoom, pan and large tap targets on a phone; check it works on the Pi origin / Worker budget (an SVG, not server-rendered images). (6) **Public side** — whether visitors ever get a map (probably not; the enclosure QR codes already cover that) — say so either way. **Schema, when built,** is its own `-schema` PR first, as usual.
- [x] **Standard diet: flag it, make a diet mandatory at intake, and show special diets on enclosure cards.** Lutan, 2026-09-25. Three parts, in order. **1. Flag the standard diet (schema PR first).** `diet_types` (0051) has no such flag today; the standard is only known by its name, "Standard Kibble + Chicken" (seeded in 0069). Add `diet_types.is_standard boolean not null default false` with a partial unique index so **only one diet type can be the standard**, and set it on the existing standard by name (re-runnable). On Management → Diets, show which diet is the standard and let management make another one the standard, which moves the flag in one transaction. **2. A diet is mandatory at intake and defaults to the standard.** Today it is optional: `IntakeForm.tsx` offers "None yet — add from the hub later" and the hint says "Optional". Remove that option, preselect the standard diet, and reject a missing diet in `src/app/residents/new/actions.ts` and in `record_intake` (`p_diet_type_id`), not just in the form. Check for residents living at the shelter with no current diet (anyone added since 0069 seeded the rest) and backfill them with the standard in the schema PR, or list them for staff to fix, whichever Lutan prefers. Update the intake wording in both dictionaries. **3. Enclosure cards show when a resident needs a special diet.** A resident is on a special diet when their current `resident_diets` row (start_date ≤ today, end_date null or ≥ today) points at a diet type that is not the standard. On `/enclosures` (`EnclosureGrid.tsx`), a card with one or more such residents shows an icon with short text (e.g. "2 special diets"), with the residents' names on hover/tap. Use an icon and text, **not colour**, because the card's colour already shows capacity (`OccupancyIndicator.tsx`). On `/enclosures/[id]`, mark those residents with their diet name. Staff views only, not the public `/e/` card. Manual: the intake and Enclosures topics in `src/lib/manual/en.ts` / `th.ts`.
- [x] **A public view behind enclosure QR codes.** Done 2026-09-24: `public_enclosures` (0079, residents embedded as their `public_resident_cards` rows, Lifecycle excluded) and `/e/<id>` — signed out, the enclosure's name, zone and a card per resident linking to `/r/`; signed in, `/enclosures/<id>` as before; Lifecycle ids 404 like unknown ones (`docs/decisions.md`). Raised 2026-09-22 when the tag links landed (#41, `docs/decisions.md`): a resident's card (`/r/<code>`) now shows a visitor a public card and a signed-in user the hub, but an enclosure's QR code (`/e/<id>`) still sends a visitor to sign-in, because there is no public enclosure page. If visitors are expected to scan the codes on the kennels — a walk round the shelter reading who lives where — give `/e/<id>` the same shape as `/r/`: signed in → `/enclosures/<id>`; signed out → a public page with the enclosure's name (and Thai name), zone, and the residents in it as cards linking to their `/r/` pages. Needs a schema PR first: an anon-readable view (say `public_enclosure_residents`: enclosure id, names, zone, and per resident the card fields from `public_resident_cards` 0068) — occupancy against capacity, notes and maintenance stay staff-only, and the Lifecycle pseudo-enclosures (Hospital, Fostered…) must not appear. Then `/e/` joins `PUBLIC_PATH_PREFIXES` / `isPublicPage` in `src/lib/public-paths.ts`, `check-public-views.mjs` gets the view, and the manual's enclosure-page topic says what a visitor sees. Decide with the user whether an enclosure page should show the residents at all, or only the enclosure — a visitor who scans a kennel is looking at the animals in it, so residents is the useful answer, but it is the shelter's call.
- [x] **Filter the enclosures page to those with open maintenance.** Done 2026-09-24: `?maint=open`, a Has open maintenance tick beside search and sort; counts jobs on the enclosure only, not zone-wide ones (`docs/decisions.md`); hides the Lifecycle cards; hidden and ignored for vets. Raised 2026-09-24: a "Has open maintenance" toggle on `/enclosures` (beside the zone filter and search in `EnclosureFilters`, kept in the URL like `zone`/`q`, e.g. `?maint=open`) that shows only enclosures with a job not `Completed`. No migration: `src/app/enclosures/page.tsx` already loads open jobs per enclosure into `open_jobs`, so it is one more `.filter()` plus the control, an i18n string (en + th) and a line in the manual topic. Decide while building: zone-wide jobs (`enclosure_id` null, counted in `zoneWideJobs`) — either count them for every enclosure in that zone or leave them out and say so in the manual; and hide the pinned Lifecycle cards (Hospital / Unassigned / Fostered) when the filter is on. Vets cannot read `maintenance` (RLS), so for them the filter would always be empty — hide it for the vet role.
- [x] **Filter the enclosures page by Internal / External, cascading into the zone filter.** Done 2026-09-25 (see `docs/decisions.md`): Everywhere / On-site / Off-site (`?place=`) above zone chips that are now multi-select toggles (`?zone=<id>,<id>`); switching place drops zones that do not belong to it; Lifecycle is offered and shown only under Everywhere. `/residents` left as a follow-up. Original brief: Raised 2026-09-24: on `/enclosures`, add an Internal / External / All choice beside the zone filter, search and the open-maintenance tick in `EnclosuresFilters`. It narrows the zone list to zones with that `zones.internal` value (0004 — no schema needed; the page already selects `internal`), so a user can pick "all external" or "all internal" and then choose a reduced set of zones within it. Today the zone filter is a single `?zone=` select, so "a reduced list" means it becomes multi-select (e.g. `?place=internal&zone=<id>,<id>`); with no zones ticked the Internal / External choice alone applies. Changing Internal / External drops any chosen zones that no longer belong to it. Decide where the Lifecycle pseudo-zone sits (it is neither a place on site nor off it) and whether its status cards still show under a filter, as `?maint=open` hides them. Consider whether `/residents`' zone filter should get the same cascade for consistency.
- [x] **Give `/residents` the same On-site / Off-site cascade as `/enclosures`.** Done 2026-09-25 (see `docs/decisions.md`): the same Everywhere / On-site / Off-site control and multi-select zone chips, one shared component (`PlaceZoneChips`) and one set of helpers in `place.ts` for both pages. Places are decided by status, so Unassigned is On-site and Hospital / Fostered are Off-site; Adopted and Deceased only under Everywhere, where Show all lives. The Location column shows the same place. Original brief: Raised 2026-09-25 as the follow-up the enclosures filter item asked to consider: `/enclosures` now has Everywhere / On-site / Off-site (`?place=`) narrowing multi-select zone chips (`?zone=<id>,<id>`), with stale zones dropped on a place switch and Lifecycle offered only under Everywhere (decisions.md, 2026-09-25). `/residents` still has its own single zone select and an Internal / External column; bring it in line, reusing `src/lib/enclosures/place.ts`. Decide how it meets the existing Show all / deceased toggle and where Hospital / Fostered residents land, since on `/residents` they are real residents rather than status cards.

## Admin

- [x] **Bug: Settings → Security shows "Minified React error #441" instead of saying what went wrong.** Reported by Lutan 2026-09-26 with a screenshot on the dev-coloured site: an action on the archived `manager@gmail.com` row (Restore or Delete) failed and the row showed only "Minified React error #441; visit https://react.dev/errors/441…". **What #441 is:** React's production stand-in for *any* error thrown from a Server Action (`resolveErrorProd` in `react-server-dom-*-client.browser.production.js`). In a production build the real message is stripped before it reaches the browser, so `throw new Error(...)` in a `"use server"` file never shows its text to the user, **not even the friendly translated ones** (`t.admin.security.errors.cantDeleteOwnAccount` and the like). **So it is two fixes. (1) This page:** `src/app/admin/security/actions.ts` throws in every action (create, approve, change role, temporary password, archive, restore, delete). Make each return a result (`{ ok: true } | { ok: false, error: string }`) and have `UsersTable.tsx` / `AccessRequests.tsx` / `CreateUserForm.tsx` show `error`. Map known failures to plain words in both languages. Log the raw error server-side. **The failure in the screenshot is confirmed** (Lutan, 2026-09-26): it was **Delete** on a login with records against it. About 20 foreign keys to `auth.users` have no delete rule, so a delete is refused as soon as the person created anything: `created_by` on ten tables (from 0001 on), `uploaded_by`, `updated_by`, `user_id` on `assistant_actions` (0070), and others. **Don't fix it by changing them to `on delete set null`:** that would erase who recorded what, and that history is the point. Archive (0063) is the right tool for someone who has left. Instead, Delete is for accounts with no history (a typo, a test login). Before deleting, check the references (a small `security definer` function that counts them, or catch the error), and either hide Delete for such users or answer "This person has records in the system, so they can't be deleted. Archive them instead." **(2) The same pattern across the app:** at planning time 13 `"use server"` files under `src/app` held 98 `throw new Error` lines. Every one that can happen in normal use (validation, "not allowed", a clash) shows #441 in production. Sweep them to the return-a-result shape (the `upload-worker-error` fix already did this for uploads). Keep `throw` only for genuinely unexpected failures, and give those a generic "Something went wrong, please try again" plus the error's digest so it can be looked up in the Worker logs. Do the Security page first as its own small PR, then the sweep. The manual needs no change. Add a releases line (people will see real messages).
- [ ] **Server Actions across the app: return a result instead of throwing (#441, part 2).** The Security page was converted on 2026-09-26 (`claude/security-action-errors`): its actions return `ActionResult` from `src/lib/action-result.ts` and use `runAction()` / `unexpectedFailure()` — see decisions.md, "Server Actions return a result, not a throw". The other `"use server"` files under `src/app` still `throw new Error(...)` for ordinary refusals (validation, not allowed, a clash), and every one of those shows "Minified React error #441" in production — including `assertAdminRole()` on the other `/admin` pages. Sweep them to the same type (and fold the existing near-duplicates, `FriendActionResult`, `ProjectActionResult`, `MaintenanceActionResult`, `TranslationActionResult`, into it). Verify against a production build (`next build` + `next start`); dev mode shows the real message and proves nothing. Probably one PR per area so they can run alongside other streams. Add a releases line. [`/admin` done 2026-09-28 (`claude/admin-actions-result-sweep`) `/residents` done 2026-09-29 (`claude/actions-result-sweep-residents`) contacts done 2026-09-29 (`claude/actions-result-sweep-contacts`) and management done 2026-09-30 (`claude/actions-result-sweep-management`) — see docs/decisions/; remaining areas: projects and maintenance, and their near-duplicate types `ProjectActionResult` and `MaintenanceActionResult` (`FriendActionResult` and `TranslationActionResult` are folded; translations lives under `/management`, so management took it). Management skipped `shelter-friends/actions.ts` (contacts' file) and `cashflow/fixed-outgoings` (already converted); `assertManagementRole()` is deleted. Residents had no near-duplicate type of its own. Shared helpers in `src/lib/placements/*` and `src/lib/archive/*` still return `{ error }` (the assistant uses them too); the residents actions translate at the boundary]
- [x] **Settings → System status: a health and usage page for admins.** Lutan, 2026-09-26. One read-only page (e.g. `/admin/status`, a card on the Settings landing page, admin only) showing whether the system is healthy, with a few plain usage figures. **Health, each a green/amber/red tile with the time it was checked and the error text when red:** database reachable (a trivial query, with its latency), Google Drive connected (a token refresh plus read access to `GOOGLE_DRIVE_ROOT_FOLDER_ID`; the token lapse of 2026-09-25 is exactly what this should have shown first), migrations in step with `main` (reuse what `scripts/apply-migrations.mjs --status` computes), the running release and when it was deployed (`src/lib/releases.ts` / the Worker version tag), release mail configured, the last weekly backup and its age (needs `scripts/backup.mjs` to record a run somewhere it can be read, e.g. a row or a Drive file timestamp), and the Pi origin once it is live (reachable, or "serving from the Worker fallback"). **Usage, with a period picker (7 / 30 / 90 days):** staff sign-ins and active users (from `auth.users.last_sign_in_at` or an audit of sign-ins), records added (residents, vet visits, weights, maintenance jobs), photos uploaded, assistant requests (`assistant_actions`), and public-site visitors. **Visitors need care:** `/privacy` promises no analytics tracking, so count them without cookies or trackers, from Cloudflare's own request analytics for the Worker (GraphQL Analytics API, aggregate counts only) or Cloudflare Web Analytics, and check the privacy text still holds. **Build notes:** run the checks server-side with short timeouts and cache them for a minute or so, so the page never hangs on a dead dependency. Never show secret values. Keep each check a small function so a later "alert me" (email or LINE when a tile goes red) can reuse them. **Overlap:** batch 1's `drive-upload-errors` stream is adding a Drive-token check; build this page on that check rather than writing a second one. Manual: a short Settings topic in `src/lib/manual/en.ts` / `th.ts`.
- [x] **System status: alert me when a tile goes red.** Claude, 2026-09-26, follow-up to the System status page. Email (and later LINE) to admins when a health check in `src/lib/status/health.ts` turns `fail`, and again when it recovers, so nobody has to open the page to find out. Every check already returns `{ state, error, facts }` through `runCheck` (timeout, never throws, secrets scrubbed); alert on `fail` only, since `off` is deliberate (decisions.md 2026-09-26). Open questions: where it runs (a Worker cron trigger is the obvious home, but the checks import Next-side code), how to remember the last state so one outage is one mail rather than one per run, and whether release mail's Cloudflare send_email binding (verified addresses only) is enough or LINE is needed first.
- [ ] **System status: set up the visitor count.** Claude, 2026-09-26. The Website visitors tile on `/admin/status` is grey until the Worker has `CLOUDFLARE_ANALYTICS_TOKEN` (a Cloudflare API token with Zone → Analytics → Read on lannacare.org, created by Lutan in the Cloudflare dashboard) and `CLOUDFLARE_ZONE_ID`. Put both in each `.env.deploy.*` and run `deploy.mjs --secrets`, then confirm the tile turns green. Its GraphQL query (`httpRequests1dGroups`, src/lib/status/usage.ts) has never run against the live API, so fix it there if Cloudflare rejects it. The counts cover the whole zone, test site included; if that proves misleading, filter by host with `httpRequestsAdaptiveGroups` instead (check the free plan's range limit first).
- [x] **System status: a card showing access requests awaiting approval.** Lutan, 2026-09-27. Access requests are only visible on Settings → Security, and that page now needs a 2-step-verified session (`src/lib/auth/two-step.ts`, 0100) — so an admin has to step up just to find out whether anyone is waiting, and in practice nobody looks. Put a card on `/admin/status` (admin only, readable at a normal sign-in) that shows **how many** requests are waiting and how long the oldest has waited, with a link to Settings → Security that leads through the step-up. **Show the count, not the people:** names and emails stay behind 2-step verification, so the card says "2 access requests waiting, oldest 3 days" and nothing that identifies who. **Counting:** use the same definition as `src/app/admin/security/page.tsx` — an auth user with no `user_roles` row — so the card and the list can never disagree; ideally move that filter into one shared helper both call (service role, server side, short timeout like the other checks). Amber when any are waiting (it needs action, nothing is broken), neutral when there are none; not red, and so not an alert under the "alert me when a tile goes red" rule unless Lutan wants an email for new requests too — ask. Check archived users are not counted as requests. Manual: a sentence in the System status topic, en/th; a releases line.
- [x] **A logo or photo upload that exhausts the Worker shows the user a minified React error.** Done 2026-09-25: it was not the Worker. Next caps a Server Action body at 1 MB and rejects a larger one before the action runs, so every logo or Website photo between 1 and 15 MB failed where no app code could catch it (reproduced on dev: HTTP 500, `E394 Body exceeded 1 MB limit`). `next.config.ts` now lets the app's one limit through (`src/lib/uploads/limits.ts`, 15 MB, also used by every upload route), the clients pre-check the size and turn any thrown failure into words (`runUploadAction`), and `proxy.ts`'s 10 MB body cap was raised with it. What a Worker can really process is still unmeasured — see the follow-up item. Found 2026-09-24 on production, uploading a Shelter Friend logo: the page printed `Minified React error #441` and a link to react.dev, which decodes to "An error occurred in the Server Components render" — the message itself is stripped in production builds. The same image failed twice and a smaller one succeeded, so it is size- or format-dependent. It is **not** the app's own validation: `MAX_FILE_BYTES` (15 MB) and `ALLOWED_MIME_TYPES` both `return { error }` with readable text before Drive is touched, and the Drive calls themselves sit in a `try/catch` that returns a friendly message. So a file that passes validation is breaking *during* the upload, where nothing catches it. The likely mechanism: `drive.uploadFile` (`src/lib/google/drive.ts`) builds a multipart `Blob` holding the whole file in memory inside the Worker, and on the free plan that can exhaust memory or the 10 ms CPU budget — the same `1102` family as the render pressure the Pi origin is meant to fix. **Two things to do, and they are independent.** (a) Catch it: wrap the action so a resource failure reaches the user as "that image is too large to process — try one under about 2 MB" rather than a React error code. A user cannot act on `#441`, and it reads as the app being broken. (b) Decide the real limit: 15 MB is inherited from `admin/website/actions.ts` and is almost certainly higher than a Worker can actually process, so the number should reflect what works rather than what the form accepts. **The same limits and upload path are used by `/admin/website` photos and blood-test attachments** (`src/app/api/blood-tests/[id]/attachments/route.ts`), so whatever is wrong is wrong in three places. Worth re-testing after the Pi origin lands, since rendering and uploads move to real CPU and memory then and the failure may simply go away — but the error message should be fixed regardless, because the next resource limit will produce the same unreadable page.
- [ ] **Measure the largest upload a Worker actually completes, and set the limit from it.** Follow-up to the #441 fix (claude/upload-worker-error, 2026-09-25), which found the reported failure was Next's 1 MB Server Action body cap, not the Worker, and raised the framework limits to the app's 15 MB (`src/lib/uploads/limits.ts`). Whether a free-plan Worker can buffer, parse and re-send a 15 MB file inside its CPU and memory budget was never measured. On test.lannacare.org, upload real photos of about 3, 6, 10 and 14 MB through a Shelter Friend logo and a resident photo, and read each request's CPU time and outcome in the Worker's observability logs. If anything below 15 MB hits 1102, lower `MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES` to what works (the `fileTooLarge` strings name the number, in en and th). The user now sees a readable message either way, so this sets the right number rather than fixing a broken page. Moot for requests the Pi origin serves once it is live.
- [x] **Refuse an "image" upload that isn't a real image.** Done 2026-09-26: `src/lib/uploads/file-signature.ts` checks the leading bytes on all seven Drive upload paths and refuses a mismatch in en/th before Drive is touched (a zero-filled `mid3.jpg` is now refused on every one); Website hero/gallery and Shelter Friend logos read the new file back before switching, and send the replaced or removed file to Drive's trash instead of deleting it. The dev hero had already been reset to a real photo by then; production's `hero_drive_file_id` was not checked from the worktree (handed to Lutan). Lutan, 2026-09-26. The dev (and test.lannacare.org) home page lost its hero photo: the hero is now `mid3.jpg` (Drive `1NAFh8O_pR5RBYTVjditj4nWo8qW1NL-2`), 3 MiB of zero bytes, uploaded on 2026-09-25 while testing the 15 MB upload limit (`docs/test-plans/upload-worker-error.md`, §4). It was accepted because every upload path trusts the browser's MIME type (`ALLOWED_MIME_TYPES`) and never looks at the bytes. Replacing the hero also permanently deleted the previous hero file from Drive, so the old photo can't be restored. **Fix:** check the file's leading bytes against the formats accepted (JPEG `FF D8 FF`, PNG `89 50 4E 47`, WebP `RIFF….WEBP`, GIF, HEIC `ftyp` box) in one helper beside `src/lib/uploads/limits.ts`. Call it from every image upload: Website hero and gallery, Shelter Friend logo, resident, project and maintenance photos. Refuse a mismatch with a readable en/th message before Drive is touched. Attachments that may be PDFs check `%PDF` the same way. **Also consider** not deleting the previous hero or gallery file until the new one is saved and confirmed readable, and moving it to Drive's trash rather than deleting it, so a bad upload can be undone. **Meanwhile:** set a real hero photo on dev under Settings → Website. If lannacare.org shows a broken hero too, check its `site_content.hero_drive_file_id` separately: that is the production database, which the upload test did not touch.

- [ ] **Review which pages belong under Management and which under Settings — the split is inconsistent.** Raised 2026-09-24 by Lutan ("Maintenance versus Setup", confirmed to mean the Management and Settings landing pages). Reference lists sit on both sides with no clear rule: medications (with its frequency options, see the item below) and diets are under Management (`/management/*`), while immunization, procedure and blood-test types, enclosures and zones are under Settings (`/admin/*`); contacts and vets moved from Settings to Management on 2026-09-21 and `/admin/contacts` / `/admin/vets` are still redirect stubs. **The rule (Lutan, 2026-09-27) — use it to discuss and slot every page:** **Settings = configurable items for the system** — the lists and options other screens pick from, set up once and changed rarely: immunization types, units of measure, frequencies (and by the same test procedure and blood-test types, zones, diet and medication option lists). **Management = operational information that is dynamic** — records about real people and things that change as the shelter runs: contacts, vet information and the like. A quick test: if editing it changes what a dropdown elsewhere offers, it is Settings; if it is a record someone looks up or updates in the course of the work, it is Management. Where one page mixes both (e.g. medications as stock vs. the medication/frequency options), split it along that line rather than forcing it to one side. **Add a third section: Shelter Operations.** A new left-nav entry, built on the same pattern as Management and Settings — one landing page of grouped tiles — to gather the day-to-day operational activities that are currently loose top-level nav links or scattered (candidates: enclosures, maintenance, stocktake, projects, and whatever else the review turns up; resident records and My day probably stay top-level, decide in the table). This leaves three homes: **Shelter Operations** = doing the daily work, **Management** = the dynamic records behind it, **Settings** = system configuration. Deliverable: a table of every tile on both landing pages and every left-nav entry (Maintenance, Stocktake, Projects, Enclosures, Vets, Contacts…) with its current home, who uses it and how often, and its proposed home — Shelter Operations, Management, Settings or top-level — under that rule. Agree it with Lutan, then build the Shelter Operations landing page and nav entry (role guards as for Management) and move the pages: tiles, routes (keep redirects from old URLs), role guards, `en.ts`/`th.ts`, the manual topics and `docs/decisions.md`. Fold in or supersede the frequency-options item below if the rule settles it.
- [ ] **User preferences: pick a colour theme.** Requested 2026-09-23 by Lutan. Let each user choose the app's look and feel from a small set of colour themes (e.g. the current dark orange, a light theme, a high-contrast one, and one or two alternative accents), saved per user so it follows them between devices — a `user_preferences` row or `user_metadata` (preferences, not permissions, so user-editable metadata is fine here), with `localStorage` only as a no-flash cache. Put the picker in the account menu beside Change password. The groundwork is there: every component reads CSS tokens from `src/app/globals.css`, so a theme is a `[data-theme="…"]` block redefining `--background`, `--surface`, `--primary` etc., set on `<html>` by the layout. **The catch is the environment colouring:** `:root[data-env="dev"]` recolours the same tokens teal so dev is never mistaken for production, and a user theme must not erase that signal — decide whether the env marker moves to something themes don't touch (a header strip or badge, as the `uat` item plans) or each theme gets a dev variant. Check contrast (WCAG AA) for every theme, and the generated PDFs stay unthemed.
- [ ] **How carers share LINE and Messenger.** The `/contacts` list shows LINE IDs but LINE's `ti/p/~` links only resolve IDs the person has made searchable; confirm with staff how carers usually share LINE (ID vs. QR / `line.me` link) and store whichever works. Same question for Messenger (username vs. a shared `m.me` link) — both are accepted as-is when pasted. Was (b) of the Contacts follow-ups item; (a) notes and (c) the maintenance link closed 2026-09-21.
- [x] **Settings → Website: break the page into tabs so it is manageable.** Done 2026-09-30 (see `docs/decisions/2026-09-30-website-settings-tabs.md`): five tabs plus a Pages accordion. Lutan, 2026-09-27. `src/app/admin/website/page.tsx` stacks everything in one long column: hero photo, featured resident, site settings and contact details (`SiteSettingsForm`), the vet-visit estimate, one full editor per information page (`SitePageForm` × 5, each with English and Thai), the gallery and published projects. Finding one thing means scrolling past all the rest, and it will get longer: the International adoption page (which replaced Pet relocation, 2026-09-27) and Become a Shelter Friend each add another editor, and the Messenger/WhatsApp/X contacts add fields. **Suggested layout:** tabs across the top for the big groups, e.g. **Home page** (hero photo, featured resident, pet of the week), **Contact & settings** (site settings, contact channels, vet-visit estimate), **Pages** (the information pages), **Gallery**, **Projects**. Inside **Pages**, an accordion with one row per page (title, public link, last edited, "Thai missing" if the translation is empty), opening one editor at a time, so a new page just adds a row. Accordion-only for the whole page was considered: fewer clicks to see everything, but with this many sections it still ends up long once two are open; tabs plus one accordion is the recommendation, but the builder can show Lutan both before settling. **Behaviour:** the chosen tab is in the URL (`?tab=pages`, and `#our-story` opens that page's row), so a reload, the back button and links from elsewhere (e.g. the manual, or a System status tile) land in the right place. Every section already saves on its own, so switching tabs must not lose typing: warn before leaving a tab or closing a row with unsaved changes, as the stocktake sheet does. Use the same tab pattern as `/stocktake` (`StocktakeSheet.tsx`, `role="tab"`, keyboard arrows) rather than a new one, and keep it usable on a phone (tabs scroll sideways or become a select). No schema change. Manual: the Website section in `src/lib/manual/en.ts` / `th.ts` (where things are now), both dictionaries for the tab names, and a `src/lib/releases.ts` line. Best not to run in the same batch as another stream that edits this page.

## Management

- [ ] **Management → Purchasing: how much of each medicine and food to buy, for a period you choose, with a safety stock.** Lutan, 2026-09-28. A Management page listing, per medication, a **recommended purchase quantity** so whoever does the ordering does not have to work it out. **The sum, per item, in its own unit:** *expected stock now* = last counted quantity (`stock_counts` / `medication.stock_on_hand`, 0093) − scheduled usage since that count (the prescription forecast, `doses_per_day` × `dose_quantity`) + receipts since that count (`stock_receipts`, 0096); *needed* = scheduled usage over the chosen period (from today) + **safety stock**; *recommended purchase* = needed − expected stock now, **never below 0**, rounded up to a whole pack if a pack size is known. **Period picker:** 1 week / 2 weeks (and perhaps 1 month / custom), reusing `src/lib/management/forecast-window.ts` as the forecasts already do. **Safety stock:** a new optional `medication.safety_stock` (numeric, the item's unit, null = none), set on the add/edit form in Management → Medications when an item is created or later, for items that are always in use whatever is prescribed today — IV/sub-Q fluids and the common antibiotics are Lutan's examples. It is a floor kept on the shelf, so it is added to *needed* on every period. Consider also adding the supplier lead time already stored (`reorder_lead_days`, 0083) to the period, so stock ordered today still covers the period once it arrives — say which in decisions.md. **Build on what exists, do not fork it:** `src/lib/management/stock.ts` already takes forecast usage off the last count to give days of stock; move the "expected stock now" part into one helper both pages call (and check whether it adds receipts since the count yet — if not, fix it there, so Medications' days-of-stock and this page agree). **Show the working** in each row (counted X on <date>, used ~Y since, received Z, need W for the period + S safety) so nobody has to trust a bare number, flag items **never counted** rather than guessing, and flag a count older than a few weeks as unreliable. A CSV / printable list of just the items to buy, grouped by supplier (the receipts' `supplier_contact_id` gives the usual supplier), is the thing someone takes to the vet wholesaler. **Cover food too (Lutan, 2026-09-28):** `diet_types` carry the same stock columns, counts and receipts, so the page covers every diet as well as every medicine — a Medicines / Food switch or two sections. **Special diets matter most**, so list them before the standard diet (the standard-diet flag from the Facility diet item tells them apart) and make the safety stock available on `diet_types` too. Diet usage comes from the residents on each diet (the diet forecast the Diets page already shows), not prescriptions. **Schema first:** `safety_stock` on both `medication` and `diet_types` (and pack size, if wanted) as a small `-schema` PR. Home: Management (or Shelter Operations if the Settings/Management review moves stock there). Manual topic en/th; a releases line.
- [ ] **Stocktake in cupboard order.** Claude, 2026-09-26, from the stocktake sheet. `/stocktake` lists items by name, which the backlog item accepted "for now"; staff walking the shelves want the list in the order the cupboard is laid out. Needs the shelter to describe the layout (shelf / box names), then a `shelf` or `sort_order` on `medication` and `diet_types` (schema PR first), a way for management to set it on the Management tables, and the sheet sorting by it with name as the tie-break.
- [x] **Saving a Shelter Friend does not publish it, and nothing says so.** Found 2026-09-24 on production, and it cost a round of "the release did not deploy properly" before the cause was clear. Making a Shelter Friend is two steps — **Save** the details, then **Publish** — implemented as two separate actions (`updateFriend` and `setFriendPublished` in `src/app/management/shelter-friends/actions.ts`). After Save the profile is a draft: `published` stays `false`, `public_shelter_friends` correctly filters it out (the view is `where sf.published and c.archived_at is null`, 0076), and so the public `/friends` page shows its empty state, the nav entry stays hidden and the home-page thank-you strip does not appear. All of that is correct behaviour, and from the outside it is indistinguishable from the feature being broken or not deployed. The card gives no indication after saving that the profile is still a draft. Options, cheapest first: a line on the card after Save — "Saved as a draft. Publish to show this on the website." — or a visible Draft badge on an unpublished profile, or a prompt on Save offering to publish straight away. Worth doing before anyone other than Lutan uses the feature, because the failure mode is silent: nothing errors, nothing is missing, the page simply looks empty. Related: the public page's empty state ("We're just getting started on thanking the businesses that help us") is written for a visitor and reads as intentional, which is right for the public but gives staff no hint that their own unpublished draft is the reason they cannot see it.

- [ ] **Base the vet forecast on recent visit frequency, not a flat per-visit estimate.** Asked for by Lutan 2026-09-23 while testing PR #60: "can we set it to look at an average rolling number of visits recorded per week in the last quarter x the cost per vet visit, or number booked for that week if it's more." Today the vet line is a flat typical-visit estimate edited on `/admin/website` — it does not know how often the shelter actually goes. The proposed rule is, per week, `max(rolling average visits/week over the last quarter, visits actually booked that week) x cost per visit`, so a quiet week still carries the historical rate and a busy one is not understated. Decisions to make before building: (a) the page forecasts by **month**, so either the weekly figure is aggregated up or the rule is restated monthly; (b) whether "cost per vet visit" stays the flat estimate or becomes the mean of actual `vet_appointments.cost` over the same quarter, which would make the whole line self-maintaining; (c) what a booked visit with a **known** cost does — it should surely use its own cost rather than the estimate, which means the rule is really `max(estimated visits, booked visits)` for the *count* and actual cost where known; (d) the empty case, where a shelter with no history gets an average of zero and the line silently reads nothing rather than "unknown". Note this interacts with the defect PR #60 already fixed, where `coalesce(va.cost, estimate)` over a LEFT JOIN charged the estimate to months with no visits at all — any frequency-based rule needs the same care about distinguishing "no visits" from "no data". **Looked at 2026-09-24 and left as the flat figure on Lutan's call:** dev held 71 completed visits, none with a `cost`, and 20 in the last quarter (about 1.5 a week) — so the frequency half has data but the cost half does not, and the change needs a migration while 0073 was in flight. See `docs/decisions.md` (2026-09-24). The dev query and the proposed rule are recorded there, so this is ready to pick up once invoices are being recorded.


- [x] **Archive contacts instead of deleting them.** Done 2026-09-24: schema in 0075 (#86); Archive / Restore on `/management/contacts` and `/contacts/[id]`, Show archived toggles on both lists, search still finds archived contacts, archived carers left out of `loadCarerOptions` and refused by `rehomeResident`. Customer request 2026-09-22. A carer who no longer fosters or adopts, a volunteer who has moved on, a supplier the shelter stopped buying from — none of them can be removed today: `deleteContact` in `src/app/management/contacts/actions.ts` refuses when `placement_history.carer_id` references them (rightly, that history must survive), and deleting the rest loses notes and contact details someone may need again. Add `archived_at timestamptz` (+ `archived_by`, optional `archive_reason` text) to `contacts` — a new migration, nullable, no RLS change. **Behaviour:** an Archive action (with reason prompt) on the contact row in `/management/contacts` and on `/contacts/[id]`; archived contacts drop out of the default `/management/contacts` and `/contacts` lists and out of every picker (`loadCarerOptions` in `src/lib/contacts/carers.ts`, the vendor/donor lookups) but stay reachable through a "Show archived" toggle, shown muted with an Archived badge and the reason, and can be Restored. **Where they still appear:** a resident's housing history keeps naming an archived carer (with the badge), and the foster/adopt form refuses to place a new animal with one unless the contact is restored first — that is the point of archiving. Keep `deleteContact` for contacts with no history at all (a typo entry); everything else archives. Reuse the same pattern the deceased-resident and hidden-deceased-list items use for "default hidden, toggle to show", and add `archived` to the contact search so a name lookup still finds them with the badge rather than reporting no match.
- [ ] **Contacts: who should see a carer's phone and address?** *Waiting on the director* — deferred to them 2026-09-21; don't start until they've said which roles need which fields. Raised 2026-09-21 in the first staff walk-through: `/contacts` (list and hub, with phone, LINE, address and one-tap call / maps) is open to every signed-in role, and the RLS in 0001 gives staff read/write and volunteers/vets read on `contacts`. The concern is privacy — a volunteer shouldn't be able to look up a carer's home address. The counter-case, recorded in `src/app/contacts/page.tsx`, is that a volunteer doing a foster pick-up needs the carer's number. Options: (a) move the directory under Management (nav + `requireManagementUser()` on `/contacts` and `/contacts/[id]`) and leave the RLS alone so the carer pickers on intake / rehome keep working for staff — quick, but staff then have no way to phone a carer; (b) keep `/contacts` for staff and management, hide it from volunteers and vets, and drop the address from the list for everyone but management; (c) tighten RLS with a `contacts_directory` view that omits phone/address for non-management, which also protects the data from anyone poking the API. Decide with the user which roles need which fields, then do it in one pass with the manual and the README roles table.

- [ ] **Machine-drafted translations (phase 2 of the free-text strategy).** The plumbing from 2026-09-21 (`0056_translations.sql`, `/management/translations`, `TranslationPanel`) has a human writing every draft; this adds a worker that fills `translations.text` with `status = 'draft'` so the managers check rather than write. Constraints agreed with the user: zero ongoing cost, open-weight models only, no PC on site. Shape: a Cloudflare Worker on a cron trigger in the same account the app deploys to (Workers AI binding, so no API key; Llama 3.x / Qwen / M2M100 all cover Thai and the free daily allowance dwarfs shelter volume), polling `translations where status = 'pending'` through Supabase REST with the service key as a Wrangler secret, writing back `text`, `engine` and `status = 'draft'`; a translate() function with one implementation per engine so a local Ollama box can replace it if one appears. Prompts differ by tier ("translate this adoption bio naturally; keep breed names" vs "translate this clinical note literally; keep drug names, doses, units and dates as written"). Then add the internal tier to `translatable_fields` (weight / vet-visit / prescription / intake notes, cause of death, behaviour notes) and attach the queueing trigger to those tables — shown to staff as a labelled machine translation under the original with **no** review step (the Weight tab finding from 2026-09-20). Before building, run twenty real bios and twenty real vet notes through two candidate models and let the two bilingual managers pick. Backfill in batches so the managers get ~20 Tier A drafts a day, not three hundred on Monday.

- [x] **Fixed monthly outgoings in the cashflow forecast (salaries, rent and the like), as settings, not payroll.** Lutan, 2026-09-29, replacing the 2026-09-23 "Salaries: pay the people" item. **Payroll is out of scope for good:** no employee records, pay rates, pay periods, payslips or payment history, because per-person pay is the most sensitive data the app could hold, and the privacy risk grows again if the app ever serves several shelters. What the forecast does need is the shelter's regular fixed costs. So: a short list of **named fixed outgoings** managed by management/admin (e.g. "Salaries", "Rent", "Electricity", "Internet"): a label, a monthly amount in baht (`numeric(12,2)`, shown through `formatBaht`), an optional note, and an active flag. **Salaries is one total line** for the whole staff, never broken down by person. Nothing in it identifies anyone. **Where it lives:** a small table (schema PR first), readable and writable by admin and management only, with no anon or staff access (add it to `check-public-views.mjs`'s refusal checks). Not columns on `site_content`, whose `select using (true)` makes every column world-readable. Edited on a page under Management or Settings (whichever the Management/Settings split item settles; Management → Cashflow is the natural home). **Forecast:** `cashflow_forecast` gains one category per active outgoing, or one "Fixed outgoings" category with the lines as its breakdown. Either way it counts each active amount once per month in the window with `basis = 'priced'`, and the page's "no salaries" sentence comes off. Decide while building: a month that is only partly inside the window (pro-rata, or whole months only, matching how the page already groups by month), and whether an amount can change from a date (a start/end month per line is enough; no history table). Manual (Cashflow topic), both dictionaries, a releases line and a `docs/decisions.md` entry recording that payroll was deliberately dropped, and why. **Schema half done 2026-09-29 (0114, see `docs/decisions/2026-09-29-fixed-outgoings-not-payroll.md`):** `fixed_outgoings` (`label`, `monthly_amount numeric(12,2)`, `note`, `active`, plus optional `starts_on` / `ends_on` as the 1st of a month, `ends_on` inclusive, so a change of amount is one line ended and one started, no history table). Admin and management only via RLS, anon grants revoked, capped at 24 lines so it cannot hold one row per person; `check-public-views.mjs` refuses anon. **Feature half done 2026-09-29:** edit page at Management → Cashflow → Fixed outgoings, forecast category "Fixed outgoings" counted pro-rata by the day, manual, dictionaries and releases line (see `docs/decisions/2026-09-29-fixed-outgoings-in-the-forecast.md`).
- [ ] **Dashboard follow-ups.** The first cut is deliberately rough; refine with the user once they've seen it against real data. Candidates: (a) **Export / print** — a "copy as text" or print stylesheet so the month section can go straight into the monthly report or a LINE message. (b) **Year view** — the same sections for a calendar year, and year-over-year comparison. (c) **Blood work location** — the report distinguishes "MW" from "vet visit" blood work; the dashboard splits on whether a test is linked to a vet appointment. If "MW" means a specific clinic rather than in-house, add a location/clinic field to `blood_tests` (or link every test to a visit) and split on that instead. (d) **Vet costs** — depends on the cost column in the Vet hub follow-ups item; spend per month belongs here. (e) **Time zone** — month boundaries for timestamp columns (`placement_history`, `vet_appointments`) are computed in the server's time zone (UTC on Cloudflare); a placement recorded before 07:00 on the 1st Thai time lands in the previous month. Decide on a shelter time zone constant and use it here and in the vet hub. (f) **Immunizations** — doses given this month and the stock forecast (decisions.md, open item 6) are the obvious next cards. (g) **Public "our impact"** — `public_shelter_stats` could grow lifetime totals (adopted since founding, sterilisations from project data) once the figures are trusted.
- [x] **Move frequency options to Admin, apart from medication management.** Done 2026-09-24: `/admin/frequencies` (Settings tile, manual topic, admin-gated actions), `/management/medications` is medications only. RLS left as it is on Lutan's call: `management_rw_frequency` is unchanged and there is no migration. See `docs/decisions.md` (2026-09-24). Raised 2026-09-21: `/management/medications` currently carries two things — the medication list with its 7/30-day forecast (a manager's concern) and the "Frequency options" pick-list (label + schedule: times a day / every N days-weeks-months / as needed, 0044). The frequency list is system configuration, like immunization types and procedure types, and belongs under Admin. Split it out to `/admin/frequencies` (same shape as `/admin/immunization-types`; move `CreateFrequencyForm` / `FrequenciesTable` and the frequency actions, keep the shared `FrequencyScheduleFields` + `parseSchedule`), add it to the Admin nav, and leave `/management/medications` as medications only. RLS: `management_rw_frequency` (0043) would no longer be needed — decide whether to revoke it back to staff's read + insert twin, or leave management able to add one inline from the prescription form. The procedure-types page (`/admin/procedure-types`, done 2026-09-21) is the template — one page per list, same table shape.
- [ ] **Cashflow follow-ups.** Raised 2026-09-23 while building the forecast page, none of them blocking. (a) **Forecast against actuals** — once a few months have passed, show what a month was forecast to cost beside what it did, which is the only way to find out whether the flat vet estimate and the diet quantities are anywhere near right. (b) **A per-vet or per-reason vet average** to replace the single flat `site_content.vet_visit_estimate`, once `vet_appointments.cost` has enough invoice history to mean anything (see `docs/decisions.md`, 2026-09-23). (c) ~~**CSV export** for the monthly report~~ — done 2026-09-24: Download CSV beside the table, with a "not priced" count column per category so an exported 0 never hides a gap. (d) **The fixed windows have diverged:** Diets and Medications draw 7 and 30-day columns from `FIXED_FORECAST_DAYS` in `src/lib/management/forecast-window.ts`; Cashflow shows one window at a time and carries its own `CASHFLOW_FIXED_DAYS` of 30 and 90, because 7 days of outgoings is not a useful figure and changing the shared constant would have reshaped the other two pages mid-flight. Decide whether that is the permanent answer or whether the three pages should share one set. (e) **Immunization doses are counted once per resident per type per window** — `immunization_next_due` (0007) gives the next due date only, so a 90-day window does not see a second monthly dose. It understates rather than overstates, which is the safe direction, but a window longer than the shortest interval is wrong by construction and worth fixing if anyone forecasts a year.
