(f) **Salaries, rent and other fixed costs** are excluded only because nothing holds them; the "Fixed monthly outgoings" item above adds them as named monthly amounts (one total line for salaries, no payroll) and takes the "no salaries" sentence off the page when it lands.

- [x] **Recurring jobs: only offer people who can actually do the job.** Lutan, 2026-09-27, Pass 1 (Vet) of the role walkthrough. **Done 2026-09-27** (`claude/recurring-jobs-eligible-assignees`): vets are never assignable (Lutan: their work comes from vet appointments), and otherwise who can do a job is derived from its link path in `src/lib/recurring-jobs/eligibility.ts`, borrowing each page's own role predicate; the picker filters by it as the link changes, save and Hand over refuse anyone it excludes, and a job already with such a person is flagged on Management → Recurring jobs and loses its link on their My tasks. No migration. Pass 0 assigned a stocktake to a vet, because the assignee picker offers every user regardless of role. Pass 1 then tapped it and was thrown out of the app — the job's link goes to `/stocktake`, which vets cannot open. The job was impossible from the moment it was assigned, and nothing said so at the point where it could have been prevented. **Filter the assignee list by what the job's target screen needs**: a stocktake job offers only the roles `canStocktake` allows (admin, management, staff, volunteer); a maintenance job only those who can write maintenance; and so on. That means a recurring job has to know which capability it needs — today it knows a link, not a permission — so part of this is deciding how that is expressed (a capability on the job type, or derived from its target path). **Also handle the ones already assigned**, since a filter on the form does not fix a job created before it: either validate on save and on display, or show an assignee who can no longer do it as a problem on the Management page rather than leaving it to fail in someone's face. The bounce itself is the public-website item under Quick wins; this is the half that stops the situation arising.

## Assistant

- [x] **Assistant demo: show it to the Director and record what they said.** The sketch is built and merged (2026-09-22): `/assistant` in the left nav with a *demo* badge, admin/management/staff, keyword parser in `src/lib/assistant/demo-parser.ts`, writes via the real move helper and vet-visit RPC after a preview card — see `docs/decisions.md` (2026-09-22, "hard-codes the understanding, not the doing") for what was deliberately left out of the sketch (slide-over, `dryRun`, env flag, audit table). **What remains:** walk the Director through "move Panda to B1" and "book a vet visit for Panda tomorrow 10am", then record what they said in `docs/decisions.md` and let that steer the full assistant item below. Original brief, kept for the real build — raised 2026-09-22: No LLM, no API key: a chat panel whose "brain" is a small keyword matcher that recognises two requests and walks each through the real flow — (1) "move <resident> to <enclosure>" and (2) "book a vet visit for <resident>" — everything else gets a polite "I can only help with moves and vet visits in this demo." **Build the reusable parts properly, hard-code only the brain:** `src/components/assistant/AssistantPanel.tsx` (slide-over from a header button, message list, input, typing indicator, works on the phone), `src/lib/assistant/types.ts` (the message / tool-call / request-details / confirm-card shapes the real assistant will use), `PreviewCard` ("Move Panda (R-0042) from A3 → B1 today" with Confirm / Change / Cancel) and `RequestDetailsModal` (a small form built from a field spec — used when the resident name is ambiguous or the enclosure / vet / date is missing). The scripted brain lives in `src/lib/assistant/demo-brain.ts` behind the same `respond(messages) → AssistantTurn` interface the LLM adapter will implement later, so swapping it out is one import. **It should do real work:** resident and enclosure lookups hit `resident_list_view` / enclosures for real (so the Director can try it on Panda), the move preview calls `src/lib/placements/move.ts` with `dryRun` and, on Confirm, for real; the vet visit preview pre-fills date / vet / reason and on Confirm inserts the `vet_appointments` row the same way `/vet-visits/new` does. Both paths reuse the existing capacity warning and the existing confirm wording, and the resulting rows carry a "via assistant (demo)" note. **Guard rails:** behind a `NEXT_PUBLIC_ASSISTANT_DEMO=1` env flag so it is on for the customer showing and off otherwise; admin/management/staff only; English and Thai trigger phrases (a handful of each — the matcher is a list of regexes, not NLP). **Not in scope:** anything the two scripts don't cover, memory between messages, streaming. Afterwards, record what the customer said in `docs/decisions.md` and let that steer the full item.
- [ ] **Assistant v1: grow the keyword assistant into the production tool until an LLM takes over.** Customer direction 2026-09-22: the Director loved the demo, so the scripted assistant becomes version 1 in production, and the LLM item below becomes version 2 — same panel, same preview cards, a smarter brain later. First, close the demo item above: write the Director's reaction into `docs/decisions.md` and tick it. **Promote, don't rewrite:** keep `src/lib/assistant/demo-parser.ts`'s shape (a request → a `Draft` with nulls for whatever the text didn't say → an editable preview card → Confirm runs the app's own helper) but split it into one small parser per intent behind a `parse(text, context) → Draft | null` list tried in order, so each new function is a file, not a bigger regex. Drop the *demo* badge from `NavLinks.tsx` and `page.tsx` once at least the first batch below is in. **Functions to add, in this order** (each reuses an existing server action or RPC under the caller's session — never a new write path — and stamps notes with `via the assistant — "<request>"` as the demo does): (1) **hospital**: "send Panda to the vet hospital" / "Panda is back from hospital" → `sendToHospital` and the return path in `src/app/residents/[id]/hospital/actions.ts`; (2) **weight**: "Panda weighs 12.4 kg" / "log weight 12.4 for Panda" → the action in `src/app/weight/new/actions.ts`; (3) **death**: "Bella died last night, lung problems" → `recordDeath` in `residents/[id]/deceased/actions.ts` with the `ConfirmDialog` wording the deceased page uses, date left blank when "last night" straddles midnight; (4) **foster / adopt / return**: "Panda goes to foster with Khun Noi" / "Panda has been adopted by …" / "Panda came back from foster" → `rehome` in `residents/[id]/rehome/actions.ts`, carer matched against the same option list the form uses (`loadCarerOptions`); (5) **immunization**: "Panda had rabies today" → the `/immunizations/new` path; (6) **read-only lookups** that need no confirm: "where is Panda", "who is in B1", "what is due this week" — answered inline from `resident_list_view`, the enclosure page's query and the due-soon queries the dashboard already runs. **Thai** trigger phrases for every intent alongside English, as the demo has for move and vet. **Ambiguity:** when a name matches more than one resident the card offers the candidates with code, enclosure and photo — the demo's "pick one" count becomes a real picker. **Where it lives:** keep `/assistant` as the full page and add the slide-over `AssistantPanel` from a header button so it can be used from any screen, including the phone; the message list and input components should be the ones the LLM version will keep. **Roles:** admin / management / staff can write; volunteers get the lookups only. **Audit:** now that it is production, add the `assistant_actions` table from the LLM item (who, when, request text, intent, draft, result) in its own schema PR first — it is the eval corpus the LLM version will be measured against, so start collecting from day one. **Manual:** a new topic in `src/lib/manual/en.ts` / `th.ts` listing exactly what the assistant understands, phrased as the sentences to type — for a keyword assistant the manual *is* the grammar. Ship in batches (hospital + weight + lookups first, then death + rehome, then immunization) so each is a small PR and the Director sees progress; each batch is one workstream. Not in scope: any model, memory between messages, streaming — that is the item below, which should now be read as version 2 and reuse everything built here. **Progress: batch 1 shipped 2026-09-23** (hospital send + return, weight, and the read-only lookups — plus the slide-over, the audit writes, the manual topic and the demo-badge removal). `demo-parser.ts` is now one parser per intent under `src/lib/assistant/intents/`, tried in the order `parse.ts` lists, so the next batch is a new file there plus a card and an action. **Next: batch 2, death + rehome**; then batch 3, immunization.
- [ ] **Doctor name through the assistant's vet booking.** Raised 2026-09-24 when the doctor-name feature shipped: `/vet-visits/new` and Edit take an optional doctor (`vet_appointments.doctor_name`, 0074), but the assistant's vet intent (`src/lib/assistant/intents/vet.ts`) strips "dr", "dr." and "doctor" as `VET_STOPWORDS` when matching the vet, so "book a vet visit for Panda with Dr Somchai" books the visit and drops the name. Accept it, but don't ask for it: parse a trailing "with Dr X" / "หมอ X" into the draft, show it on the preview card as an editable, optional field, and write it the way `bookVetVisit` does (see `docs/decisions.md`, 2026-09-24). If a schema PR touches vet visits first, fold a `p_doctor_name` parameter into `schedule_bulk_appointments` then, so both callers write the doctor in the RPC's own transaction.
- [ ] **A volunteer turned away from an assistant write leaves no audit row.** Noticed building batch 1 (2026-09-23). Volunteers get the read-only lookups; if one types a write request the browser answers "that one is for staff and management" and nothing reaches the server, so `assistant_actions` (0070) never sees it. Those are worth having — they say what the people with the least access actually want the assistant to do, which is exactly what should steer whether volunteers get more of it. The status enum is created in `0070_assistant_actions.sql` as `('confirmed','cancelled','unmatched')` and none of them is true of a refusal, so a `refused` value has to be added before the client can record it. **Two constraints, both confirmed 2026-09-23 — read them before planning this:** (1) it is **two migration files, not one**, per `docs/decisions.md` (PR #50, "An enum value and the code that uses it need two migration files"): `alter type … add value` cannot share a transaction with anything that uses the new value, and `apply-migrations.mjs` wraps each file in its own `begin…commit` — so the `alter type` goes alone, then whatever references it, and `--dry-run` of the second file fails until the first is really committed (move the second aside, apply the first, restore it, dry-run, apply). (2) CLAUDE.md allows only **one in-flight branch carrying a migration**, and `claude/cashflow-schema` holds that slot with 0071 — so this either waits for that to merge or folds into the same schema PR. It cannot be a parallel schema stream. Small, and not urgent — but the longer it waits the bigger the hole in the corpus.
- [x] **`fetchAssistantContext()` has no role check of its own.** Noticed 2026-09-25 building `assistant-nav-link`. The server action in `src/app/assistant/actions.ts` calls `loadAssistantContext()` for whoever asks, so a vet — who has no Assistant button and whose `/assistant` page shows only "can't use the assistant" — can still call it directly and get the resident list, vets and enclosure options, limited only by RLS. Writes and lookups each re-check the role; this one does not. Return an empty context with `notAuthorized` unless `canUseAssistant(role)`, the way `lookups.ts` does, and check whether `page.tsx` should skip loading for the same roles.
- [ ] **In-app chat assistant that does things, not just answers.** Raised 2026-09-22: the Director's biggest pain point is time, and the ask is an experience like working with Claude here — type "move Panda from A3 to B1" or "Bella died last night, lung problems" and the system makes the changes, popping a modal only when it needs something it can't infer. **Hosting (user's direction 2026-09-22):** the production assistant should run on an LLM the shelter hosts itself — on the Raspberry Pi 5 at home or a cloud box — not on a metered Claude subscription. Claude is for the prototype and for teaching: build against the Claude API first because it gets tool use right with the least effort, and while doing so record every real exchange (request, tool calls, corrections) into an eval set that the self-hosted model is then measured against and, if needed, fine-tuned on. To keep that swap possible the model layer must be **provider-neutral from the first commit**: one `src/lib/assistant/llm.ts` adapter that takes messages + tool definitions and returns text / tool calls, with a Claude implementation (`@anthropic-ai/sdk`, `claude-opus-5`, streaming, adaptive thinking; `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` as a Cloudflare secret) and an OpenAI-compatible implementation for whatever the shelter hosts (Ollama / llama.cpp / vLLM all speak that shape), selected by env var. Nothing outside the adapter may import a vendor SDK. **Honest sizing note for the Pi:** a Pi 5 with 8–16 GB runs a 3–8B quantised model at a few tokens/second; that is workable for a short reply but tool-calling accuracy in mixed Thai/English at that size is marginal and each turn will take tens of seconds. Expect the self-hosted target to be a small cloud GPU (or a hosted open-weight endpoint) and treat the Pi as the stretch goal — measure both against the eval set before deciding. **Shape:** a chat panel (slide-over from a header button, also a full page `/assistant`; works on the phone) that sends the conversation to a route handler which calls the adapter with a **fixed toolset that wraps the app's existing server-side operations** — never raw SQL. First tools: find resident (by name/Thai name/code, returning candidates), find enclosure, move resident (`src/lib/placements/move.ts`), send to / return from hospital (`hospital.ts`), record death (`deceased.ts`, with cause), foster/adopt/return (`rehome.ts`), log weight, log vet visit, log immunization, add prescription, and read-only lookups (resident hub summary, who is in an enclosure, what's due). Each tool runs under the caller's own Supabase session, so RLS and the role checks apply exactly as in the UI and the assistant can never do more than the person could by clicking. **Confirmation:** every write is a two-step — the model calls the tool with `dryRun: true`, the UI shows a preview card ("Move Panda (R-0042) from A3 → B1 today, no notes") with Confirm / Change / Cancel, and only a Confirm re-invokes the tool for real; irreversible ones (death) use the existing `ConfirmDialog` wording. This matters doubly for a smaller self-hosted model: the confirm step is what makes a mis-parsed request harmless. **Missing information:** a `request_details` tool the model calls when a required field is unknown (which of two residents named Panda; date of death when "last night" is ambiguous across midnight; the enclosure when only a zone was named) that renders a small modal form built from the tool's field spec; the answers go back as the tool result. **Ambiguity:** resident lookup always returns candidates with code, enclosure and photo so the model can ask "the Panda in A3 or the one in Foster?" instead of guessing. **Audit:** log each assistant conversation and every executed tool call (who, when, input, result, which model served it) to a new `assistant_actions` table, and stamp the resulting rows' notes with "via assistant" so the hub history shows it — this table doubles as the eval/training corpus. **Scope of first cut:** the placement and medical tools above, English + Thai input, admin/management/staff only (volunteers get read-only tools), no memory across conversations, Claude behind the adapter. **Second cut:** stand up the self-hosted model, run the eval set, switch the env var when it clears the bar. **Later:** voice input on the phone, "what happened this week" summaries from the management dashboard queries, drafting the Facebook post for a project. **Decide with the user before building:** whether low-risk logs (weight, immunization) may skip the confirm to keep it fast; the accuracy bar the self-hosted model must reach on the eval set before it replaces Claude; whether conversations are retained beyond the audit rows.

## Auth

- [x] **A vet can still write another clinic's records, and record a visit against any clinic, through the database.** Found 2026-09-28 building "A vet should see only the residents their own clinic treats" (0108). That item decided which residents a vet *sees*, and shows a visible resident's whole history, other clinics' visits included (docs/decisions.md, 2026-09-28). Writes were left as they were: a vet's JWT can still edit or delete another clinic's visit, prescription, procedure or blood test on a resident they can see, and can insert a visit with any `vet_id` — `src/lib/vets/scope.ts` holds "own clinic only" in the forms, not in RLS. Decide whether another clinic's rows are read-only to a vet (for `vet_appointments` it is a `vet_id = current_user_vet_id()` condition on update/delete; for prescriptions, procedures and blood tests it goes through their visit), and move the booking rule into `with check`, keeping the forms' "keep the clinic a visit already has" case working. Schema PR. **Done 2026-09-29** (`claude/vet-cross-clinic-writes`, 0110, see `docs/decisions.md`): another clinic's rows are read-only to a vet, and a visit can only be booked at the vet's own clinic.
- [ ] **Should a vet read the shelter's address book at all?** Found 2026-09-28 building 0108, which left `vet_read_contacts` (and enclosures, zones, shelter_friends) as they were: the resident hub shows a carer's name from `contacts`, so dropping the policy breaks the hub for vets. But the Data API with a vet's JWT still returns every contact — adopters, donors, carers, with phone and email — though #182 took Contacts out of a vet's menu. Options: a view or definer function that gives the hub the carer name only, then drop the vet policy; or scope contacts to carers of residents the vet can see (`current_vet_resident_ids()`). Ask Lutan whether a vet ever needs an adopter's or carer's details before choosing.
- [x] **A visible "Request access" on the sign-in page — today nobody can tell how to ask.** Lutan, 2026-09-28: "I'm not sure how to request access right now." **How it works today, invisibly:** pressing **Continue with Google** on `/login` with an account that has no role creates the `auth.users` row, which *is* the access request on Settings → Security (`src/app/auth/callback/route.ts` refuses the session and redirects to `/login?error=no_role`). But nothing says so: the login page only offers sign-in, and the refusal reads "This account doesn't have access. Ask an administrator to assign you a role" — which sounds like a dead end, when the request has in fact just been made. **Build:** (1) a **Request access** link or button on `/login` (below the sign-in options) leading to a short explainer or step: "Continue with Google using the account you want to use; an administrator will be told and will approve you" — the button can simply run the same Google flow with a flag (e.g. `?intent=request`), so no new mechanism. Optionally ask for a name and a short note ("I'm the new volunteer vet") stored in `user_metadata`, shown beside the request on Settings → Security so the admin knows who it is. (2) **Reword the `no_role` message** to confirm what happened: "Your request has been sent. An administrator will approve it — try signing in again later." (en and th, `login.errors.noRole`). (3) Say what to do if they have no Google account: email login cannot self-register (admins create those users), so the explainer says to contact the shelter, with the shelter's email. (4) Tie-in: the System status card for waiting access requests (Admin section) and a possible email to admins on a new request — this is the other end of the same flow. Check the callback does not let a request intent do anything but create the unapproved row (no role, no session kept). Manual: the sign-in topic, en/th; a releases line.
- [x] **Settings → Security requires 2-step verification.** Done 2026-09-27 (see `docs/decisions.md`): `/admin/security/verify` sets up an authenticator app and asks for the code once per sign-in; all eight Security actions check `aal2` themselves; Reset in the new 2-step column and `bootstrap-admin.mjs --reset-2step` are the recovery routes; no recovery codes. Lutan, 2026-09-26, replacing an earlier "admins and managers must sign in with Google" draft. **Scope, deliberately narrow:** only `/admin/security` and the actions behind it (create a login, give or change a role, approve or deny an access request, issue a temporary password, archive or restore a user) require a second step, because that page decides who can get in at all, which is the system's weakest point. Everything else stays as it is: the rest of the app changes shelter data, which can be corrected or reversed, so it keeps today's sign-in (password or Google). **Why 2-step and not "Google only":** the admins use personal Gmail accounts, so nothing enforces or reveals whether Google's own 2-step verification is on, and Google-only adds a lock-out if that sign-in ever breaks. Supabase Auth's built-in authenticator-app (TOTP) factor is free on the current plan, and a session that has passed it carries `aal2` in its JWT, so the rule can be checked in code and in the database. **Build:** (1) **Step-up, not a new login:** opening Security with an `aal1` session (`supabase.auth.mfa.getAuthenticatorAssuranceLevel()`) goes to a small page. The first time, it enrols an authenticator app (QR code + a manual key, verify one code, then show a few one-time recovery codes if Supabase supports them, otherwise explain the recovery route below). After that it asks for the 6-digit code and returns to Security. The same step-up works whether the admin signed in with a password or with Google. (2) **Enforce it where the power is, not just on the page:** every server action in `src/app/admin/security/` checks `aal2` itself before doing anything, since the service-role calls there bypass RLS. Add an RLS check (`auth.jwt()->>'aal' = 'aal2'`) on writes to `user_roles` too, so no path can change a role without it; that part is a small schema PR first. (3) **Settings landing page:** the Security tile says it needs your authenticator app. **Recovery:** a lost phone must not lock the shelter out. Another admin who has passed 2-step can reset a colleague's factor from Security, and `scripts/bootstrap-admin.mjs` (service role, run by Lutan) can remove a factor as the last resort. Write both down in README and `docs/decisions.md`. **Test on dev first:** confirm the `aal` claim after TOTP verification, what a Google session reports, how long `aal2` lasts in a session, and that a step-up survives the proxy's refresh. Manual (Security topic: set up the app, what to do if the phone is lost), both dictionaries, a releases line, a test plan covering each refused path, and the security review item should note it. **Not in scope:** requiring 2-step for management pages or for sign-in generally. Revisit if salaries (Management) land, since those figures cannot be "undone" once seen.

- [x] **Cut the vet's world down to residents and their own tasks.** Lutan, 2026-09-27, Pass 1 (Vet) of the role walkthrough. The vet menu today is the same as everyone's bar Stocktake: My tasks, Residents, Enclosures, Maintenance, Vets, Contacts, Projects. Lutan's call is that only **Residents** and **My tasks** belong there. Enclosures and Maintenance are shelter operations; Projects is the shelter's own programme of work; **Contacts** is the shelter's address book; and letting one clinic browse the list of **other clinics** the shelter uses is the one that is actively inappropriate rather than merely irrelevant. A vet is an external party with a clinical job, and the app should read that way. **Two halves, and the second is the one that matters.** The menu is `src/app/NavLinks.tsx`, where these five entries are ungated — easy. The routes are `/enclosures`, `/maintenance`, `/vets`, `/contacts`, `/projects` and their detail pages, each of which needs a guard, because hiding a link leaves the URL open and Pass 1 confirmed a vet reaches all five by typing them. Note `/maintenance` is already *effectively* shut — a vet has no maintenance RLS policy (`0001`, `0039`), so the board returns empty rather than forbidden — which is exactly the misleading state the walkthrough flagged, and the guard fixes the reading as well as the access. **Refuse properly, not with `redirect("/")`** — see the public-website bounce item. Manual and the roles topic need the same edit, and `roleSummary.vet` in `src/lib/manual/en.ts` / `th.ts` currently promises "Can read resident details", which stays true.
- [x] **A vet should see only the residents their own clinic treats.** **Done 2026-09-28** (`0108_vet_resident_scope.sql`; see `docs/decisions.md`): held in RLS through `current_vet_resident_ids()` — every vet policy on a resident's rows, the five owner-rights views and `record_attachment` — so a hand-typed `/residents/<id>` is a 404 and the Data API with a vet's JWT returns nothing outside the clinic. A cancelled visit counts; other clinics' rows on a visible resident are shown; a vet with no clinic sees no residents and `/residents` says why. Proved by `scripts/check-vet-resident-scope.mjs`. Closes Pass 1. Lutan, 2026-09-27, Pass 1 (Vet) of the role walkthrough. A vet signed in today sees the whole `/residents` list. Lutan expected it scoped: a doctor at Novel sees the residents Novel has records for — a vet appointment, and probably a procedure, prescription or blood test booked through them — and nobody else's. This is the largest item from Pass 1 and the one to think hardest about, because it is **data scoping, not menu gating**: it has to hold in RLS, not just in a page query, or the first `/residents/<id>` typed by hand walks straight through it. **What has to be decided first:** a vet account today has no link to a `vets` row at all — the role says "vet", not "which clinic" — so this needs that relationship to exist before any policy can reference it, which is a schema PR of its own and overlaps the doctor/clinic hierarchy item. Then: which records count as "treating" (appointments only, or prescriptions and procedures too); whether the scope is the clinic or the individual doctor; what a vet sees on a resident they treat but whose history includes another clinic's visits; and what happens to a resident whose only appointment is cancelled. Also whether this is even wanted before go-live — the shelter currently uses few clinics, and a scope that hides a resident from the vet about to treat them is worse than one that shows too much. **Decide the rule with Lutan before building.** **RULE DECIDED (Lutan, 2026-09-28): any clinical record, clinic-level.** A vet sees every resident their own clinic holds any clinical record for — a vet appointment, a prescription, a procedure or a blood test — and nobody else. Not appointments only: the broadest safe reading was chosen deliberately, because this item's own warning is that hiding a resident from the vet about to treat them is worse than showing too much. Clinic-level, not per-doctor, so a colleague covering a shift sees the same list. `user_roles.vet_id` and `current_user_vet_id()` (0102) are what the policy references. Still open for whoever builds it: what a vet sees on a resident they treat whose history includes another clinic's visits (the resident is visible either way — the question is the other clinic's rows), and what happens to a resident whose only appointment is cancelled. Both are narrower calls that can be made and recorded in `docs/decisions.md` rather than waiting on Lutan again.
- [x] **A vet can still edit the shelter's list of clinics through the database.** Found 2026-09-27 building "Cut the vet's world down to residents and their own tasks". That PR refuses a vet on `/vets`, `/contacts`, `/enclosures`, `/maintenance` and `/projects`, but the pages are only the UI: `vet_rw_vets` (`0001`) still gives a vet **full read/write on the `vets` table**, so a vet calling the Supabase API with their own session could rename or delete another clinic. Checked in dev's `pg_policies`. Reads are also still open — `vet_read_contacts`, `vet_read_enclosures`, `vet_read_zones` — so the shelter's address book is one API call away for a vet even though no page shows it. **The write is the one to fix**: drop `vet_rw_vets` for a read-only policy (the vet visit form needs to read clinic names), in a schema PR. Whether to narrow the reads belongs with the resident-level scope item above, which is where a vet's database access is being decided — `enclosures`/`zones` reads may be needed by resident pages a vet uses (current placement), so check before removing them. **Done 2026-09-27, `0105`**: `vet_rw_vets` replaced by `vet_read_vets` (select only), proved with a vet's own JWT by `scripts/check-vets-readonly.mjs`. The three reads were checked and left open: removing any would blank enclosure, zone and carer names on `/residents`, the resident hub, its housing section and adoption updates (`resident_list_view` is `security_invoker` and joins `enclosures`/`zones` directly) — see decisions.md, 2026-09-27, for what the resident-level scope item would have to move first.

- [x] **A vet's work comes from their appointments: My tasks and the menu built around vet visits.** **Done 2026-09-29** (`claude/vet-appointments-tasks`; see `docs/decisions/2026-09-29-vet-appointments-page.md`): built as a new `/appointments` page rather than inside My tasks, per Lutan; the rest of the note below is the original draft. Lutan, 2026-09-27, reviewing `claude/recurring-jobs-eligible-assignees`: "vets should only be assigned tasks to perform actions related to a vet appointment, for example add a vaccination record — so even recurring tasks could come off navigation for vets, and they can just manage their appointments and the residents associated with the appointments." That PR already stops vets being *given* recurring jobs; this is the rest. **Draft user story, for Lutan to correct before it is built:** *As a visiting vet, I want to open the app and see my appointments at this shelter — upcoming and ones I still have to write up — so that I can get to the animal I am seeing and record what I did (vaccination, procedure, prescription, weight, notes) without finding my way through the shelter's own screens.* Acceptance, to be confirmed: (1) a vet's landing page is their appointments, not My tasks' recurring and maintenance sections, which a vet never has; (2) each appointment opens its resident, and the records a vet adds from there are pre-linked to that appointment; (3) the vet's menu is Appointments and Residents only — Recurring jobs never appears, and My tasks either goes or shows only appointment follow-ups; (4) a vet reaches only the residents on their appointments (this is the resident-level scope item above, order 4 — decide the rule there, not here); (5) nothing a vet has open is lost when the appointment is marked done. **Open questions for Lutan:** is an "appointment" the existing vet visit record, or something booked ahead that a visit is recorded against? Does a vet see the clinic's appointments or only their own (0102 links a vet account to a clinic and a doctor)? Depends on `vet-scope-navigation` (menu and route guards) landing first, and overlaps the batch 2 vet visit form item — sequence with both.

## Architecture

- [ ] **Promote the `test-plan` check from a red flag to a required check.** Requested 2026-09-23 by Lutan, alongside enlisting a test manager: every feature gets a test plan checklist, filled in during verification and signed off before the feature reaches production. The mechanism is built — `docs/test-plan-template.md`, copied per feature to `docs/test-plans/<feature>.md`, plus `scripts/check-test-plan.mjs` and a `test-plan` CI job that fails a PR which does not add or update a *completed* checklist (every line ticked or `n/a: <reason>`, no placeholders left, `Result:` one of three values, `fail` blocks). That is PR #55 — open, CI green, carrying its own completed checklist as the first one through the gate. **The part deliberately not enabled yet:** `test-plan` is not a required check in branch protection on `main`, so it reports red and the PR merges anyway. That is a GitHub settings change on the repo (Settings → Branches → `main` → require `CI` *and* `test-plan`). **No exemptions, including schema-only PRs** — ruled 2026-09-23: an exemption was granted and then reversed within minutes, on the grounds that a checklist which is mostly `n/a: no UI surface, no code reads these columns yet` costs about a minute and is exactly the record you want when that migration later turns out to matter. If it is ever revisited, derive the exemption from the diff (the PR touches nothing outside `supabase/migrations/`) rather than from a label: a label is a manual click that can land on a PR which is not schema-only, so it has to be verified against the file list anyway, at which point the file list is doing the work and the label only adds a way to get it wrong. Note also that the gate sits before merge, not before deploy — `main` moves several times a day (four merges on 2026-09-23 alone), so untested work accumulating on `main` would make every release all-or-nothing. Keep a thin release-time smoke list as well (sign in, residents list, one resident hub, `/adopt` signed out), because a per-feature gate cannot catch two features interacting badly. Everything merged up to `f32f2c1` (#51–#54) is the agreed baseline and is exempt; testing starts from the next feature. **Staged deliberately, 2026-09-23:** Lutan's call is to run `test-plan` as a red flag first and promote it to a required check once the process has visibly bedded in — "see how we go, and if it is working smoothly then we can change to a hard block". So red-without-blocking is a planned first stage, not an oversight, and this item is the scheduled revisit rather than a defect. The reasoning (a soft gate people follow beats a hard gate they resent, and nothing is live until the Pi lands) is recorded in `docs/decisions.md` on PR #55.

- [x] **`check-test-plan.mjs` can't tell a post-merge sign-off PR from a feature PR that forgot its release line.** Raised 2026-09-24 on PR #80. #77 (intake capacity warning) merged with its plan complete apart from a real-phone check; the phone pass was then recorded in a follow-up PR from the same branch that touched only `docs/test-plans/intake-capacity-warning.md`. The checker reads the plan's **Release notes.** line against *that PR's* diff, and a ticked line with no new `unreleased` entry fails ("gained no line in this PR"), even though #77 had added it. The workaround was to reword the line as `n/a: in this follow-up PR … went into unreleased with #77` — honest, but it rewrites the feature's permanent record to suit a later PR, and every sign-off recorded after merge will hit the same thing. This will be common: section 8's deployed smoke test can only happen after merge, so a manual check moved there (as the phone pass was) is always recorded post-merge. Fix options: (a) when a PR changes only files under `docs/test-plans/`, check the release-notes line against the diff that introduced the plan (`git log --diff-filter=A` on the file, or the first PR that touched it) rather than the current diff; (b) accept a ticked line when `unreleased` — or a cut release — already contains a line the plan quotes; (c) an explicit marker such as `release line: #77` that the checker verifies by looking at that PR's diff. (a) needs no new syntax and is probably the right default. Afterwards, restore #80's line to its ticked wording. Soft gate, so nothing is blocked meanwhile — but a false red on every sign-off PR is exactly what teaches people to ignore a red `test-plan`.

- [ ] **Top-to-bottom architectural review by Fable.** Requested 2026-09-22. Open a session on the Fable model (`claude-fable-5-1`) and ask for a read-only review of the whole system, not a feature: the Next.js app (`src/app` routes, `src/lib` services, `src/components`, `src/proxy.ts` auth/redirect handling), the Supabase schema, RLS policies and the migration history in `supabase/migrations/`, the Cloudflare Worker / OpenNext deployment and the environment split (`test.lannacare.org` vs `lannacare.org`, `scripts/deploy.mjs`), the operational scripts (`scripts/apply-migrations.mjs`, `scripts/backup.mjs`, `scripts/pi/`), the Google Drive integration, the public website vs the staff app, and the docs (`README.md`, `docs/decisions.md`, `docs/pi-hosting.md`, `docs/manual`). Ask for: (a) a plain description of the architecture as it actually is, to check against the README; (b) ranked findings — security and data-loss risks first, then coupling / duplication / anything that will hurt when the shelter grows or a second developer arrives, then nits — each with the file(s) and a concrete suggested change; (c) what is fine and should be left alone. Write the result to `docs/architecture-review-2026-09-22.md` (dated, so later reviews sit beside it) and turn each finding worth acting on into its own backlog item under the matching heading. No code changes in that session — the review is the deliverable.


- [ ] **Full security review and cyber threat assessment of the platform.** Requested 2026-09-24 by Lutan. The deliverable is an **assessment report**: `docs/security-review-<date>.md`, dated so later reviews sit beside it. It maps every threat surface, says who could attack each one and how, rates each risk, and proposes mitigations. This is a separate item from the Fable architectural review above. That review ranks security first but covers the whole architecture. This one is only about security, and it asks "who would attack this, and what would they get?" not "is this well built?". Whichever runs second reads the other's report and does not repeat its findings. **Threat surfaces to cover, at minimum:** (a) **the public website and anonymous access**: `/adopt`, `/foster`, `/volunteer`, `/donate`, `/our-work`, `/r`, `/e`, `/privacy`; the anon key that ships in the client bundle by design; every table and view anon can reach through PostgREST, found by listing grants and `pg_policies`, not from `scripts/check-public-views.mjs`'s four-view allowlist (note `site_content`'s unqualified `using (true)`: every column of it is world-readable); and spam and abuse on any public form; (b) **authentication and sessions**: Google OAuth (`src/app/auth/callback`), email/password with temporary passwords and `must_change_password` in `app_metadata`, the access-request queue (a stranger's Google sign-in leaves an auth row), archived users, the `src/proxy.ts` guards, `bootstrap-admin.mjs`, whether the pages under `/admin` and `/management` each check the role or rely on the nav hiding them, and MFA for admins (since 2026-09-27 Settings → Security and its actions require `aal2` — an authenticator-app step-up; review the step-up page `/admin/security/verify`, the per-action check in `src/app/admin/security/actions.ts`, the reset routes including `bootstrap-admin.mjs --reset-2step`, that a reset reaches an open session only at its next refresh, and that nothing else asks for 2-step; `docs/decisions.md` 2026-09-27); (c) **authorization and RLS**: role escalation (can a volunteer write `user_roles`?), `security definer` functions and triggers (0024, 0026 and later), write paths for each role, and service-role use in server actions; (d) **file handling**: the attachment and photo routes under `src/app/api/*` (upload type and size limits, who may read a `photos/[fileId]`, whether a Drive file ID can be guessed or enumerated) and the Google Drive service credentials, including what those credentials can reach beyond the shelter's folders; (e) **the assistant** (`/assistant`, `assistant_actions` 0070): prompt injection through resident notes, contact records or uploaded documents; what the assistant can read and write as the signed-in user; and whether its actions need confirmation; (f) **infrastructure and deployment**: the Cloudflare Worker (`worker/index.mjs`, including the Pi-origin fallback in `fetchFromOrigin`), `wrangler.jsonc`, the secrets in Workers and `.dev.vars`/`.env.local`, the dev/prod split (`test.lannacare.org` vs `lannacare.org`) and whether dev can ever reach prod data, `scripts/deploy.mjs`, the Pi host (`scripts/pi/`, `docs/pi-hosting.md`), security headers (CSP, HSTS, frame-ancestors) and Cloudflare WAF/rate-limit settings; (g) **the supply chain and repo**: npm dependencies (`npm audit`, lockfile integrity), GitHub Actions permissions in `ci.yml`, branch protection, the auto-push hooks, secrets in git history, and the Supabase Management API token that `apply-migrations.mjs` uses (who holds it, what it can do, and the 90-day token whose location is unknown); (h) **data protection and resilience**: what personal data is held (contacts, adopters, volunteers; payroll is deliberately out of scope, 2026-09-29), backups (`scripts/backup.mjs`, `backup-schedule.ps1`: where they go, whether they are encrypted, whether a restore has been tested), ransomware or account-takeover recovery, and PDPA (Thailand) obligations; (i) **people and process**: phishing of staff and admin Google accounts, shared logins, offboarding (archive vs delete), the lannacare.org email routing catch-all as a spoofing and impersonation risk (SPF, DKIM and DMARC on the domain), and how much depends on one developer with many AI sessions (bus factor, and what an AI session is permitted to do). **For each finding:** the surface, a plausible attacker (opportunist or bot, disgruntled ex-volunteer, targeted attacker), likelihood × impact → Critical / High / Medium / Low, evidence (file and line, or the query that showed it), and a concrete mitigation with a rough effort estimate. Also list what is already done well, so nobody "fixes" it. Open with a one-page executive summary and a top-5 list that a non-developer (the Director) can read. **Rules of engagement:** read-only, plus hands-on testing **against dev / `test.lannacare.org` only**, never production (production reads are off-limits from sessions). Use the anon key and a test token for each role to confirm what RLS actually allows, not what the policies appear to say. Do not load-test or fuzz Cloudflare. Do not write out real secrets in the report: name where a secret lives, never its value. **Afterwards:** turn every Critical/High finding into its own backlog item (on the `backlog` branch), note Medium/Low in the report only, and add a `docs/decisions.md` entry on any risk that is deliberately accepted. The report goes out as its own PR with a test-plan checklist, which is mostly `n/a: documentation only`.

- [x] **Enforce migration numbering with a check, not prose.** Raised 2026-09-22 after two in-flight branches both shipped `0069` (`0069_seed_standard_diet.sql` on the diet branch and `0069_assistant_actions.sql` on `claude/assistant-v1-schema`) and both were applied to dev before anyone noticed — the same clash as the two `0067` PRs earlier. CLAUDE.md already says the next number is one past the highest on `main` and that only one in-flight branch may carry a migration, but it is prose that nothing enforces, and `apply-migrations.mjs --status` reports `0 pending` while dev holds rows main has no file for, so the divergence is invisible. Add a check that fails at commit time (`.githooks/pre-commit`) and in CI: a new file under `supabase/migrations/` must be numbered exactly one past the highest on `origin/main`, and no two files may share a number. Worth pairing with a `--drift` mode on `apply-migrations.mjs` that lists rows in `schema_migrations` with no matching file — dev currently has **two**, not three: `--status` reported 72 applied against 70 files on `main` with `0 pending` (checked 2026-09-23), so exactly two recorded rows have no file. They are the two renumberings — `0067_public_resident_cards.sql` (now `0068`) and `0069_assistant_actions.sql` (now `0070`, commit `34bf49e`). `0069_seed_standard_diet.sql` kept its number and is still a file on `main`, so it is not an orphan. Both leftovers are harmless, but note that the count in this item was wrong until someone did the arithmetic by hand — which is the whole argument for the flag: an orphan count nobody can read off a command is an orphan count that drifts in the notes as well as the database.

- [x] **Make production drift visible: nothing records what production has actually run.** Raised 2026-09-22, alongside the numbering item above and arguably the larger half of it. `schema_migrations` lives inside each database, so the repo has no idea what production has applied and `apply-migrations.mjs --status` only ever answers for the database it is pointed at. `0069_seed_standard_diet.sql` was applied to **production** while `main` had no file for it at all, and sat that way until PR #46 merged; dev meanwhile held three rows with no file on `main`, and `--status` cheerfully reported `0 pending` throughout. Drift is silent in both directions and nobody finds out until someone rebuilds a database or renumbers a file. Note the only thing that prevented a second production apply was accidental: `scripts/worktree.mjs new` copies `.env.local` but not `.env.deploy.production`, so a feature worktree physically cannot reach production — while the main checkout has the file and can. That is luck, not design. Worth: a `--drift` mode that diffs `schema_migrations` against the files on `origin/main` for a named environment and names both halves (applied-but-missing, present-but-unapplied); a `--status` that says which files on `origin/main` production has **not** run, so the pre-deploy gap is one command; and a loud refusal (or at least a prompt) when `--env production` is used from a branch whose migration is not yet on `origin/main`, since schema is supposed to reach `main` before any database. See `docs/decisions.md` 2026-09-22 for the 0069 clash this came out of.

- [ ] **`deploy.mjs` ships code without ever asking whether the database has the schema it needs.** Raised 2026-09-30 after it went wrong twice in one day. `deploy.mjs` guards a lot before it builds — branch is `main`, working tree clean, `main` level with `origin/main`, `package.json` matching `latestRelease`, `unreleased` empty — and then deploys without checking a single migration. `apply-migrations.mjs --drift <env>` answers exactly that question and already shares its implementation with Settings → System status (`src/lib/migration-drift.ts`), so the work is wiring, not building. **Two consecutive releases, both on 2026-09-30, and the difference between them was luck rather than process:** on `0.10.0` the deploy was started before `0113`–`0116` were applied, and got away with it only because a deploy spends its first minutes building — the migrations were applied alongside it and reached `No drift` at 116 files while the deploy was 134 seconds in and still compiling, so the schema landed before the Worker upload. It was noticed only because an unrelated `--drift` could not write to `deploy.log` (`Device or resource busy`). On `0.10.1` the luck ran out: `0117` went on about an hour *after* the release was live, and its consumer had shipped in that release (`17c7f0d`, the Microchipped tick on `/adopt/[id]`, with `src/app/adopt/[id]/page.tsx` and `src/lib/residents/public.ts` both selecting `is_microchipped`), so the **public adoption profile was querying a column that did not exist**. How long that lasted cannot be established, because that deploy was run without `>> deploy.log` and its start time is unrecorded. Both are written up in `docs/releases/2026-09-30.md`. **What to build:** in the `GUARDED` block (`uat` and `production` only — leave `test` alone, where running ahead is normal and wanted), refuse the deploy when the target database is missing any migration, listing the pending filenames and the `apply-migrations.mjs` command to fix it, and exit `2` like the sibling guards. **Get the comparison right, because the obvious one is wrong:** the rule is *every migration file in the commit being deployed must be applied to the target*, **not** "the target matches `origin/main`". Extra applied rows are fine — schema ahead of code is the safe direction this project deliberately uses, and a migration belonging to the *next* release can legitimately sit on `main` already. `0118` was in exactly that position during `0.10.1`: on `main`, belonging to `#236` which merged after the cut, and correctly not applied. A naive equality check would have refused that deploy for a file it did not need. In practice the two formulations coincide, because the existing guard already requires `HEAD` to equal `origin/main` — but they should not be *written* as the same thing, or the check breaks the first time someone deploys a tag or a hotfix. **And distinguish "the database says it is behind" from "could not ask":** refuse on the first, warn loudly and continue on the second. A deploy already depends on the network, so this is not the `release-prs.mjs` situation where failing closed on a blip was the thing to avoid; but a Supabase timeout should not be able to block a release either. Worth pairing with the reverse check at the other end: `apply-migrations.mjs` could warn when it applies a file whose consumer is not yet deployed, which is the same information read the other way round.


- [x] **A release's PR list is built from `git log --first-parent`, which cannot see every PR.** Found 2026-09-29 preparing `0.9.1`. #213 (`claude/stock-edit-history`, which brought migration `0112`) reached `main` through a plain `Merge remote-tracking branch 'origin/main'` rather than a merge commit naming the PR, so it does **not** appear in `git log --oneline --first-parent <last-release>..HEAD` — the listing every cut plan and every release record uses to enumerate a release's PRs. It was found only by tracing `0112`'s author commit back to its branch, and the release was nearly recorded as eight PRs rather than nine. **Nothing was lost this time**: #213's test plan existed and its release-notes line was a sound `n/a: a shelter user sees no change; the history it now feeds is not shown anywhere`. The failure mode is what matters — a PR the audit cannot see is a PR whose test plan nobody checks and whose *missing* release note nobody notices. Note which gate this defeats: `docs/release-smoke-test.md`'s pre-deploy item is "Every PR in this release has a completed `docs/test-plans/<feature>.md` — **check this by hand**", explicitly by hand because `test-plan` reports red without blocking a merge. A hand check against an incomplete list passes silently, which is worse than no check, because it produces a tick. **How it happens:** any route by which a PR's commits join `main`'s first-parent line without their own merge commit — a branch that merges `origin/main` into itself and is then fast-forwarded, a squash or rebase merge, or a second branch that carried the first one's commits along with it. So it is not a one-off mistake to be avoided by discipline; it is a property of the merge shape, and three of the four shapes are ones GitHub offers by default. **Fix options:** (a) enumerate from GitHub rather than git — `gh pr list --state merged` filtered to PRs whose merge commit is an ancestor of `HEAD` but not of the previous release's SHA; authoritative, but needs network and `gh` auth; (b) drop `--first-parent` and read every commit in the range, grouping by branch name from the merge subjects; catches more, but lists nothing for a branch whose name is gone; (c) **cross-check against the two artifacts every feature must have** — for each file added under `supabase/migrations/` and each `docs/test-plans/*.md` in the range, assert its introducing PR is in the list. This is the cheap safety net and it would have caught this exact case: `0112` was in the range with no PR in the list; (d) a `scripts/release-prs.mjs <since-sha>` doing (a) with (b) as fallback and (c) as an assertion, printing the list a cut plan pastes and exiting non-zero when they disagree. (c) alone is worth having even without the rest, since it needs no network and no new syntax. Worth doing before the next release rather than after: the by-hand check is the only thing standing between an unverified feature and production, and it has now been shown to be checkable against the wrong list.




- [x] **The photo proxy serves any known Drive file to a signed-out visitor, internal attachments included.** Found 2026-09-25 while closing anon's function EXECUTE (`0082`, see `docs/decisions.md`, "Anon loses the functions"). `/api/photos/<fileId>` asks `is_known_drive_file`, which says yes for *every* `attachments` row (blood-test and procedure files, not just resident photos), `project_photos`, `maintenance_photos`, the site photos and Shelter Friend logos, and then streams the bytes with no session check. The only barrier is that a Drive file id is hard to guess; anon cannot list them, but an id that leaks (a shared link, a screenshot of a URL, browser history on a shared shelter PC) opens that file to anyone. And the edge cache (`caches.default`) is keyed by URL alone, so a file a signed-in user viewed is served from cache to anyone with the URL for 24h whatever the database says. Decide what the public side needs: probably the files behind `public_resident_photos`, `public_project_photos`, `site_content(_photos)` and `public_shelter_friends` logos. Then either split the check (`is_public_drive_file` for anon, the current one for signed-in users) or require a session for anything else, and make the cache key or `Cache-Control` distinguish the two (`private` for non-public files). Not a schema-only PR: the route changes with it.
- [x] **Take `is_known_drive_file` back from anon.** Since `claude/photo-proxy-session-check` the photo proxy asks `is_public_drive_file`, and for anything else the caller's own RLS on `attachments`, `project_photos`, `maintenance_photos` and `shelter_friends`; nothing anon-facing calls `is_known_drive_file` any more. `0082` still grants anon EXECUTE on it, and it answers yes for any internal file id, so a signed-out caller can confirm that an internal file exists. A small schema PR: revoke from anon (and probably authenticated: the app no longer calls it), and move it out of `check-public-views.mjs`'s allow-list in the same PR, or that check fails. `check-shelter-friends.mjs` and `check-public-drive-file.mjs` call it as the owner and should be unaffected. **Only after the route change is deployed to production**: until then production's proxy still calls it as anon, and revoking would break every public photo.

- [ ] **Spike: can the app hold several shelters in one database? Measure it, do not estimate it.** Lutan, 2026-09-28: seven shelters have shown interest — five in Chiang Mai, two elsewhere in Thailand — and the system would be **a gift to all of them**, so the ongoing cost must be as close to zero as possible (Lutan carries his own time and Claude costs, the shelters carry their own domains). **That constraint decides the architecture.** A silo (one Supabase project per shelter) cannot meet it: the free tier allows two projects per organisation and pauses a project after a week idle, so seven shelters means seven paid projects, forever, for a gift. Pooling — one database, a `shelter_id` on every table — keeps the marginal cost of each shelter at zero, because it adds no new services: the same two projects, the same Worker, the same Pi origin, the same weekly backup. **So the decision is effectively made; what is not known is the cost of getting there, and that is what this item buys.** Do it on a throwaway branch and do not merge it: add `shelter_id`, a `current_user_shelter_id()` helper (shaped like `current_user_vet_id()` from `0102`), and tenancy as **restrictive** policies — the pattern `0100` already uses for the `aal2` check on `user_roles`, where an `as restrictive` policy is AND-ed with every existing permissive one. That means roughly **one restrictive policy per table (~45), not a rewrite of all 181**. Then push it through the parts restrictive policies do not save you: **53 `security definer` functions**, which bypass RLS by design and each need their own tenant check, and **58 views**. Report a real number of hours and a list of the places that fought back. **Design the spike around the anonymous tier specifically** — the `public_*` views are served to signed-out visitors, so a tenancy miss there publishes another shelter's animals to the internet. That exact class of miss has already happened twice in this codebase at a smaller scale: `0101` closed the public gallery and the photo proxy and profile photos still leaked through three other views until `0103`, and #182 gated five route trees while a vet could still write the `vets` table through the API until `0105`. **Not before Lanna is live** — go-live is still gated on the Pi origin, the domain cutover and the AppSheet cutover, and one running reference shelter is what turns seven interested into seven committed. But **pre-production is the cheapest this will ever be**: adding a tenant column when one shelter has data is far cheaper than when seven do. Record the outcome in `docs/decisions.md` whichever way it goes. Note what is being given up: one database means one blast radius, so a bad migration takes all seven down at once where a silo could roll shelter by shelter. **The product needs its own name and domain (Lutan, 2026-09-28).** Once more than one shelter uses it, the app is no longer Lanna's, so it should not live on `lannacare.org` — that stays Lanna's public site, and each shelter keeps its own domain for its own public pages. Choose a name for the application itself and register a matching **`.org`** to host the staff app on (e.g. `app.<name>.org`, with the shelters' domains or subdomains pointing at the same Worker). **The whole project is in honour of Cooper, Lutan's dog who died recently**, so the name should carry him — shortlist a few Cooper-based `.org` names, check which are free (Cloudflare Registrar, where `lannacare.org` already is, sells at cost), and let Lutan choose. *Shortlist checked 2026-09-28 against the .org registry (RDAP), all unregistered that day: cooperscare.org, coopercare.org, coopershaven.org, cooperskeeper.org, coopersheart.org, coopersden.org, cooperspaws.org, friendsofcooper.org, cooperslegacy.org, cooperscircle.org. Already taken: coopercares, coopersfriends, coopersrescue, coopersplace, teamcooper. Re-check at checkout; a registry premium price shows only there.* Register it through Cloudflare so DNS, Email Routing and the Worker routes sit alongside the existing domain. Decide the name before the tenancy build, because it goes into the Worker routes, auth redirect URLs (Supabase and Google OAuth), release mail's sender and the manual.
- [ ] **Per-shelter Drive: each shelter keeps its photos in its own Google account.** From the seven-shelter discussion, Lutan 2026-09-28. Do **not** pool the photos even if the database is pooled. Each shelter uses their own Google account — 15GB free each — so storage cost never lands on Lutan and never grows without bound, and the photos live in an account the shelter owns, which matters if they ever leave or the arrangement ends. That makes Drive **per-shelter configuration, not per-shelter infrastructure**: a folder id and OAuth credentials keyed by `shelter_id` rather than the single set of env vars `src/lib/google/drive.ts` reads today. **The risk this creates is the one that has already bitten twice, multiplied by seven.** A Drive refresh token dying breaks that shelter's uploads silently until someone tries to add a photo: on 2026-09-25 the `lanna-care-dev` consent screen was still in *Testing*, so the token expired after 7 days and every upload on lannacare.org failed; a 90-day expiry is set somewhere and still unlocated (see Deployment). With seven shelters this stops being an incident and becomes a recurring support load. **Most of the fix already exists** — `scripts/check-drive-token.mjs`, the Drive-connected indicator on `/admin`, and the status-alert mailer from `0098` (#174). Extend the alerting to check each shelter's token and mail before a shelter notices, and treat that as a **prerequisite of onboarding shelter #2, not a follow-up**. Also decide where per-shelter OAuth credentials live: Worker secrets do not scale to seven, so most likely the database, and then how they are protected.
- [ ] **Ask the Chiang Mai five whether they want one regional adoption listing or five separate sites.** From the seven-shelter discussion, Lutan 2026-09-28. This is a product question that decides architecture more firmly than hosting cost does, and it is cheap to ask now and expensive to discover later. Five of the seven interested shelters are in Chiang Mai. Either they want **five separate sites** — each its own brand, adoption page and donors — or they want **one regional network**: a combined "dogs available in Chiang Mai" listing, Shelter Friends shared across shelters, and vet clinics entered once rather than five times (they will largely use the same clinics). The second is the stronger mission outcome — more animals seen by more people — and under a pooled database it is **nearly free to build**, where under a silo it would need a data warehouse on top. If even two of the five want the combined listing, pooling wins outright and the spike above becomes urgent rather than exploratory. Ask before the spike reports, so its answer can shape what the spike measures. Record what they say in `docs/decisions.md`.

- [ ] **Spike: would serving the published photos from the Pi make them load faster? Measure before building.** Lutan, 2026-09-30, once the Pi was serving the site. Today `/api/photos/<fileId>` (`src/app/api/photos/[fileId]/route.ts`) fetches a public photo from Drive through the authenticated API and caches it 24 hours at Cloudflare's edge and in the browser; only a cold miss costs a Drive round trip. Under `next start` on the Pi there is no edge Cache API (the route feature-detects it), so the Worker's own caching is what covers repeat views. The idea is to hold public photos on the Pi's SSD so a miss never waits on Drive. **Questions the spike answers first:** (1) where the time goes today, on a cold photo and a warm one, from Thailand and from elsewhere: Drive fetch, the tunnel hop, or the image size itself (are photos resized, or served at full upload size?); (2) whether a smaller image or a resized variant fixes most of it without touching hosting. **The caution:** a photo served from the Pi leaves through the home connection's upload, which is likely slower than Cloudflare's edge, so the Pi should be the origin behind the edge cache and never a replacement for it. **If it goes ahead, keep the design small:** a disposable pull-through cache on the SSD (Drive stays the source of truth, nothing is uploaded to the Pi), only for files `is_public_drive_file` approves so internal attachments never land on disk, no backup of it, and safe to empty at any time. It should also fit the per-shelter Drive item above rather than assume one Drive. Success is a measured drop in photo load time on a real phone connection, not a Drive request count.
## Documentation

- [x] **Bring the role walkthrough's Vet pass up to date before it is re-run.** Found 2026-09-29 reviewing whether the Vet role is ready for a second test. All twelve items from Pass 1 of `docs/uat/2026-09-27.md` are merged and deployed (test is on 0.9.1), but `docs/role-walkthrough.md` was last touched on 2026-09-27 (`f8cd13b`) and still describes the role as it was **before** those fixes. Run as written it scores correct behaviour as failures, which is worse than not running it. **Pass 1 — what is now wrong:** the "Menu must show" line still lists Enclosures, Maintenance, Vets, Contacts and Projects, all five of which are now correctly refused (order 3, `claude/vet-scope-navigation`); the *Can do* lines "`/vets` and `/contacts` open and read", "`/enclosures` opens" and "`/projects` opens and reads" now land on `/no-access`; and the `/maintenance` rough-edge line ("empty rather than forbidden … write down which it is") is answered — the page calls `requireRole(isShelterRole)` and a vet is refused, so the question should go and a plain refusal line replace it. **Pass 1 — what is missing:** the five *Must not be able to* routes are the test for finding 6 and should say so explicitly (refused **inside** the app at `/no-access`, not on the public home page); the Residents list now names the clinic and shows only that clinic's residents (`0108`); another clinic's vet visits, prescriptions, procedures and blood tests are visible but **read-only**, and Edit says so (`0110`); the vet-visit form offers only the vet's own clinic and a doctor from the managed roster (`0102`, `claude/vet-visit-form-scoping`); a prescription cannot link to a future visit (`0107`) and a weight cannot attach to a visit that already has one (`0106`); the photo upload has no folder picker for a vet and the route answers 403 to anything but Medical; `/manual` and `/releases` now open on the reader's role. **Pass 0 — two changes, both blocking:** (a) add a step to **set the vet account's clinic** at Settings → Security (`user_roles.vet_id`, `0102`) and to check the test resident has a visit, prescription, procedure or blood test from that clinic — without both, `0108` means the vet sees no residents at all and the pass dead-ends on its first line; (b) the "create a recurring job assigned to the test account" step can no longer be done for a vet — `claude/recurring-jobs-eligible-assignees` removed vets from the picker, so the seed that exposed findings 5 and 6 is impossible by design. Rewrite it to seed the job for the Staff and Volunteer passes and to confirm, as a vet line, that the vet is *not* offered. **Not in scope:** Passes 2–6 were never run, so leave them as they are; this is the Vet pass and the Pass 0 lines it depends on. **Also worth doing while here:** every vet fix's `docs/test-plans/*.md` reads `Manual verification by: pending`, and pending on precisely these lines, so the rewritten pass should name which plans a completed run lets someone sign (`vet-scope-navigation`, `vet-clinic-resident-scope`, `vet-cross-clinic-writes`, `vet-visit-form-scoping`, `vet-doctors-roster-screen`, `recurring-jobs-eligible-assignees`, `prescription-no-future-visit`, `one-weight-per-visit`, `role-based-manual`, `release-notes-by-role`, `medical-photos-profile`, `mobile-date-clear`, `file-type-icon`). Docs only, no schema, no `src/` change. **Still not fixed and not this item's job:** finding 17, Cloudflare 1102 on the test site — `ORIGIN_HOST` is still `""` in all three environments of `wrangler.jsonc` and the Pi is deferred to 2026-10-02, so a full run may still be cut short. Finding 6's fix reduces the load (a refusal no longer renders the public home page) but does not remove the mechanism. **Done 2026-09-30** (`claude/walkthrough-vet-pass-update`): most of it landed in #225; this stream added what #227 changed (Appointments replaces My tasks, `/my` redirects) and a marked-pending note for the microchip feature.

- [ ] **Review the manual against real use.** Have a staff member and a volunteer follow it on a phone for a day and note every step that didn't match, was missing (e.g. what to do when an upload fails, how to update a vet visit's status), or used a word the shelter doesn't. Fold the answers back into `en.ts` and re-run the screenshot script.
- [ ] **Thai edition of the manual.** A second content file (`src/lib/manual/th.ts`, same `Manual` shape) picked by locale in `src/app/manual/page.tsx`, with screenshots captured with the app switched to Thai (the script would need a locale flag and a `public/manual/th/` output folder). Wait until the English text has been through the review above so it's translated once.
- [x] **Manual as a printable PDF.** Volunteers without a login (and the office wall) want a paper copy. `@react-pdf/renderer` is already a dependency for the resident summary, so a `/manual/pdf` route that renders the same `Manual` data is the natural route; the Thai fonts under `src/lib/archive/fonts/` cover the Thai edition.
- [x] **Give the manual's table of contents its own scroll bar.** Customer request 2026-09-24: on `/manual` the Contents list and the text scroll together, so on a long page the contents are out of reach and anything below the fold of the list can only be reached by scrolling the whole page. The desktop `<aside>` in `src/app/manual/page.tsx` is already `lg:sticky lg:top-6` but has no height limit, so once the list is taller than the window it just scrolls away with the page. Cap it to the viewport (e.g. `lg:max-h-[calc(100dvh-…)]` allowing for the app header, plus `lg:overflow-y-auto` and `overscroll-contain`) so the contents stay put with their own scroll bar while the sections scroll beside them. Check sticky actually engages — if the app shell scrolls an inner container rather than the window, the offset and height must be measured against that — and consider scrolling the active entry into view in the list. The phone `<details>` version is unaffected. Update the manual's own description of the Contents panel in `src/lib/manual/en.ts` if it has one.
- [x] **Deep links into the manual land short of their topic.** Found 2026-09-24 while verifying `claude/manual-toc-scroll`: a cold load of `/manual#getting-help` on dev (1400×900) ended with the section 1279px below the top of the window. The manual's screenshots are `loading="lazy"` `<img>`s with no `width`/`height` (`Screenshot` in `src/app/manual/page.tsx`), so they load after the browser's jump to the anchor and push the text down. Give each screenshot its intrinsic size (store `width`/`height` in `ManualScreenshot`, or read them in `scripts/manual-screenshots.mjs` when capturing) so the space is reserved, then recheck a deep link cold. Pre-existing; the Contents list itself highlights the right entry.
- [x] **Four manual screenshots are missing from `public/manual/`.** Found 2026-09-25 by `claude/manual-deep-links`: `diet-new.png`, `management-diets.png`, `management-cashflow.png` and `admin-blood-test-types.png` are referenced in `src/lib/manual/en.ts` but 404, so those topics show a broken image. `scripts/manual-screenshots.mjs` has no entry at all for `diet-new` or `management-cashflow`, so the planned full re-run will not fix those two by itself — add them to `SIGNED_IN` first (`diet-new` needs a resident id, like `prescription-new`).
- [x] **The last topics in the manual cannot scroll to the top of the window.** Found 2026-09-25 by `claude/manual-deep-links`: `/manual#getting-help` (the last topic) lands about 800px down a 900px window because the page ends 100px below it and is already at maximum scroll. Closing it means blank space under the manual (e.g. bottom padding of about a screen height minus the last topic), which is a design call. The image-shift half of the old deep-link item is fixed.
- [x] **Release notes as an accordion: version and title first, details on click.** Done 2026-09-24: each release is a `<details>`, newest open; `OpenReleaseFromHash` opens the release a `#v<version>` link targets; no Expand all yet (three releases; `docs/decisions.md`). Customer request 2026-09-24. `/releases` (`src/app/releases/page.tsx`) renders every release in full — version, title, date, Major and environment badges, then every note — so it grows by a screenful with each release (0.2.0 alone has seven long notes) and older releases are only reached by scrolling past everything newer. Make each release a collapsed row showing **version · title**, the date and the badges, which expands to its notes on click. Native `<details>`/`<summary>` is enough — keyboard and screen-reader accessible with no client JavaScript, so the page stays a server component; style the marker as a chevron that turns when open. Decide while building: (a) whether the **newest release starts expanded** (probably yes — it is the one people come to read) with the rest collapsed; (b) the existing `id="v<version>"` anchors must still land on an **open** release, since links such as `/releases#v0.2.0` would otherwise point at a closed `<details>` with its notes hidden — open the targeted one (a small client effect reading `location.hash`, including on `hashchange`); (c) the dev-only "Not released yet" block stays expanded as it is; (d) optionally an Expand all / Collapse all control once there are enough releases to want it. Check it on a phone, where the badges already wrap under the title. The manual's release-notes topic gets a line saying to click a release to see what changed, and the PR adds its own line to `unreleased`.

- [x] **A role-based manual: show each role the manual for what it can do.** Done 2026-09-27 (see `docs/decisions.md`): `/manual` opens on the reader's role; other roles' topics are hidden until found (Find on page and #anchors reveal them, greyed), a section with none of the reader's topics goes with its intro, and Show everything (`?view=all`) brings the rest back greyed. Lutan, 2026-09-27, Pass 1 (Vet) of the role walkthrough. The manual already knows the answer — every topic carries `roles` in `src/lib/manual/en.ts`, and the page shows them as badges — but it shows everybody everything, so a vet reads intake, stocktake, cashflow and Settings to find the seven medical topics that apply to them. Filter `/manual` to the signed-in role by default. **Keep a way to see the rest**, a "show everything" toggle, because "can I do this?" is a real question and an empty search result is a worse answer than a greyed one. Two things to get right: topics with no `roles` mean *everyone* and must not vanish; and the section intros describe whole sections ("For the management and admin roles…") so a filtered view needs the section to disappear too when nothing in it survives. Worth pairing with the vet-scope item — as the vet's menu shrinks, a manual that still explains Enclosures is actively wrong.
- [x] **Release notes segmented by role.** Done 2026-09-28 (see `docs/decisions.md`): `/releases` opens on the reader's role with the manual's `isForRole`; every release stays listed with its number, and one with nothing for the role carries a "Nothing for Vet" badge and says so; `?view=all` shows the rest greyed. A note may now be `{ text, roles }`; released history stays untagged, which reads as everyone. The release mail stays unfiltered. Raised by Lutan, 2026-09-27, Pass 1 (Vet) of the role walkthrough. Nice to have, and Lutan flagged the difficulty in raising it. A vet reading `/releases` gets stocktake, cashflow and deploy-tooling entries that will never mean anything to them. Tagging each note with the roles it affects and filtering the page the same way the manual would is the obvious shape. **The hard part is versions, not tags:** releases are numbered whole-app, so a vet filtering the list would see `0.7.0` followed by `0.4.0` with gaps where nothing touched them, which reads like a broken page. Options are showing every version with "nothing for you in this one", or dropping version numbers from the filtered view and showing dated changes. Also the release mail: it goes to admins, so it is not affected, but any per-role notes work should decide whether that stays true. Low priority — do the manual first, it has the same shape and more value.

## Public website

- [x] **Settings → Website: let the shelter choose its preferred contact channel for the public site.** Lutan, 2026-09-28. The public pages lead with **LINE** wherever they offer one way to get in touch: the sticky "Ask on LINE" bar at the bottom of each animal's profile (`src/app/adopt/[id]/page.tsx`), the "get in touch" card on site pages (`SitePageView.tsx`, "Email us or message us on LINE"), the footer's Contact us (`PublicFooter.tsx`) and the order of the phone menu's "Talk to us" buttons (`PublicHeader.tsx` / `PublicNav.tsx`, "LINE first"). But Lanna actually use **Facebook Messenger, Instagram and WhatsApp**, and with the multi-shelter plan each shelter will have its own habits, so this belongs in a setting, not the code. **The links are already settings:** `site_content` holds `contact_line`, `messenger_url`, `whatsapp_number` and `instagram_url` (0080), edited and validated on Settings → Website (`SiteSettingsForm.tsx`, `src/lib/links/validate.ts`). What is missing is the choice. **Add:** a "Preferred way to contact us" picker on Settings → Website (LINE / Messenger / WhatsApp / Instagram / Phone / Email), offering only channels that have a value, stored as e.g. `site_content.preferred_channel` (a small `-schema` PR first; default `line` so nothing changes until someone picks). Consider an ordered list instead of one choice, so the second and third buttons follow the shelter's order too. **Use it everywhere LINE is hard-coded first:** the sticky bar's label and link ("Message us on Messenger", `m.me` link; WhatsApp opens `wa.me/<number>` with the message pre-filled as the email subject is today; Instagram opens the profile, since it has no pre-filled DM link), the get-in-touch card and its hint text, the footer and the Talk to us order. One helper (e.g. `preferredChannel(content)` returning label, href and icon) so every page asks the same question. Fall back to the next channel set, then phone, then email, if the chosen one is cleared. Text in en and th for each channel's call to action. **Multi-tenancy:** `site_content` is one row today; the tenancy spike's `shelter_id` makes it one per shelter, so a setting here is already per-tenant — no extra design needed, but list it among the tables the spike covers. Manual: the Website settings topic; a releases line (visitors see the button change). **Schema half done 2026-09-29 (migration `0111`, `claude/schema-contact-channel`):** an ordered list, `site_content.preferred_channels text[]` default `{line}` (first = preferred, the rest = button order and fallback), not a single `preferred_channel`; see `docs/decisions.md`. **Feature half done 2026-09-29 (`claude/contact-channel-picker`):** the picker on Settings → Website, `preferredChannels(content)` in `src/lib/site/channels.ts`, and the four public surfaces all converted; see `docs/decisions.md`.
- [x] **A "Pet relocation" information page on the public site.** Done 2026-09-27 (see `docs/decisions.md`): `/relocation` is a `SitePageView` page with `PuppyFlying` (sketch B, crate, in pyjamas) beside the heading, under a new **Services** menu group (Lutan: services is its own tree, desexing drives to follow) and in the footer, with a line on `/adopt` for adopters abroad. Ships with generic starter text in English and Thai, shown until an admin saves a body and prefilled in the editor; no licence, timeline or price claims.
