Original item: Lutan, 2026-09-26: the Director is licensed to manage pet relocations, both international and within Thailand, and offers them as a service. The website should say so. **An information page, not a booking system:** no enquiry form, no case tracking, no payments. People get in touch through the contact channels the site already shows (LINE, phone, email, and Messenger / WhatsApp once that item lands). **Build it the way `/foster`, `/volunteer` and `/donate` are built:** a new `site_pages` slug (0059) rendered by `SitePageView`, with title and body typed by an admin on `/admin/website` and the Thai version going through the translation queue, so the Director can change the wording herself. `site_pages.slug` has a check constraint listing the five slugs (0059), so adding `relocation` (and seeding its empty row) is a tiny schema PR first. **Where it links from:** the redesign's menu (under "Get involved" or "About & contact"; agree which with Lutan) and the footer, plus a line on `/adopt` for overseas adopters ("Adopting from abroad? We can arrange the journey"). **Content to get from the Director** (the page ships with her text, not placeholder copy): what she offers (international and domestic moves, and which parts she handles: paperwork, vet checks and vaccinations, export permit, flights, road transport), her licence details (licence number and issuing body, and any wording the licence requires), typical timelines, whether it is for shelter adopters, the public or both, and how to get in touch. `generateMetadata` with Open Graph tags like the other public pages. A releases line. **Unblocked 2026-09-27 (Lutan): ship generic copy now, modelled on what a Thai pet-relocation service page covers** — e.g. Boonma (boonma.com), Thailand's oldest mover, whose pet service is one-stop: documents, vet checks and vaccinations, microchip, export permit, crate, flight booking, customs clearance at both ends, and door-to-door delivery, international and domestic. So the page no longer waits on the Director's copy, and the body is admin-editable anyway — she edits it herself when she has time. **Two things that do NOT become generic.** (a) **Write original text.** Do not copy Boonma's wording or anyone else's; describe what the service involves in our own words. (b) **Licence claims still come from her.** The generic "what's involved" content is safe, but a statement that *this* shelter or Director is licensed — licence number, issuing body, any wording the licence requires — is a factual claim about a real person and must not be invented or implied. Ship the page without a licence block, or with a placeholder only she fills, and leave "Licensed by…" out until she supplies it. Same for typical timelines and prices: omit rather than guess. Still a tiny schema PR first for the slug. **A flying caped puppy in the page heading** (Lutan, 2026-09-27): the same character as the loading animation — the apricot poodle-cross from `src/components/PuppyLoader.tsx` (#148) — in a cape, flying, as the hero illustration. It suits the subject: the dog that carries pets across the world. Add it as a third drawing beside `PuppyAtLaptop` and `PuppyGlyph` in that file, same approach (plain SVG, CSS keyframes in `globals.css`, redesign palette, `prefers-reduced-motion` respected — a still pose rather than a hover/flap loop), and it is then reusable anywhere else a "journey" illustration is wanted. **Keep it our own character, not a Superman pastiche.** A cape and a flying pose are generic and fine; Superman's costume (the S-shield, the red/blue/yellow scheme) and Krypto the Superdog (DC's white dog in a red cape) are DC's, so no shield, no chest emblem, and not that colourway — use our terracotta and forest green. Agree the drawing with Lutan the way the loader was agreed: two or three sketches first.

Its contact card adds Call, a prefilled email, and a prefilled LINE message when the LINE id is an Official Account. Original brief: From the backlog page's suggestion box, 2026-09-26: on the redesigned homepage the Shelter Friends band's **Become a Shelter Friend** button and the dashed **Your business here?** tile both link to `#contact` (`src/app/page.tsx`, around lines 280 and 315), so a business owner who clicks lands on the footer's phone numbers with nothing about what being a Shelter Friend means or how to join. **Build it like the relocation page above:** a new `site_pages` slug (e.g. `shelter-friends-join`) rendered by `SitePageView` at `/friends/join` (or a section at the top of `/friends`), with title and body edited on `/admin/website` and Thai through the translation queue. It explains what a Shelter Friend is, the ways to help (donating goods such as food, litter, medicine or building materials, giving supporters a discount, offering a service), what the business gets in return (logo and profile on `/friends` and in the homepage band, and which contact details are shown only with their agreement, per the opt-ins from 0076), and how to get in touch (LINE, phone, email, with an optional prefilled LINE or email message). **Link it from:** both homepage tiles, a line at the top of `/friends`, and the `/donate` "Give in kind" mention. **Schema:** `site_pages.slug` has a check constraint (0059), so the new slug needs a tiny schema PR. **Fold it into the same schema PR as the relocation page's slug.** Starter text from the Shelter Friends item's own description, then reworded by the Director. A releases line.
- [x] **Link to the LCA Facebook page from the public site.** Done 2026-09-25: columns in 0080 (#105); Facebook page and Instagram fields on `SiteSettingsForm.tsx` checked by `src/lib/links/validate.ts` (Instagram hosts added beside Facebook's), an icon row under LINE in the footer, and the Facebook icon in the header from `sm` up. Icons are inline SVGs (`src/components/FacebookIcon.tsx`, `InstagramIcon.tsx`) because lucide-react 1.x has no brand icons, and there was no JSON-LD block, so no `sameAs`. Both fields ship empty until the customer supplies the links. Customer request 2026-09-22. The public footer (`src/app/adopt/PublicFooter.tsx`) already shows address, email, phone and a LINE link from `site_content`, but nothing points at the shelter's Facebook page, which is where most of their supporters actually are. Add `facebook_url` (and while there `instagram_url`, both nullable text) to `site_content` in a new migration, fields for them on the admin Website form (`/admin/website`, `StoryForm.tsx` / `actions.ts` — validate they are `https://` URLs on the right hosts), and in the footer render a small row of social icons (lucide `Facebook` / `Instagram`, `target="_blank" rel="noreferrer"`, `aria-label` from the dictionaries) after the LINE link, shown only for the URLs that are set. Put the same Facebook link in `PublicHeader.tsx` next to the language switcher on desktop only, and add `sameAs` with the URLs to the site's JSON-LD Organization block if one exists. Distinct from the low-priority "Publish to Facebook from the app" item below, which is about posting, not linking.
- [x] **"Shelter Friends" — thank the businesses that help, on the public site.** Done 2026-09-24 (schema 0076 in #89, feature in `claude/shelter-friends`; see `docs/decisions.md`): `/friends` reads only `public_shelter_friends`; a Shelter Friend card on the contact hub (make / edit / opt-ins / publish / live preview), badge and filter chip on both contact lists, Management → Shelter Friends for the order; header/footer link, home logo strip and `/donate` mention appear once someone is published. The link validator is `src/lib/links/validate.ts` for the Facebook-link item to reuse. Thai title is a placeholder until the customer confirms it. Original brief: Customer request 2026-09-24. Local companies help the shelter by donating goods (food, litter, medicine, building materials) or giving discounts (a vet clinic, a feed shop, a hardware store). The shelter wants to feature them publicly: who they are, what they do for the shelter, links to their website / Facebook page, and a map and contact details — both as a thank-you and so supporters can give them custom. **Build on contacts, don't duplicate them:** the business is already (or should be) a row in `contacts` — the Vendor it buys from, with phone, email, LINE, address and the resolved maps link from the contact-hub tidy-up — so a Friend is a contact with a public profile, not a second address book. Keep the curated public face in its own 1:1 table rather than more columns on `contacts`, so nothing private leaks by accident and the public view has an obvious boundary: **schema PR first** — `shelter_friends` (`contact_id` unique FK → `contacts`, `blurb` text through the translations table/queue like `site_pages`, `help_kind` (Donates goods / Discount / Services / Sponsorship — or free text, ask), `discount_note` e.g. "10% off for adopters, show your adoption card", `website_url`, `facebook_url`, `logo_drive_file_id` (Drive-hosted like the hero photo), `friend_since date`, `sort_order`, `published boolean default false`, and per-field opt-ins `show_phone` / `show_email` / `show_line` / `show_address` / `show_map`, all default false), admin/management RLS, and a `public_shelter_friends` view granted to `anon` that returns only published rows and only the opted-in contact fields — add it to `scripts/check-public-views.mjs`. A friend whose contact is archived (see "Archive contacts" under Management) drops out of the view. **Staff side:** a "Shelter Friend" card on `/contacts/[id]` (Make a Shelter Friend / edit profile / publish toggle / preview link), a Friend badge and filter chip on `/contacts` and `/management/contacts`, and a list on `/admin/website` (or Management → Shelter Friends) to reorder and unpublish. Validate URLs as `https://`, Facebook on facebook.com / fb.com — share the validator with the Facebook-link item above. **Contact type: stays `Vendor`** (decided 2026-09-24) — any business, including one that only donates, is entered as a Vendor; no enum change in this item. Keep the Friend profile keyed on `contact_id`, not on the type, so a type added later (e.g. `Business` / `Supporter`) can become a Shelter Friend with no further schema work — the Make a Shelter Friend action may be offered on Vendors only for now, but gate it in one place so widening it is a one-line change. **Public side:** `/friends` (title "Shelter Friends" / Thai to confirm with the customer) — a card per friend: logo, name, what they do for the shelter, discount note, website / Facebook icon links (`target="_blank" rel="noreferrer"`), phone / LINE tap-to-contact and the lazy-loaded map embed reused from the contact hub, only for what they opted into; linked from `PublicHeader` / `PublicFooter`, a small logo strip on `/` ("Thank you to our Shelter Friends"), and a mention on `/donate` ("give in kind — our friends' discounts go further"). `generateMetadata` with OG tags like the other public pages. **Consent:** the opt-in flags are the point — staff tick only what the business agreed to show, so the manual topic should say to ask first. **Link to Fundraising:** if donations are recorded later, an in-kind gift from a friend should be able to point at the same contact, so the two meet on `contact_id`. Manual topic, dictionary strings in both languages, a releases line and a `docs/decisions.md` entry (separate table vs. contact columns; why opt-in per field).
- [x] **Shelter Friends: make "Remove profile" clearly about the Friend status, not the contact.** Found by Lutan checking #103 on test, 2026-09-24. The action exists — **Remove profile**, a red button at the bottom of the form after tapping Edit profile — and he found it, but the wording is the risk: on a contact's own page, a red "Remove profile" reads as deleting the contact, so staff may avoid it when they only mean "no longer a Shelter Friend", or press it expecting the contact to go. (It only deletes the `shelter_friends` row, its text and translations; the contact stays, and so do its placements.) Relabel it **Remove Shelter Friend status** (or "Stop being a Shelter Friend"), and make the confirm lead with "The contact stays" rather than end with it. Consider moving it out of the edit form into the card's own row beside Unpublish / Edit profile / Preview (managers only; `src/app/contacts/[id]/ShelterFriendCard.tsx`), with a line saying Unpublish is "hide for now" and Remove is "no longer a Friend". Update the manual topic (`shelter-friends` in `src/lib/manual/en.ts`) and both dictionaries (`shelterFriends.card.removeProfile` / `removeConfirm`). No schema. When done, also record the rest of #103's manual sign-off in `docs/test-plans/shelter-friends.md`: Lutan passed items 1 (staff read-only), 3 (Thai wording, title kept) and 4 (the look, "fine for now") on 2026-09-24; item 2 (Remove profile) he found and it works, and the wording concern is this item.
- [x] **Resident photo thumbnails: stop the ever-growing grid.** Done 2026-09-22: `src/components/ThumbnailStrip.tsx` (single-row scrolling strip) on the adoption and story galleries; the staff Photos tab shows 8 tiles with a "Show all (N)" toggle. Customer feedback 2026-09-22 (see Panda on lannacare.org): a resident with many photos shows every thumbnail at once and the block just keeps growing. Public page `src/app/adopt/[id]/PhotoGallery.tsx` already has a hero image but its thumbnail strip is `flex-wrap`, so it wraps into more and more rows; the staff Photos tab (`src/components/PhotoGallery.tsx`) is a plain grid with no cap. Replace both with a bounded layout: a single-row thumbnail strip that scrolls left/right (`overflow-x-auto`, snap, arrow buttons on desktop, swipe on phones, keep the selected thumbnail in view), or show the first N (say 8) with a "Show all (23)" toggle that expands in place — pick one and use it in both galleries so they feel the same. Prefer the scrolling strip on the public page (the hero stays put, the strip is secondary) and "show all" on the staff tab, where set-as-profile and delete need every tile reachable. Keep lazy loading so a long strip doesn't fetch every image through the proxy up front.
- [x] **Keep PDFs out of the public story gallery.** Noticed 2026-09-28 while building the file-type icon (`claude/file-type-icon`), not reproduced. Project uploads accept PDFs (`src/app/api/projects/[id]/photos/route.ts`), and the `public_project_photos` view (last defined in `0056_translations.sql`) returns every attachment in a public subfolder with no type filter, so a PDF filed in a public project folder would reach `/our-work/[id]`, where `StoryGallery.tsx` and `ThumbnailStrip.tsx` render it as a `next/image` — a broken picture on the public site. The staff side is safe: only images can be made the cover, and `PhotoSection.tsx` shows a PDF as a file icon. **Fix:** exclude non-images in the view (`where a.file_name ~* '\.(jpe?g|png|webp|heic|heif|gif)$'`, the same rule as `src/lib/uploads/file-kind.ts`), a tiny schema PR, and add the case to `scripts/check-public-views.mjs`. First check on test whether any public folder holds a PDF today. No releases line unless one was live.
- [ ] **"Contact us about an animal" form (later).** Raised 2026-09-21 while reviewing the public site: a public form for reporting a stray, an injured animal or a welfare concern (what / where / photo / how to reach you) that lands somewhere staff see it — a `public_enquiries` table with an admin or management list, or simply an email to the shelter's contact address via the existing Gmail plumbing. Overlaps with the Lost & found item below; probably one form with a "what is this about" choice. Not started.
- [ ] **Lost & found report form (future).** RSPCA ACT has a public "report a lost pet" form. Out of scope until the core app is wired up, but a natural later public feature: a public form (species, description, photo, location, contact) writing to a `lost_pet_reports` table that staff review from an admin list, with a link to the intake flow if the resident arrives at the shelter.
- [ ] **Publish to Facebook from the app (low priority, discuss first).** Raised 2026-09-22: could a resident profile, a project story or a "pet of the week" be posted to the shelter's Facebook Page (feed post, photo, or a 24-hour Story) from inside the app? Technically yes — the Meta Graph API publishes to a Page the app has been granted (`pages_manage_posts` + a long-lived Page token held as a Worker secret; Page Stories have their own photo/video endpoint; Instagram Business publishing is a sibling if the Page has an IG account). Personal profiles are not publishable by API. The cost is not code but Meta's App Review (screencast, business verification of the Page; `/privacy` already exists) and a token that needs refreshing. Before building anything, decide between: (a) no API at all — a **Share on Facebook** button on `/adopt/[id]` and `/our-work/[id]` that opens the share dialog pre-filled with the public URL (the OG tags are already there, so the card renders well) and staff post from their own account; (b) Meta Business Suite scheduling straight from the Page, no app changes; (c) the full integration — an Admin → Website action that composes a post from a resident or project (photo, bilingual caption from `translations`, link back) and publishes to the Page, with a log of what was posted. Start with (a) if anything; only go to (c) if staff are posting several times a week and the copy-paste is the pain.
- [x] **A deliberate delay on the way to the available dogs, to show off the puppy loader.** [done 2026-09-28: `SHOWCASE_LOADER_MS` env var, on (1800ms) in test/uat/production per Lutan — see `docs/decisions.md`] Lutan, 2026-09-27. Going from the home page to the dogs up for adoption (`/adopt`, linked from `src/app/page.tsx`) is usually fast enough that the puppy-at-a-laptop loader (`src/components/PuppyLoader.tsx`, root `src/app/loading.tsx`) never shows: it waits 300 ms before appearing so quick loads don't flash it. Add a short, deliberate pause on that page so people see the animation, e.g. 1.5–2 s. **How:** an `await` pause at the top of the `/adopt` server page (before its data fetch), so the route's `loading.tsx` streams the puppy during it. Only on that page's first load from navigation, not when changing filters or paging within it. **Keep it switchable, not hard-coded:** read the length from an env var (e.g. `SHOWCASE_LOADER_MS`, `0` or unset = no pause), set in `wrangler.jsonc` per environment, so it can be on for demos on test/UAT and turned off (or shortened) for real visitors in production without a code change — a slower page costs adopters and search ranking. Clamp it (say, at most 3 s) so a typo cannot hang the page. Decide with Lutan whether production gets it at all at launch; record the choice and the reason in `docs/decisions.md`. Check it on a phone: the animation plays through, the page arrives cleanly after it, and the back button does not replay the pause. No manual change (public page); a `src/lib/releases.ts` line only if production gets the pause.
- [x] **Public site: make the way back obvious — a Home link and "back to the list" on detail pages.** Lutan, 2026-09-27: on the website's sub pages there is no back button, and it is not obvious how to get back to the home page. Today the only way home is the logo in `src/app/adopt/PublicHeader.tsx` (it does link to `/`, but visitors don't know that), the menu has no Home entry, and detail pages such as a resident (`/adopt/[id]`) and a project (`/our-work/[id]`) have no link back to their list, so on a phone the only way out is the browser's back gesture. **Suggested:** (1) a **Home** entry at the start of the menu, at least in the mobile menu (`PublicNav.tsx`), where the logo is least likely to be tried; on desktop, decide with Lutan whether the logo plus a "Home" word is enough. (2) A clear **"← All dogs and cats"** link at the top of `/adopt/[id]` and **"← All projects"** on `/our-work/[id]`: if the visitor came from the list, go back in history so their filters and scroll position are kept; if they arrived from outside (a shared link, Facebook, search), link to the list instead of leaving the site. (3) **Breadcrumbs, at least on desktop (Lutan, 2026-09-27):** a breadcrumb line under the header on every sub page — Home › Adopt › Panda, Home › Our work › <project>, Home › Get involved › Foster — each part a link, the last one plain text for the page you are on. On a phone, decide whether the breadcrumb shows too (shortened, e.g. only the parent: "‹ Adopt") or whether (2) does that job there. Mark it up as a `nav` with `aria-label="Breadcrumb"` and `aria-current="page"`, and add schema.org `BreadcrumbList` data so search results show the path. Also a Home link in the footer. The information pages (Foster, Volunteer, Donate, Friends, Our story) get the Home entry from the menu; they have no list to go back to. Both languages (`src/lib/i18n/dictionaries/en.ts` / `th.ts`), big enough tap targets on a phone, and check the look against the redesign mockups (Next up) so this doesn't fight them — if the redesign stream is still open, fold this into it. No schema change. A `src/lib/releases.ts` line.
- [x] **Homepage polish: centre the impact figures, and rethink the Shelter Friends logo tiles.** Lutan, 2026-09-27, from a screenshot of the live homepage. Both are in `src/app/page.tsx`. **(1) Impact band:** the four figures (in our care today / adopted this year / with foster families / in vet care) sit left-aligned in their columns, which leaves the right of each column empty and the band looking lopsided. Centre each number and its label in its column (`items-center text-center` on each figure), on the two-column phone layout as well as the four-column desktop one. If the Impact band item (Next up) adds figures, the centring should hold for five or six too. **(2) Shelter Friends logos:** Lutan is not a fan of the white box behind each logo. Each logo sits in a cream "paper" tile with a border on the sand band, and most business logos bring their own background (I Blue Paw's is a navy square), so it reads as a box inside a box inside a band. Worth trying, and showing Lutan side by side before choosing (as with the puppy loader sketches): (a) **no tile** — the logo straight on the sand band at a common height, the business name small underneath, a gentle lift on hover; (b) **the logo is the tile** — the image fills a rounded tile edge to edge (`object-cover`), so the logo's own colour is the background; (c) **round badges** — each logo in a circle, like stickers; (d) a **softer tile** — no border, a shade darker than the band instead of lighter. Whatever is chosen, the dashed "Your business here?" tile must match its shape and size, logos of very different proportions (wide wordmarks, square icons) must all look deliberate, and a friend with no logo still shows their name nicely. Check the same tiles on `/friends` and keep the two consistent. Check contrast (the band colours already sit near AA, see the comment on the dashed tile). No schema change. A `src/lib/releases.ts` line is optional — a visitor would barely notice.

- [ ] **Desexing drives under Services on the public site.** Lutan, 2026-09-27, while placing Pet relocation: the public menu got a **Services** group (`PublicHeader.tsx`) for what the shelter offers the public, as opposed to ways to help it. Pet relocation has since come off the site (International adoption replaced it, under Adopt), so Services is empty and hidden in the header and footer until this item gives it an entry. Desexing drives — days when people bring their own pets to be desexed — belong there too. **Scope decided by Lutan, 2026-09-28: dated events with sign-up, not an information page.** So this is **not** a `site_pages` slug and not a tiny schema PR — that was the alternative and it was turned down. It is **two streams in sequence**: `claude/schema-desexing-drives` (a real `desexing_drives` table plus a sign-ups table, RLS, and a `public_desexing_drives` view granted to `anon`, added to `scripts/check-public-views.mjs`), then `claude/desexing-drives-page` (the public route, the **Services** entry in `PublicHeader.tsx` / `PublicNav.tsx` and the footer, and a management list to create drives and read sign-ups). Services stays empty and hidden until the second one lands. **Do not hand the schema half to a session as a one-liner — it needs a scoping pass with Lutan first,** because the table shape follows answers nobody has yet: what a drive record holds (date, venue, species, capacity, whether a drive can be cancelled or full), what a sign-up holds (owner name and contact, pet name / species / sex, a slot or just the day), and above all **whether the public sign-up writes to the database at all or is a "message us on LINE" handoff**. That last one is the real decision: a public write path is a personal-data question before it is a table. If it does write, the sign-up list is never public (anon gets `insert` only, never `select`, and the public view exposes drives, not the people who signed up), it needs rate limiting, and there is no CAPTCHA option here — so decide what abuse is acceptable rather than assuming it can be blocked. Also: whether drives appear on the home page. No dates, prices or eligibility rules invented — those come from the shelter. Manual topic and a `src/lib/releases.ts` line with the page.
- [x] **Check the website's LINE link opens the shelter's LINE Official Account.** Noticed 2026-09-27 while building `/friends/join` (#175), not yet tested on a phone. `lineLink()` in `src/lib/site/content.ts` strips the `@` from an id like `@lannacare` and builds `https://line.me/R/ti/p/~lannacare`. As LINE documents it, `~` is the add-friend form for a **personal** LINE id, and an **Official Account** uses `https://line.me/R/ti/p/@lannacare` (the `@` kept, URL-encoded as `%40`). If the shelter's LINE is an Official Account, every LINE button on the site may be opening the wrong thing or nothing: the footer, the phone menu's Talk to us, the Get in touch cards, Ask on LINE on resident pages, and Shelter Friends cards (check `FriendCard` too, in case it builds its own). **First, on a phone with LINE:** tap the LINE button on test and see where it lands. Also find out from the Director whether the shelter's LINE is an Official Account or a personal id. **If it is broken:** keep `~` for an id typed without `@` and use `@` for one typed with it, so that both kinds work. Leave pasted URLs as they are. Keep `lineMessageLink()` (the prefilled message on `/friends/join`, which already uses the `oaMessage/@id` form) consistent with it. Update the Settings → Website hint if it says how to type the id. No schema. Add a releases line if it was broken for visitors.
- [ ] **Later: let the public register their own pet's microchip, as a start towards a national register.** Lutan, 2026-09-29. Microchipping is not standard in Thailand and there is no national register that most owners use, so once residents carry chip numbers (the Resident operations item), open a public page where an owner records their own pet's chip against their contact details, so a shelter that scans a found animal can reach them. **Depends on:** the resident microchip item first, and the multi-shelter spike (Architecture), since a register is only useful across shelters. **Scope a paper before building, like the merch and B2B items:** who runs and is accountable for the data (the shelter, a foundation, or the app's owner), PDPA consent and retention for owners' personal details, how an owner proves the animal is theirs and how a disputed or transferred chip is handled, what a scanning shelter is shown (a way to contact the owner through the system, not the owner's phone number and address), abuse and spam on a public form, and whether this should join an existing register rather than become another one (find out first whether the Department of Livestock Development or a local authority keeps one, and which international lookup services Thai-issued chips are listed with). No schema and no public form until that is agreed; the privacy stakes are close to those that took payroll off this backlog.

## Fundraising

- [ ] **Make `/donate` ask for money and record what arrives.** Customer request 2026-09-23. Today `/donate` is just one of the five `site_pages` slugs (0059) — a title and a body the admin types on `/admin/website`, rendered by `SitePageView` (`src/lib/site/pages.ts`) and translated through the manager's queue — so the shelter can *describe* how to give, but the site asks for nothing and the system records nothing. Note also that `contact_type` lost its `Donor` value in 0067 as unused, so a donor is currently not modelled at all. **Ask the customer before any schema:** which channels people actually use today — Thai bank transfer / PromptPay QR, cash at the shelter, a Facebook fundraiser, PayPal or card from overseas supporters — and who thanks them and keeps the list now. Then build to that answer, in this order. (a) **A give block on `/donate`** — a PromptPay QR image (Drive-hosted like the hero photo) plus bank account name and number and whatever overseas option they use, as nullable columns on `site_content` edited on `/admin/website` beside the contact block; no integration at all, covers most Thai giving, and worth shipping on its own. (b) **Record donations** — schema first, its own PR: a `donations` table (`amount numeric(12,2)`, `received_on date`, method, optional `contact_id` plus a free-text donor name for one-offs, optional designation to a resident or a project, note, `recorded_by`), an admin/management RLS pair, and a **Management → Donations** page to enter one, list a window and total it. That total is the income half the Cashflow forecast item above has no source for, so design the two to meet. (c) **Appeals with a target** — a campaign row (title, body, goal, deadline, cover photo) with a progress bar on `/our-work/[id]` and a "sponsor {name}" block on `/adopt/[id]`, summing the donations designated to it; the translation plumbing and the `site_pages` body renderer already cover the prose. (d) **Card / online payment**, only if the customer says they need it — a hosted checkout (Opn/Omise covers PromptPay and Thai cards, Stripe for overseas), keys as Worker secrets, a webhook route under `src/app/api` writing the donation row, and no card data ever touching the app or the Pi. Don't skip to (d): (a) and (b) are most of the value for none of the compliance. Manual topic, dictionary strings in both languages and a `docs/decisions.md` entry once the shape is settled.

- [ ] **Scope an online merchandise shop — the deliverable is a scope, not a build.** Customer request 2026-09-23. The ask is selling shelter merchandise online (t-shirts, calendars, tote bags and the like); this item ends with `docs/merch-shop.md` plus a `docs/decisions.md` entry and a recommendation the customer can say yes or no to — no schema and no routes until they have. **Questions to answer with them:** what is actually for sale and at what price; who packs and posts a parcel (shelter staff in Suphan Buri? Thailand Post / Kerry / J&T, who pays the postage, domestic only or overseas too?), or whether print-on-demand removes stock entirely; whether collection at the shelter is the common case; how many orders a month realistically — that number decides everything below; who answers "where is my order"; stock counts, sizes and variants, and what a sold-out item does; receipts, refunds, and whether the shelter's tax position cares that this is trading income rather than donations. **The real decision is build vs. link:** (a) point the public site at a hosted storefront — LINE MyShop or a Facebook/Instagram Shop, which is where their supporters already are, or Shopify — so the app adds a nav link and nothing else, and the shelter gets payments, stock and shipping labels for free; (b) build `/shop` into this app, reusing the Drive photo plumbing, the `site_pages` copy pattern, the translation queue and the Worker, with `products` / `orders` tables and a hosted checkout; (c) a middle path — a catalogue in the app that hands off to (a) for payment. Weigh it honestly: (b) is only worth the work if an order needs to tie back to something the system already knows (a resident, an appeal, a supporter record shared with donations), and it puts payments, stock and customer support on infrastructure that is about to move onto a Raspberry Pi in Suphan Buri (`docs/pi-hosting.md`), where an hour of downtime is a lost sale rather than a staff member waiting. Read it next to the Fundraising item above: if both happen, a buyer and a donor are usually the same person and should not end up as two unrelated tables.

- [ ] **Investigate options for a drop-shipment B2B offering on the system — the deliverable is an options paper, not a build.** Requested 2026-09-24 by Lutan. The idea: sell goods through the system without the shelter holding stock or posting parcels, a supplier shipping direct to the buyer, with businesses as the buyers or the partners. **Blocked: do not start until the Director has answered the prerequisites below** (Lutan is taking them to the Director); record the answers here, dated, before the work begins. **Prerequisites (answers from the Director):** (1) **What "B2B" means here**, because the options differ completely. Is it (i) the shelter *sourcing* from a B2B drop-ship supplier (print-on-demand merch, or pet food and supplies from a Thai wholesaler) and selling on to supporters; (ii) the shelter *selling to businesses* (vets, pet shops, cafés, the companies on the "Shelter Friends" list) buying branded or bulk goods shipped direct by the supplier; (iii) businesses selling *through* the shelter's site with a cut going to the shelter (affiliate or marketplace); or (iv) something else, e.g. offering this system itself to other shelters? (2) **What would be sold**, roughly at what price, and to whom: supporters in Thailand only, or overseas too? (3) **Suppliers already in mind**: any wholesaler, printer or partner business the Director has spoken to, and any known margins or minimum orders. (4) **Who handles the customer side**: payment, VAT invoices / tax receipts for business buyers, returns and "where is my order". Shelter staff, the supplier, or nobody yet? (5) **Tax and legal position**: whether trading income (rather than donations) changes the shelter's status, and who can confirm that (their accountant?). (6) **Expected volume**: realistic orders a month, which decides whether anything is worth building. (7) **How it relates to the merch shop and donations**: one effort or separate ones? **Once answered, the work:** research which suppliers actually drop-ship inside Thailand (and overseas, if it matters), their integration (API, CSV feed, email order, or a manual order form), margins and minimums; work out what the system would have to hold for the chosen model (products, business accounts, orders, supplier routing); and **weigh build vs. link honestly**, as the merch item does: a hosted storefront or the supplier's own white-label shop may give most of the value for none of the payments, support and Pi-uptime risk (`docs/pi-hosting.md`). The deliverable is `docs/drop-shipping-b2b.md` plus a `docs/decisions.md` entry and a recommendation Lutan and the customer can say yes or no to. No schema and no routes until they have. Read it next to **Scope an online merchandise shop** and **Make `/donate` ask for money** above, and **"Shelter Friends"** on the public site: if more than one happens they should share one catalogue, one checkout and one record of who the buyer, donor or business is. Folding this into the merch scope as one of its options is a fine outcome.

## Completed

Done items, kept under the same headings as the open work above so follow-ups can be traced back to what they build on.

### Process

- [x] **Run several workstreams at once.** Done 2026-09-22: one git worktree per feature (`scripts/worktree.mjs new|dev|sync|list|done`), the main checkout stays on `main`, `/plan-day` syncs `main` and proposes the day's two or three non-overlapping streams from this file, schema changes land first as their own PR, CI type-checks/lints/builds every PR, and `docs/decisions.md` merges by union. `CLAUDE.md` "Workstreams", README "Day-to-day workflow", `docs/decisions.md`.

### Deployment

- [x] **Set the Pi up: production origin and weekly backup, one box.** **Done 2026-09-30, guided session** (PRs #237 and #238, then `claude/pi-setup`; see `docs/decisions/2026-09-30-one-pi-hosting-and-backup.md` and `docs/pi-hosting.md`). **Hosting:** the Pi (Debian 13, built from a micro SD then copied to the NVMe drive) serves lannacare.org (`x-lanna-served-by: pi`), `pi.lannacare.org` returns 403 without the WAF key, and the failover drill passed both ways (app stopped, tunnel stopped: `worker`, still 200; restarted: `pi`). Release 0.10.1 carried the switch. **Backup:** `backup.mjs --local-copy` added; two real production dumps taken on the Pi (one by hand, one under cron's minimal environment), uploaded to Drive and kept in `~/backups/lannacare` (`0700`/`0600`, outside the repo); Sunday 03:00 cron installed. **Still open (follow-ups on the `backlog` branch):** the first scheduled Sunday run (2026-10-04) has not fired, and the laptop task stays until it is confirmed; the backups are not encrypted (DB-1). Text below is the original brief. Lutan is home in Suphan Buri and ready to set it up (2026-09-30). **There is only one Pi** — corrected 2026-09-30. This item replaces the former pair ("Bring the Suphan Buri Pi up as the production origin" and "Move the weekly backup to the Raspberry Pi 5 at home"), which assumed two machines: a hosting box in Suphan Buri and a separate backup Pi 5 "at home". Lutan's home **is** in Suphan Buri and there is one Raspberry Pi 5, so both roles land on it. Treat anything written on the two-box assumption as wrong, including `docs/pi-hosting.md`'s "Second Pi (later)" section, which stays genuinely later. **Do it as a guided session, not a handover document:** Lutan is at the box and runs the commands, the session reads the output and decides the next step, and writes up what actually worked afterwards. **(1) Hosting.** The Worker is already deployed with the tunnel fallback logic and `ORIGIN_HOST` empty (2026-09-22). Follow `docs/pi-hosting.md` end to end: clone, copy the two env files, create the tunnel, `scripts/pi/setup.sh <tunnel-id>`, the WAF rule and `ORIGIN_KEY`, set `ORIGIN_HOST` and `npm run deploy:prod -- --secrets`, then the failover drill. `curl -sI https://lannacare.org/` should report `x-lanna-served-by: pi`. This also clears the Cloudflare 1102s on the test site, which were always the environment rather than a defect. **(2) Backup.** An always-on Linux box with a 2 TB SSD beats a laptop that has to be awake on Sunday mornings, and the SSD gives a second copy next to Drive. `scripts/backup.mjs` already runs there unchanged: it looks for `pg_dump` on `PATH` first. Install Node 22+ and `postgresql-client-17` from the PGDG apt repo (`apt.postgresql.org` has arm64 builds; Debian bookworm's own package is 15, which pg_dump refuses against a 17 server); copy `.env.local` + `.env.deploy.production` across (never commit them); run `node scripts/backup.mjs --env production` once by hand; then a cron line such as `0 3 * * 0 cd ~/Animal_Shelter_App && node scripts/backup.mjs --env production >> backup.log 2>&1`. Add a `--local-copy <dir>` flag (upload *and* keep a copy on the SSD, unlike `--local`) so the Pi holds the last N too. Then unregister the laptop task (`scripts\backup-schedule.ps1 -Remove`) and update README "Backups". **(3) The two roles now share a box, which the old split avoided.** Hosting was designed to hold no data ("The data is never on the Pi", `docs/pi-hosting.md`) while the backup role puts a production dump on the SSD — so the machine holding the backups now also runs an internet-reachable render service. The tunnel is outbound-only so the exposure is modest, but it is no longer zero: keep the dumps readable only by the backup user, never serve them from the Next.js app, and **Drive stays the off-site copy** — a Pi in the house is one flood or theft away from the data it protects. Record the single-Pi correction and this trade-off in `docs/decisions/`.

- [x] **Lock UAT (lannacare.org) and test.lannacare.org behind sign-in — no public access until go-live.** Done 2026-09-25 (PR #123): `"PUBLIC_SITE": "locked"` on the test, uat and production Workers; signed-out `/` is a sign-in landing page, everything else but `/login`, `/login/forgot`, `/auth/callback`, `/privacy` and `/robots.txt` redirects to sign-in and back, all responses noindex. **The cutover must drop the var from the production block.** Original brief: Lutan, 2026-09-25: lannacare.org is UAT for good (the live site moves to lannacareforanimals.org at the cutover, see `wrangler.jsonc`), so strangers should not land on the public pages there yet. Signed-out visitors to any page get a sign-in landing page instead of the public site; staff sign in as now. Keep the exceptions that must stay open: `/login`, `/login/forgot`, `/auth/callback`, `/privacy` (the Google consent screen links to it and to the homepage), `/api/photos/` only as far as signed-in use needs. Switch it on with a per-Worker var in `wrangler.jsonc`, not `getAppEnv()` (lannacare.org still reports "production" until the cutover sets `UAT_PROJECT_REF`), so the cutover can turn it off for the real live site. Lock test.lannacare.org too (Lutan, 2026-09-25). Add `noindex` while it's on.

- [x] **A third environment, `uat`, in the app and the deploy tooling.** Done 2026-09-23, plumbing only: `src/lib/app-env.ts` returns `dev | uat | production`, with `UAT_PROJECT_REF` left empty until the cutover sets it (it will be `dbkodyyxxhtygxcxmfcu`, production's today). UAT keeps production's colours; a **UAT** badge in `AppHeader` and a diagonal watermark on the archive PDF are the only differences (dev builds get a `DEV` watermark too, since they share UAT's Drive). `wrangler.jsonc` has a `uat` block (`lanna-animal-care-uat`, no routes yet), `--env uat` works in every `scripts/lib/env.mjs` script with `.env.deploy.uat`, `npm run deploy:uat` exists, and `deploy-pi.sh` knows its service and host. `deploy.mjs` now refuses any environment whose project app-env would badge as another, so `deploy:uat` refuses until the cutover, and UAT gets production's clean-main and written-release guards. No thin top strip: the badge was judged enough. README "Environments" lists what the cutover changes.
- [x] **Builds no longer type-check the dev server's `.next/dev/types`.** Done 2026-09-22: `tsconfig.build.json` (`tsconfig.json` minus `.next/dev`) is what `next build` type-checks with (`typescript.tsconfigPath` in `next.config.ts`, keyed on NODE_ENV so `next dev` and the editor keep `tsconfig.json`) and what `npm run typecheck` runs `tsc` against. Reproduced with truncated `routes.d.ts` / `validator.ts` under a running dev server: `next build` failed before, and `npm run opennext:build` (the deploy script's build step) completed after with the same files in place. Root cause is Next 16's `experimental.useTypeScriptCli` default, which hands the tsconfig straight to `tsc --project`; Next's own checker filters `.next/dev/types` out and the CLI path doesn't. Original brief: **A production build can fail type-checking on half-written `.next/dev/types/*.ts`.** Seen 2026-09-22: `tsconfig.json` includes `.next/dev/types/**/*.ts`, which a running `next dev` rewrites; when `next build` type-checks while that is mid-write (or after an interrupted build wiped `.next`), it fails with a wall of `TS1005 ';' expected` in `routes.d.ts` / `validator.ts` and the deploy stops. Workaround: delete `.next/dev/types/*` and rerun. Fix: have `scripts/deploy.mjs` build in a scratch copy of `.next` (or stop the dev server first / exclude `.next/dev/types` from the build-time `tsc`), so a deploy never depends on what the dev server is doing.
- [x] **Scheduled production backups.** Done 2026-09-21: `scripts/backup.mjs --env production` (`pg_dump` custom format of `public` + `auth` over the session pooler — the direct host is IPv6-only — into `Backups/` under the Drive root, newest twelve kept) and `scripts/backup-schedule.ps1` for the Sunday 03:00 Task Scheduler job; README "Backups". The restore rehearsal is its own open item above. Original brief: Free-tier Supabase has no backups. Write `scripts/backup.mjs --env production`: `pg_dump` (custom format) over the project's direct connection string, uploaded into a `Backups/` folder under the Drive root with the date in the name, keep the last N. Run it weekly from Task Scheduler on this machine (or a reminder) until something better exists. Test a restore into a scratch project once. Nothing destructive should happen on production until this runs.

### Quick wins

- [x] **"Today" is UTC everywhere, so it is yesterday in Thailand until 07:00.** Done 2026-09-24, in the three parts the item asked for. *The code*: `todayIso()` in `src/lib/format.ts` formats in the shelter's timezone (`SHELTER_TIME_ZONE`, `Asia/Bangkok`), and every one of the 27 call sites across 24 files now uses it. *The SQL*: Postgres had its own copy of the same bug — `current_date` in a UTC session — closed separately by `0073_shelter_today.sql` (`shelter_time_zone()` / `shelter_today()` / `shelter_date()`), which is its own completed item under Architecture. *The data*: `docs/utc-date-audit-2026-09-23.md` compared every `date` column against its `created_at` on both dev and production; the three real candidates turned out to be a single event, so nothing was bulk-corrected — the audit was brought to the user rather than acted on unilaterally, as the item asked. See `docs/decisions.md` (2026-09-23, shelter dates are Asia/Bangkok, not the viewer's clock and not UTC). **Ticked 2026-09-24 on the `backlog` branch:** the work merged that morning but no PR ticked the item, so it sat open for a day while three planning rounds flagged it as already finished.
- [x] **Fix mobile row click on residents list.** In `src/app/residents/ResidentsTable.tsx` the name link's `after:absolute after:inset-0` overlay relies on `relative` on the `<tr>`, which mobile WebKit ignores, so tapping anywhere opens the last row. Anchor it to the cell or make the row properly clickable.
- [x] **Add "Book vet visit" button to the resident hub.** Put it on the vet-appointments section (and stat card) linking to `/vet-visits/new?residentId=<id>`, mirroring how immunizations already do it.
- [x] **Show age on the public resident page.** Add `estimated_age_years` and `intake_date` to the `public_resident_profiles` view (new migration) and render an Age row on `/adopt/[id]` using the existing `formatAge()` helper.
- [x] **Strategy for free-text fields across languages.** Decided and built 2026-09-21 (see `docs/decisions.md`): public-facing prose (resident bio / temperament / past story, project story, photo captions) gets one row in a `translations` side table (`0056_translations.sql`) — the text in the other language, a status, and a snapshot of the source it was written against, so an edit to the original re-queues it as *Out of date* rather than leaving the Thai silently wrong. Source language is detected from the script of the text. Managers write and approve at `/management/translations` (stale first, editor open on every row) or in the same `TranslationPanel` under the field on the resident hub / project folder; approved text is what a visitor reading that language sees, else the original. `summary_th` / `caption_th` moved into the table and were dropped; `thai_name` / `name_th` stay as labels. Maintenance job title and description joined the same day (`0057_translations_maintenance.sql`: tiers renamed to `reviewed` / `machine`, board and job page show the reader's language) — the user's first question was whether tasks were covered. Internal notes are untouched until the machine phase (Management item above); zone and enclosure names are the Facility item below. Original brief: The UI is fully bilingual, but staff-typed text (weight and vet-visit notes, bio and behaviour notes, cause of death, prescription notes, intake notes) shows in whatever language it was typed — a Thai user sees English notes and vice versa (noticed 2026-09-20 on the Weight tab). Options to weigh: leave as-is with a house rule on which language notes are written in; paired `_th` columns where a field is public-facing (the bio already has precedent in `thai_name`); or on-demand machine translation shown as a hint under the original, never replacing it. Decide before the public site or foster portal shows free text to a different audience than the one that wrote it.
- [x] **Contact hub tidy-up (four customer requests, 2026-09-22).** Done 2026-09-22 — Donor/Other removed from the enum outright (migration 0067, see `docs/decisions.md`); the address line and a map preview replace the duplicated detail rows; shared `maps.app.goo.gl` links are resolved server-side. Original item: All in `src/app/contacts/[id]/ContactHub.tsx` unless noted; one branch, one PR. (a) **Drop the Donor and Other contact types.** `CONTACT_TYPES` in `src/lib/contacts/contacts.ts` and the `contact_type` enum (`0001`) both carry `Donor` and `Other`; remove them from the constant, the Management → Contacts form and the `t.contacts.types` labels (`en.ts` ~2550 and `th.ts`). Check the dev and production `contacts` tables for rows of those types first (`select type, count(*) from contacts group by 1`) and decide with the user what they become (Volunteer? Vendor?) before writing the migration — Postgres can't drop an enum value, so either leave the values in the enum and stop offering them (simplest) or recreate the type; say which in `docs/decisions.md`. `scripts/import-appsheet.mjs` maps AppSheet's Donor too. (b) **Phone and WhatsApp appear twice on the card**: `ContactActions size="lg"` already renders each channel as an orange tile with its value, and the `<dl>` beneath repeats the same values as text. Remove the duplicated text rows — keep the `<dl>` only for details that have no action tile (today that's none, since every detail has a matching action), or fold the value into the tile and drop the list; whichever, the address must stay readable in full (long Thai addresses are truncated in a tile). (c) **Map preview for the address.** `mapHref()` treats an `http(s)` address as a pasted maps link and everything else as a search query. When the address is a link (or, cheaper, always), show a small embedded map under the address: a keyless `<iframe src="https://www.google.com/maps?q=<query>&output=embed">` works without an API key for a search query; a shared `maps.app.goo.gl` short link can't be embedded directly (it redirects, and iframes don't follow it into a usable embed) — either resolve it server-side once and store the coordinates, or fall back to the search embed on the text after the link. Lazy-load the iframe, keep it ~200px tall, and don't render it in the `/contacts` list. (d) **Alignment between Residents in care and Past placements.** The two sections sit in a `lg:grid-cols-2`; the left one has the `residentsInCareDetail` helper paragraph under its heading and the right one doesn't, so the two lists start at different heights. Either give both sections a helper line (e.g. "Residents who used to live with this carer.") or move the helper below the list next to `assignFromResident`, so the headings and the first card line up.
- [x] **Pin User manual, Change password and Security to the bottom of the left nav.** Done 2026-09-22: `NavLinks` splits its list into `mainItems` and a `footerItems` group (User manual, Change password, and Security for admins) rendered after `mt-auto` and made `sticky bottom-4` — the sidebar is stretched to the height of the page, not the window, so `mt-auto` alone parked the group thousands of pixels below the fold on a long list. It now sits on the bottom of the window above a divider, and comes last in the phone drawer. Security left the Admin accordion, which made two knock-ons: `/admin` redirects to `/admin/website` (the first page the group still lists) instead of `/admin/security`, and a group counts as “inside” for auto-expand when the route matches one of its own children rather than the group href, so opening Security no longer springs Admin open. Manual’s navigation topic and the Security topic’s path updated. Original brief: Customer request 2026-09-22. In `src/app/NavLinks.tsx` the `/manual` and `/account/password` entries sit in the middle of `items`, between the Assistant demo and the Management group; Security is a child of the Admin accordion. Split the list into a main group and a footer group rendered after a `mt-auto` spacer so the footer sits at the bottom of the sidebar (the drawer on mobile can just list them last with a divider). Footer contents: User manual, Change password and — for admins — Security, which then leaves the Admin accordion (keep `/admin` itself listing it on its landing page). Update the manual's navigation topic (`src/lib/manual/en.ts`, `th.ts`).
- [x] **The pinned nav footer group hides the last Admin links when both accordions are open.** Closed 2026-09-23 by the nav rework rather than fixed separately: the Management and Settings children are gone, and the pinned group is now pinned only on windows at least 44rem tall (`[@media(min-height:44rem)]:sticky`). Even the flat list plus the group needs ~690px for an admin, so it still covered Settings on a 640px window when first built; below that height the group now follows the list behind its divider, as in the phone drawer. Bounding the sidebar with its own scrollbar (below) was not needed. Original brief: Noticed 2026-09-22 while building the footer group. `src/app/NavLinks.tsx` renders it `mt-auto sticky bottom-4 bg-surface`, which holds it against the bottom of the window — but the sidebar is a flex item stretched to the height of the *document*, so the links stay in the flow at the top while the group floats over them. An admin with Management *and* Admin expanded on a window under ~950px has 21 links plus the group: at the top of the page `/admin/zones`, `/admin/immunization-types` and `/admin/procedure-types` sit behind it, and since the divider looks like the end of the menu they read as missing rather than hidden. Scrolling ~200px clears them; one group expanded never overlaps. The proper fix is to bound the sidebar to the window and give it its own scrollbar (`md:sticky md:top-14 md:h-[calc(100dvh-3.5rem)] md:overflow-y-auto`), which needs `AppHeader` pinned at a fixed height and made sticky — a visible change on every page, so decide with the user first. Making the two accordions mutually exclusive would also do it without any layout change.
- [x] **Nav rework: rename Admin to Settings, drop the chevrons, reorder, add icons.** Done 2026-09-23: Management and Settings are single links to their tile-grid landing pages, so the ~13 child links, both chevrons, the `nav-expanded` sessionStorage and the auto-expand rules are gone from `src/app/NavLinks.tsx`. Order agreed with Lutan: Residents, Enclosures, Maintenance | Vets, Contacts, Projects | Assistant | Management, Settings, with a thin rule between groups, then the pinned footer. Every link has the icon its page already uses (`NAV_ICONS` in `src/components/hub-icons.ts`, which the Settings Security tile now shares). The active link is the longest matching href, so `/admin/security` lights Security and not Settings too. The rename is label-only — `/admin/*` URLs and the `admin` role keep their names (`docs/decisions.md`); nav, Settings landing heading, menu paths in `en.ts` / `th.ts` and the manual changed. Original brief:
  Requested 2026-09-23 by Lutan; this absorbs the earlier "collapse the accordions to plain links" follow-up, which was waiting on exactly this decision. Four parts, one stream:
  1. **Drop the chevrons.** `/management` and `/admin` are tile-grid landing pages now (built 2026-09-23), so the sidebar lists Management and Settings as ordinary links and the landing page is the menu. That removes ~13 child links, the two chevron buttons, the `sessionStorage` (`nav-expanded`) choices and the auto-expand rules in `src/app/NavLinks.tsx` — and closes the pinned-footer overlap item above as a side effect, so tick that one too.
  2. **Rename "Admin" to "Settings"** in the nav label, the landing page heading, the i18n dictionaries (`en.ts` / `th.ts`) and the manual. Decide whether the URL moves too: `/settings` with `/admin/*` redirects (as `/admin/vets` → `/management/vets` was done) is cleaner, but a label-only rename is much smaller — ask. The `admin` *role* keeps its name; this is the menu, not the permission.
  3. **Reorder the nav.** Propose an order grouped by how often each link is used (daily field work first — Residents, medical, Facility — then Management, Settings, and the pinned footer) and **agree it with the user before building**; a quick sketch of two orders is enough.
  4. **Icons beside every nav item.** The landing-page tiles already carry an icon each — reuse the same icon set (and the same icon per page) so the nav and the tiles match. Icons should also make a collapsed/narrow sidebar feasible later, but don't build that here.

  `NavLinks.tsx` is a conflict magnet, so give it a stream of its own with nothing else in it, and update the manual's navigation topic (`src/lib/manual/en.ts`, `th.ts`).
- [x] **The assistant still carries its own copy of the "not deceased" filter.** Done 2026-09-24: `src/lib/assistant/data.ts` (where the assistant query had moved) now imports `NOT_DECEASED`; it was the only literal copy under `src/lib/assistant/` and `src/app/assistant/`. Original item: Noticed 2026-09-23 while doing the hide-deceased list item, which moved that PostgREST expression into `src/lib/residents/status.ts` as `NOT_DECEASED` and pointed the residents list and the vet-visit and immunization pickers at it. `src/app/assistant/page.tsx` was being rewritten on another branch at the time, so its literal `.or("current_status.neq.Deceased,current_status.is.null")` was left alone. Swap it for the import once the assistant work has landed, so there is one definition of which residents are still with us.

### Mobile
